This project has received funding from the European Research Council (ERC) under the European Union’s Horizon 2020 research and innovation programme (grant agreement No. 692452).

Thanks to Ofer Zeitouni for suggesting the problem and for many useful discussions. We also thank an anonymous referee for many helpful comments improving the presentation of this paper.

2
Preliminaries: Barrier Events and a Many-to-One Lemma

We first give a rather general definition of barrier events and probabilities.

Definition 2.1

(Barrier Events)
.

For
I\subseteq\mathbb{R}
,
t:=\max I
,
(Z_{s})_{s\in I}
a real valued process,
f:I\to\mathbb{R}
a function,
y\in\mathbb{R}
and
J\subseteq\mathbb{R}
an interval, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{I,f}^{y,J}(Z_{\cdot}):=\{\forall_{s\in I}\,y+Z_{s}+f(s)\leq 0,y+Z_{t}+f(t)\in J\}.

If
f=0
or
y=0
we suppress them from the notation. Also set
J_{x}:=[x-1,x]
for
x\in\mathbb{R}
. If
J=\mathbb{R}
we suppress it from notation. We call events of this form barrier events.

Remark 2.2
.

We emphasize that
\mathcal{B}_{I,f}^{y,J}
is the probability that
y+Z_{\cdot}
stays
below
the barrier given by
-f
and ends up in the interval
J-f(t)
. Often estimates of barrier events like the ballot theorem are stated in terms of
Z_{\cdot}
staying above a barrier. However, this change of sign is common in the study of the maximum of BRW (see
A_{v}
in
[
27
, p. 11]
,
X_{n}(\delta)
in
[
20
, p. 15]
and Lemma 2.4 in
[
22
, p. 9]
).

Definition 2.3
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
(X_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be i.i.d. with
X_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(\vartheta^{\ast},1)
and set

\displaystyle S_{n}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}X_{k}\sim\mathcal{N}(n\vartheta^{\ast},n),\quad B_{n}:=S_{n}-n\vartheta^{\ast},\quad W_{n}:=K_{n}/\vartheta^{\ast}-n\vartheta^{\ast}.

We can realize
(B_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
as the values at integer times of a Brownian motion
(B_{s})_{s\geq 0}
independent of
\mathcal{L}
. Furthermore we define
W_{s}
for all
s\geq 0
by linear interpolation. For
s\geq 0
set
T_{s}:=\vartheta^{\ast}(B_{s}-W_{s})
.

Finally, denote by
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{k}
the measure using the environment
(\mathcal{L}_{j+k})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
. We also introduce the notation
S_{n}^{(k)}:=\sum_{j=k+1}^{k+n}X_{j}
and
T_{n}^{(k)}=\vartheta^{\ast}S_{n}^{(k)}-\sum_{j=k+1}^{k+n}\kappa_{j}(\vartheta^{\ast})
.

Remark 2.4
.

Since
(\mathcal{L}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is i.i.d., so is
(W_{k+1}-W_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
. Furthermore, by choice of
\vartheta^{\ast}
we have
\mathbb{E}[W_{2}-W_{1}]=0
and finally, since we assume that
\Var[\log(\mathcal{L}_{1})]<\infty
, we have
\Var[W_{2}-W_{1}]<\infty
. Thus we can apply the law of iterated logarithms for
(W_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, a version of which can be found in Theorem 5.17 in
[
23
]
.

We also note that under the annealed law
T_{n}
is the sum of i.i.d. random variables with mean zero and finite variance. Thus
T
behaves similarly to a Brownian motion.

Now we have defined all random variables necessary to state the many-to-one lemma for our model.

Lemma 2.5

(Lemma 2.1 equation (2.4) and equation (2.5) in
[
20
]
)
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
non-negative, we have
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\sum_{|u|=n}f(\vartheta^{\ast}V(u_{j})-K_{j},j\leq n)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[e^{-T_{n}}f(T_{1},\dots,T_{n})].

We also have, remembering the definition
S_{n}^{(k)}=X_{k+1}+\dots+X_{n+k}
, the time-shifted version of this

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{k}\left[\sum_{|u|=n}f(\vartheta^{\ast}V(u_{j})-K_{j},j\leq n)\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{k}\left[e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}S_{n}^{(k)}+\sum_{j=k+1}^{k+n}\kappa_{j}(\vartheta^{\ast})}f(T_{1}^{(k)},\dots,T_{n}^{(k)})\right].

Proof.

We only need to show that our definition of
S_{n}
has the same distribution as the
S_{n}
from
[
20
]
. They take

\widetilde{S}_{n}:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\widetilde{X}_{k},

with
(\widetilde{X}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
an independent sequence of random variables such that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\widetilde{X}_{k}\leq x\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\sum_{\ell\in L_{k}}\mathbf{1}_{\ell\leq x}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}\ell-\kappa_{k}(\vartheta^{\ast})}\right].

(2.1)

Here, due to the simplified model we consider,
L_{k}
is a set of
\mathcal{L}_{k}
independent random variables with law
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Thus taking Definition 1.1 into account and letting
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, (
2.1
) simplifies to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\widetilde{X}_{k}\leq x\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{k}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{Z\leq x}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}Z-\log(\mathcal{L}_{k})-\frac{(\vartheta^{\ast})^{2}}{2}}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{Z\leq x}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}Z-\frac{(\vartheta^{\ast})^{2}}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{x}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{2}}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}z-\frac{(\vartheta^{\ast})^{2}}{2}}\;\mathrm{d}z=\int_{-\infty}^{x}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{(z-\vartheta^{\ast})^{2}}{2}}\;\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}(\vartheta^{\ast},1)((-\infty,x]).

This shows that
\widetilde{X}_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}X_{k}
from Definition
2.3
, which implies
S_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\widetilde{S}_{n}
.
∎

We recall that
\xi_{0}
is a fixed negative integer.

Lemma 2.6
.

For
t\geq 0
define

\displaystyle p_{t}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t]}^{\xi_{0},J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right].

(2.2)

Then there is a
C>0
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{|\log(p_{n})|}{\log(n)}\leq C.

Remark 2.7
.

Because of Theorem 3.3 in
[
20
]
we expect that in
\mathbb{P}
-probability as
n\to\infty
we have

\log(p_{n})/\log(n)\to-\lambda,

with
\lambda
as in equation (3.3) of
[
20
]
. To see this we can take
y=(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}
,
x=-\xi_{0}/\vartheta^{\ast}
,
k=k^{\prime}=0
in equation (3.4) of
[
20
]
to get that with high probability (as
n\to\infty
) we have
p_{n}\leq n^{-\lambda+\varepsilon}
, i.e.
\log(p_{n})/\log(n)\leq-\lambda+\varepsilon
. We can’t extract an analogous lower bound from equation (3.4) in
[
20
]
, since the infimum only is taken over
[a_{n},x_{n}]
with
\liminf_{n\to\infty}a_{n}/\log(n)>0
. Lemma
2.6
provides a lower bound, we note however that the lower bound we achieve is far from exact, i.e. is not close to
-\lambda
.

The following lemma allows us to factorize the probability of barrier events of Markov processes into two factors. This will be useful since we mostly do barrier computations for time intervals
[0,t]
but want to apply them also for time intervals
[0,t_{1}]
and
[t_{1},t]
, which equations (
2.3
) and (
2.4
) enable. The proof of Lemma
2.8
is a straightforward application of the Markov property, which we omit.

Lemma 2.8
.

Let
I\subseteq\mathbb{R}
,
t:=\max I
,
(Z_{s})_{s\in I}
be a real valued and translation invariant Markov process on some measure space
(\widetilde{\Omega},\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}})
, and
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
. Take
t_{0}\in I
and set
I_{1}:=\{s\in I:s\leq t_{0}\}
,
I_{2}:=\{s\in I:s\geq t_{0}\}
. We have that for
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{I}^{z_{1},J_{z_{2}}}(Z_{\cdot})\right]

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{I_{1}}^{z_{1}+1,J_{x_{0}}}(Z_{\cdot})\right]\inf_{x\in J_{{x_{0}}-1}}\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{I_{2}}^{x,J_{z_{2}}}(Z_{\cdot}-Z_{t_{0}})\right],

(2.3)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{I}^{z_{1},[z_{2}-2,z_{2}]}(Z_{\cdot})\right]

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{I_{1}}^{z_{1},J_{x_{0}}}(Z_{\cdot})\right]\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{I_{2}}^{x_{0},J_{z_{2}}}(Z_{\cdot}-Z_{t_{0}})\right].

(2.4)

3
Structure of the proof

The first step in the proof of Theorem
1.2
is an upper bound on
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}-m_{n}\geq-y\right]
for
y<0
. We can get a good enough upper bound for
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}-m_{n}\geq\xi_{0}+\log(n)^{2}\right]
by applying Lemma
2.5
. Thus we need to consider
y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]
. This is done using a first moment approach. For this, we introduce the barrier
\left((\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}K_{k}+\frac{k}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}\log(p_{n})-y\right)_{k\in\{0,\dots,n\}}
and take
\tau(y)
to be the first time at which any particle in our system breaks this barrier. If no particle breaks the barrier until time
n
, we define
\tau(y)
to be equal to
n
. It is immediate that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}-m_{n}\in[\xi_{0}-y-1,\xi_{0}-y]\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 61.17325pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\tau(y)<n\right]+\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\tau(y)=n,M_{n}-m_{n}\in[\xi_{0}-y-1,\xi_{0}-y]\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 61.17325pt\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\tau(y)<n\right]+\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\bigcup_{u:|u|=n}\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},-(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}(\frac{\cdot}{n}\log(p_{n})+K_{\cdot})}^{y,J_{\xi_{0}}}(V(u_{\cdot}))\right].

(3.1)

The second inequality of (
3.1
) uses that if
\tau(y)=n
no particle breaches the barrier before the last step while if
M_{n}-m_{n}\in[\xi_{0}-y-1,\xi_{0}-y]
there is a particle which ends up in
[m_{n}+\xi_{0}-y-1,m_{n}+\xi_{0}-y]
. Both combined mean that there is a particle which satisfies the barrier event
\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},-(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}(\frac{\cdot}{n}\log(p_{n})+K_{\cdot})}^{y,J_{\xi_{0}}}(V(u_{\cdot}))
.

Applying the Markov inequality and the many-to-one Lemma
2.5
to the second summand in (
3.1
) yields that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}-m_{n}\in J_{\xi_{0}-y}\right]\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\tau(y)<n\right]+Cp_{n}^{-1}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},\frac{\cdot}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}\log(p_{n})}^{y,J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right].

(3.2)

The heart of the matter is now to prove that for all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},\frac{\cdot}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}\log(p_{n})}^{y,J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]}{p_{n}}\leq C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

(3.3)

In Figure
1
we illustrate an event similar to the one in (
3.3
). The main difference is that for (
3.3
) the thin black Brownian motion sample would need to stay below the dashed line instead of below the thick black line. We recall that
p_{n}=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,n]}^{\xi_{0},J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]
. Equation (3.4) in
[
20
]
roughly corresponds to our equation (
3.3
). In
[
20
, Eq. (3.4)]
they bound the growth in
n
of
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,n]}^{y,J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]
from above (in
\mathbb{P}
-probability and uniformly in
y
with
|y|
small enough). In particular, they bound the growth of
p_{n}
. In contrast equation (
3.3
) controls how much starting lower increases the probability to stay below the barrier. We sketch below how equation (
3.3
) is proved. Handling the second summand in (
3.2
) is immediate once (
3.3
) is established, the first summand can be handled using (
3.3
) and standard techniques (these techniques are for example sketched in Remark 9 of
[
27
, p. 16]
). In the actual proof we need a slightly more complicated barrier, which has an additional (deterministic) curve added, this does only marginally change the proof of (
3.3
), however we need to then remove this curve after we have moved the starting point. This is mostly an application of Girsanov.

The next step in proving Theorem
1.2
is a lower bound on
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}-m_{n}\geq\xi_{0}-y-1\right]
for
y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]
. This is done using a second moment approach, for this we define
Z_{n}(y)
to be the number of particles for which
(V(u_{k})-(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}K_{k})_{k\in\{0,\dots,n\}}
stays below
(\frac{k}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}\log(p_{n})-y)_{k\in\{0,\dots,n\}}
and ends up in
[(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}\log(p_{n})+\xi_{0}-y-1,(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}\log(p_{n})+\xi_{0}-y]=J_{(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}\log(p_{n})+\xi_{0}-y}
. Using Cauchy-Schwartz yields that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n}\geq\xi_{0}-y-1]\geq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)\geq 1]\geq\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)^{2}]}

and a lower bound on the right tail can be achieved by bounding
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)]
and
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)^{2}]
. The first is done similar to the calculation in the upper bound on the right tail of
M_{n}-m_{n}
, using that for all
\varepsilon>0
there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},\frac{\cdot}{\vartheta^{\ast}n}\log(p_{n})}^{y,J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]}{p_{n}}\geq C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{-c_{\varepsilon}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon

(3.4)

instead of (
3.3
). In Figure
2
we illustrate an event similar to the one in (
3.4
). The main difference is that for (
3.4
) the thin black Brownian motion sample would need to stay below the dashed line instead of below the thick black line. For the bound on the second moment we use
\Lambda_{k}(y)
to denote the number of pairs of particles in generation
n
, which are both counted in
Z_{n}(y)
, whose last common ancestor is in generation
k
. By definition

Z_{n}(y)^{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\Lambda_{k}(y)+Z_{n}(y).

Thus we need to prove an upper bound on
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]
, we note that this proceeds along similar lines as
[
20
, Section 4.1]
. We quickly sketch how to get the upper bound. For this, we let
w:=u\wedge v
be the
k
-generational common ancestor of a pair
u
,
v
counting for
\Lambda_{k}(y)
. Then we condition on the position
V(w)
and use that given
V(w)
and the environment
\mathcal{L}
, both
(V(u_{j})-V(w))_{j\in\{k,\dots,n\}}
and
(V(v_{j})-V(w))_{j\in\{k,\dots,n\}}
are independent and identically distributed to write

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[u,\ v\ \text{are counted by }\Lambda_{k}(y)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[\mathcal{B}_{\{k,\dots,n\},-\frac{\cdot-k}{\vartheta^{\ast}n}\log(p_{n})}^{r,J_{m_{n}+\xi_{0}-y}}(V(u_{\cdot})-V(w)-(K_{\cdot}-K_{k})/\vartheta^{\ast})]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,k\},-\frac{\cdot}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}\log(p_{n})}^{y,\mathbb{R}}(V(w_{\cdot})-K_{j}/\vartheta^{\ast})|V(w_{k})-K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}\in dr].

The two factors are then handled by using Lemma
2.5
and (
3.3
). In the proof it will be helpful to use a (downwards) curved barrier instead of the straight barrier used here to force
V(w)
to be distanced from
K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}
.

The upper bound on the left tail of
M_{n}-m_{n}
follows from the lower bound on the right tail by using the tree structure and the fact that quenched the subtrees below two different vertices in generation
k
are independent of each other (for the homogeneous situation this argument can be found on the first half of page 17 in
[
27
]
).

Having upper bounds on both the left and the right tail of
M_{n}-m_{n}
then quickly allows to prove Theorem
1.2
, i.e. tightness of
(M_{n}-m_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.

We finish this section by sketching how we prove (
3.3
), in our opinion this is the most interesting part of our paper. We ignore the random part and the
\log
-term of the barrier in this sketch, the difficulties added by these are either already handled by
[
19
]
or minor.
1
1

1

Of course without the random part and the
\log
-term in the barrier we could just use the reflection principle to get an exact result. Because of this, the strategy we use is not needed in the homogeneous setting.
We also switch to continuous time, which can be justified using standard arguments. Thus let
(B_{s})_{s\geq 0}
be a Brownian motion with respect to
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}
. As a first step we use a correlation inequality for Brownian bridges to prove that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,n]}^{\xi_{0},J_{\xi_{0}}}(B_{\cdot})\right]\geq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,y^{2}]}^{\xi_{0},[Cy,cy]}(B_{\cdot})\right]\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[y^{2},n]}^{\xi_{0},J_{\xi_{0}}}(B_{\cdot})\right]

(3.5)

From
[
19
]
we know that there is a
\gamma>0
such that
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,y^{2}]}^{\xi_{0},[Cy,cy]}(B_{\cdot})\right]\geq C|y|^{-2\gamma}
with high probability, such that we approximately get

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,n]}^{\xi_{0},J_{\xi_{0}}}(B_{\cdot})\right]\geq C|y|^{-2\gamma}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[y^{2},n]}^{\xi_{0},J_{\xi_{0}}}(B_{\cdot})\right].

(3.6)

On the other hand by monotonicity we get

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,n]}^{y,J_{\xi_{0}}}(B_{\cdot})\right]\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[y^{2},n]}^{y,J_{\xi_{0}}}(B_{\cdot})\right].

(3.7)

By using (
3.5
), (
3.6
) and (
3.7
) we reduce the proof of (
3.3
) to proving an upper bound on

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[y^{2},n]}^{y,J_{\xi_{0}}}(B_{\cdot})\right]/\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[y^{2},n]}^{\xi_{0},J_{\xi_{0}}}(B_{\cdot})\right].

Using the Markov property for Brownian motion at time
y^{2}
we can write for
x\in\{\xi_{0},y\}

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[y^{2},n]}^{x,J_{\xi_{0}}}(B_{\cdot})\right]=\int_{-\infty}^{0}g_{x,y^{2}}(z)\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\forall_{s\in[y^{2},n]}z+(B_{s}-B_{y^{2}})\leq 0,z+(B_{n}-B_{y^{2}})\in J_{\xi_{0}}\right]\;\mathrm{d}z,

where we use
g_{\mu,\sigma^{2}}(z)
to denote the density of a normal distribution with mean
\mu
and variance
\sigma^{2}
. For
0\geq z\geq y\log(|y|)
we know that
g_{y,y^{2}}(z)/g_{\xi_{0},y^{2}}(z)\leq Cy^{c}
and we can compare the integral over this region for
x=\xi_{0}
to the same integral for
x=y
. The region
z\geq-C\log(t)
is negligible for
C
big enough, since we know from
[
19
]
that
\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,n]}^{y,J_{\xi_{0}}}(B_{\cdot})\right]
is polynomial in
t
. The region
z\in[-C\log(t),y\log(|y|)]
requires extra care, we handle this with an inductive scheme in which we recursively drop the barrier for a time interval depending on
z
and again split up the possible locations after this time interval into three regions. A more detailed overview of this inductive scheme is given in Section
8.5
.

3.1
Remarks on convergence in distribution

The natural next question is whether
M_{n}-m_{n}
converges in distribution. We think that this is the case and can be proved by improving our techniques and adopting the approach from
[
3
]
. We set
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},\frac{\cdot}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}\log(p_{n})}^{-z,J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]/p_{n}=:f_{n}(z)
,
z\in\mathbb{R}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
. One in our opinion sufficient improvement of (
3.3
), is to show that there exists a
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
such that for all big enough
z\in\mathbb{R}
,
a_{l}\to\infty

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}f_{n}(z)

\displaystyle=f(z),

(3.8)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{l\to\infty}\frac{f(z+a_{l})}{f(a_{l})}

\displaystyle=g(z),\qquad\lim\limits_{z\to\infty}f(z)=\infty.

(3.9)

If one were able to establish these equalities, it is likely that the procedure from
[
3
]
can also be applied to our situation, although we have not checked this carefully.

3.2
Difficulty in extending to the model from
[
20
]

As mentioned above, in
[
20
]
the environment
(\mathcal{L}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of point process laws instead of a sequence of natural numbers. As a consequence we’d need to change the definition of
\kappa_{n}(\vartheta)
and
v
from Definition
1.1
as follows

\kappa_{n}(\vartheta)=\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\sum_{\ell\in L_{n}}e^{\vartheta\ell}\right],\quad v=\inf_{\vartheta>0}\frac{\mathbb{E}[\kappa_{1}(\vartheta)]}{\vartheta},

where
L_{n}\sim\mathcal{L}_{n}
. Furthermore, we’d need to implicitly define
\vartheta^{\ast}>0
via
\vartheta^{\ast}\mathbb{E}[\kappa_{1}(\vartheta^{\ast})]-\mathbb{E}[\kappa_{1}(\vartheta^{\ast})]=0
. This has already been done in
[
20
, p. 3]
and we refer the reader there for more details on these changes and the assumptions, which need to be made. Beyond this, the more general model introduces two further difficulties.

(i)

We recall that for a particle
u
in generation
n
we use
u_{j}
to denote the
j
-th generational ancestor of
u
and
V(u)
to denote the position of
u
. For
u^{(1)}
,
u^{(2)}
two different descendants of a particle
u
the two random variables
(V(u^{(1)}_{j})-V(u))_{j\geq|u|}
,
V((u^{(2)}_{j})-V(u))_{j\geq|u|}
won’t be independent in the more general model. The main place in which we use this independence is in the upper bound of
\mathbb{E}[\Lambda_{k}^{2}(y)]
in Section
6.1
, which is used for the second moment calculation in Section
4.2.2
. There we consider pairs of particles
u^{(1)}
,
u^{(2)}
in generation
n
for which the last common ancestor
u:=u^{(1)}\wedge u^{(2)}
is in generation
k
. Then we condition on everything happening up to generation
k
, in particular
V(u)=V(u^{(1)}_{k})=V(u^{(2)}_{k})
is measurable with regard to this conditioning. We use that under this conditioning for any function
f:\mathbb{R}^{n-k}\to\{0,1\}
the events
\left\{f\left((V(u^{(1)}_{k+1}),\dots,V(u^{(1)}_{n})\right)=1\right\}
,
\left\{f\left((V(u^{(2)}_{k+1}),\dots,V(u^{(2)}_{n})\right)=1\right\}
are independent.

To fix this lack of independence, we can instead condition on everything happening up to generation
k+1
, such that
(V(u^{(1)}_{k+1}),V(u^{(2)}_{k+1}))
is measurable with regard to the conditioning. This change will slightly complicate the calculations in Section
6.1
, but won’t be a big deal. This was already relevant and dealt with for Section 4.2 in
[
20
]
, see for example their equation (4.5).

(ii)

The main difficulty in extending our result is that in the general model
S_{n}
and
B_{n}
from Definition
2.3
won’t be Gaussian random variables. As in Section 2 in
[
20
]
we’d need to define
S_{n}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}X_{k}
,
(X_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
independent with

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[X_{k}\leq x]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\sum_{\ell\in L_{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\ell\leq x}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}\ell-\kappa_{n}(\vartheta^{\ast})}\right],

which won’t be Gaussian in general. In Sections
8
and
9
we use that
(B_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Brownian motion evaluated at integer times. Namely, we use a correlation inequality for Brownian bridges, Lemma
8.5
, the explicit form of the Gaussian density and the Girsanov theorem, in particular we use that there is a simple way of tilting normal random variables in a way only changing the mean. Similar issues appear when talking about BRW in homogeneous environment and have been handled there, see
[
3
]
. The way Section 6 in
[
19
]
tackled this difficulty, is by approximating
(B_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
(and the barrier
(W_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
) by Brownian motion, using a variant of the KMT approximation for independent sums of random variables proved in
[
25
]
. This approach is somewhat more delicate in our situation for the following reason. Applying KMT on all of
[0,n]
at once introduces an error of size roughly
\log(n)^{c}
, which then changes the starting point relative to the barrier by
\log(n)^{c}
. This changes the probability by a factor, which is polynomial in
\log(n)
. Since
[
19
]
cares about

\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{-\log\left(\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\forall_{k\leq n}x+B_{k}\geq W_{k},B_{n}-W_{n}\in[a\sqrt{n},b\sqrt{n}\right]\right)}{\log(n)}

they can ignore such a multiplicative factor. However, for (
3.3
) we need to understand how changes of
O(1)
in the starting position change the probability and thus an error of size
\log(n)^{c}
in the starting point is far to rough for our results. This can be circumvented by splitting
[0,n]
into dyadic intervals
I_{1}
, …,
I_{\log_{2}(n)}
and using KMT on each of these intervals individually, but doing so is somewhat technical.

4
Proof of Theorem
1.2

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.2
leaving quite some details and proofs to the later sections.

4.1
Upper Bound on the Right Tail of
M_{n}-m_{n}

In this section, we prove the following theorem, postponing certain auxiliary estimates to Sections
5
and
12
.

Theorem 4.1
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a
y_{\varepsilon}<0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n}\geq-y_{\varepsilon}]\leq\varepsilon\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

The proof of Theorem
4.1
uses two ingredients. Lemma
4.2
controls the probability that
M_{n}-m_{n}
is large and Proposition
4.3
controls small to medium deviations of
M_{n}-m_{n}
.

Lemma 4.2

(Lemma 2.3 in
[
20
]
)
.

For any
y<0
, we have
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\exists u\in\mathbf{T}:V(u)+y>(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}K_{|u|}\right]\leq e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}.

Proposition 4.3
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n}\in[\xi_{0}-y-1,\xi_{0}-y]]\leq C_{\varepsilon}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
assuming Proposition
4.3
.

Take
y_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(-\infty,\xi_{0}]
such that for
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}
as in Proposition
4.3

C_{\varepsilon}\sum_{y\in(-\infty,y_{\varepsilon}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}^{\infty}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}y}\leq\varepsilon/2
. Then, by Proposition
4.3

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}-m_{n}\in[\xi_{0}-y_{\varepsilon},\xi_{0}+\log(n)^{2}\right]\leq\varepsilon/2\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

(4.1)

On the other hand, from Lemma
4.2
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}-m_{n}\geq\xi_{0}+\log(n)^{2}\right]\leq e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}\log(n)^{2}-\vartheta^{\ast}\xi_{0}-\log(p_{n})}.

(4.2)

Combining (
4.2
) and Lemma
2.6
yields that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}-m_{n}\geq\xi_{0}+\log(n)^{2}\right]\to 0
. In particular, this holds in probability, which implies

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}-m_{n}\geq\xi_{0}+\log(n)^{2}\right]\leq\varepsilon/2\right]=1.

(4.3)

Combining (
4.1
) and (
4.3
) finishes the proof.
∎

Thus it remains to prove Proposition
4.3
, which uses barrier computations. For this, we need the following definition, for which we recall (
1.1
), (
2.2
) and Definition
2.1
.

Definition 4.4
.

For
t>0
,
y,x<0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, define

\displaystyle h_{t}^{\smallfrown}

\displaystyle:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R},\ s\mapsto-(((1+s)\wedge(1+t-s))^{1/6}-1),

\displaystyle m_{t,h}^{\smallfrown}(s)

\displaystyle:=h_{t}^{\smallfrown}(s)-\frac{s}{t\vartheta^{\ast}}\log(p_{t}),

\displaystyle p_{n}^{\smallfrown,x}(y)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}}^{y,J_{x}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right],

(4.4)

See Figure
1
for an illustration of the event in (
4.4
). For
u
with
|u|=n
, let

\tau(u,y):=\min\left\{k\in\{0,\dots,n\}:V(u_{k})-(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}K_{k}+m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}(k)+y+1\geq 0\right\}

and
\tau(y):=\min_{u:|u|=n}\tau(u,y)\wedge n
. The dependency on
n
of
\tau(y)
is omitted from notation. Finally, define

\displaystyle A_{n}(y,x)

\displaystyle:=\bigcup_{u:|u|=n}\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}}^{y,J_{x}}(V(u_{\cdot})-(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}K_{\cdot}).

Figure 1:
The event in (
4.4
). Drawn are
(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast})_{k\leq n}
(dotted line),
(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}+\frac{k}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}\log(p_{n}))_{k\leq n}
(dashed line),
(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}+\frac{k}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}\log(p_{n})-h_{n}^{\smallfrown}(k)/2)_{k\leq n}
(thick black line) and a sample path of
(y+B_{s})_{s\leq n}
realizing the barrier event
\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}}^{y,J_{x}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})
(thin black line). We have the parameters
n=10
,
\mathcal{L}_{1}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{2,3\})
,
(\mathcal{L}_{k})_{k\leq 10}=(2,2,3,3,2,2,3,2,3,3)
.

It follows directly from the definitions that for all
y<0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}-m_{n}\in[\xi_{0}-y-1,\xi_{0}-y]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[\tau(y)<n]+\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[A_{n}(y,\xi_{0})\right].

(4.5)

It will be useful to be able to compare
p_{n}^{\smallfrown,x}(y)
from (
4.4
) to
p_{n}
from (
2.2
). The next Proposition
4.5
allows just that. Proposition
4.5
will be proved in Section
12
, the term
|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}|x-1|^{c_{\varepsilon}}
in Proposition
4.5
comes from moving the start- and endpoint. Going from discrete to continuous time and removing the curve of the barrier only costs a constant factor. We recall that
\xi_{0}
is negative.

Proposition 4.5
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{x\in[-\log(n),\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}\atop y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\frac{p_{n}^{\smallfrown,x}(y)}{p_{n}}\leq C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}|x-1|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

By (
4.5
), proving Proposition
4.3
is reduced to bounding
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[A_{n}(y,\xi_{0})]
and
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[\tau(y)<n]
, which we do in Propositions
4.6
and
4.7
.

Proposition 4.6
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
C_{\varepsilon},c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[A_{n}(y,\xi_{0})]\leq C_{\varepsilon}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Proof of Proposition
4.6
assuming Proposition
4.5
.

Applying Lemma
2.5
yields that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[A_{n}(y,\xi_{0})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[e^{-T_{n}};\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}}^{y,J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]\leq p_{n}^{-1}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}(1+y-\xi_{0})}p_{n}^{\smallfrown,\xi_{0}}(y).

Applying Proposition
4.5
and pulling the factor
|\xi_{0}-1|^{c_{\varepsilon}}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}(1-\xi_{0})}
into the
C_{\varepsilon}
, which is possible, since
\xi_{0}
is a fixed (negative) constant, finishes the proof.
∎

The proof of the forthcoming Proposition
4.7
appears in Section
5.1
.

Proposition 4.7
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[\tau(y)<n]\leq C_{\varepsilon}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Proof of Proposition
4.3
assuming Proposition
4.7
.

We combine (
4.5
), Proposition
4.6
and Proposition
4.7
.
∎

4.2
Lower Bound on the Right Tail of
M_{n}-m_{n}

Theorem 4.8
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that for
y\leq\xi_{0}
,

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n}\geq\xi_{0}-y-1]\geq e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{-c_{\varepsilon}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

We will prove Theorem
4.8
using a second moment approach. For this, we need the following definition, for which we recall the Definitions
1.1
,
2.1
and (
2.2
).

Definition 4.9
.

For
t>0
,
s\in[0,t]
,
y<0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, define

\displaystyle h_{t}^{\smallsmile}(s)

\displaystyle:=((1+s)\wedge(t-s+1))^{1/6}-1,

\displaystyle m_{t,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(s)

\displaystyle:=y+h_{t}^{\smallsmile}(s)-\frac{s}{t\vartheta^{\ast}}\log(p_{t}),\quad m_{t,h}^{\smallsmile}(s):=m_{t,h,0}^{\smallsmile}(s),

\displaystyle p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}}^{J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right],

(4.6)

\displaystyle Z_{n}(y)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|u|=n}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}}^{J_{\xi_{0}}}(V(u_{\cdot})-(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}K_{\cdot})}.

See Figure
2
for an illustration of the event in (
4.6
)

Figure 2:
The event in (
4.6
). Drawn are
(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast})_{k\leq n}
(dotted line),
(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}+\frac{k}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}\log(p_{n}))_{k\leq n}
(dashed line),
(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}+\frac{k}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}\log(p_{n})-h_{n}^{\smallsmile}(k))_{k\leq n}
(thick black line) and a sample path of
(y+B_{s})_{s\leq n}
realizing the barrier event
\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},m_{n,h/2}^{\smallsmile}}^{y,J_{x}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})
. We have the parameters
n=10
,
\mathcal{L}_{1}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{2,3\})
,
(\mathcal{L}_{k})_{k\leq 10}=(2,2,3,3,2,2,3,2,3,3)
.

The random variable
Z_{n}
counts the number of particles, which stay below the barrier
-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(j)+(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}K_{\cdot}
and end up in
[m_{n}+\xi_{0}-y-1,m_{n}+\xi_{0}-y]
, in particular we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n}\geq\xi_{0}-y-1]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)\geq 1]\geq\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)]^{2}}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)^{2}]},

(4.7)

where the last step uses the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality. We will establish the following two Propositions
4.10
,
4.11
. We prove Proposition
4.10
in Section
4.2.1
. We sketch the proof of Proposition
4.11
in Section
4.2.2
and fill in the detail in Section
6.1
.

Proposition 4.10
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that for all
y\leq\xi_{0}
,

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)]\geq C_{\varepsilon}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}|y|^{-c_{\varepsilon}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Proposition 4.11
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that for all
y\leq\xi_{0}
,

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)^{2}]\leq C_{\varepsilon}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)]\Big]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Proof of Theorem
4.8
assuming Propositions
4.10
and
4.11
.

Combining (
4.7
) and Proposition
4.11
yields that there are
c_{\varepsilon/2},C_{\varepsilon/2}>0
such that

1-\varepsilon/2\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n}\geq\xi_{0}-y-1]\geq\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)]}{1+C_{\varepsilon/2}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon/2}}/\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)]}\right].

Applying Proposition
4.10
yields that there are
C_{\varepsilon/2}^{\prime}
,
c_{\varepsilon/2}^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n}\geq\xi_{0}-y-1]\geq\frac{C_{\varepsilon/2}^{\prime}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}|y|^{-c_{\varepsilon/2}^{\prime}}}{1+C_{\varepsilon/2}C_{\varepsilon/2}^{\prime}e^{2\vartheta^{\ast}y}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon/2}+c_{\varepsilon/2}^{\prime}}}\right]

However,
C_{\varepsilon/2}C_{\varepsilon/2}^{\prime}e^{2\vartheta^{\ast}y}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon/2}+c_{\varepsilon/2}^{\prime}}
is bounded uniformly in
y<0
, such that the last display implies the statement of Theorem
4.8
.
∎

4.2.1
Lower Bound on
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)]

In this section, we sketch the proof of Proposition
4.10
, it closely resembles the proof of Proposition
4.6
, with the forthcoming Proposition
4.12
replacing Proposition
4.5
.

The proof has two steps, first applying the many-to-one Lemma
2.5
gives that

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)]\geq e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y-\vartheta^{\ast}\xi_{0}}p_{n}^{-1}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)\qquad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(4.8)

Similar to Proposition
4.5
we prove a lower bound on
p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)/p_{n}
, recall (
2.2
) and (
4.6
). The following Proposition
4.12
will be proved in Section
12
.

Proposition 4.12
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that for
y\leq\xi_{0}
,

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)}{p_{n}}\geq C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{-c_{\varepsilon}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Proof of Proposition
4.10
assuming Proposition
4.12
.

We combine (
4.8
) with Proposition
4.12
.
∎

Remark 4.13
.

The
|y|^{-c_{\varepsilon}}
in Proposition
4.12
is far from optimal, with more effort one can prove that the statement holds (as one expects) with
|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}
instead, however we do not need that.

4.2.2
Upper Bound on
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[Z_{n}(y)^{2}]

In this section, we outline the proof of Proposition
4.11
.

The random variable
Z_{n}(y)^{2}
roughly counts the number of pairs of particles
u,v
in generation
n
, for which both particles stay below the barrier
-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(\cdot)+(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}K_{\cdot}
and end up in
[m_{n}+\xi_{0}-y,m_{n}+\xi_{0}-y+1]
. We partition with regard to
|u\wedge v|
, which leads to the following definition.

Definition 4.14
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
y\leq\xi_{0}
, we define

\displaystyle\Lambda_{k}(y)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|u|=k}\sum_{|u^{1}|=|u^{2}|=n\atop u^{1}\wedge u^{2}=u}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(\cdot)}^{J_{\xi_{0}}}(V(u_{\cdot}^{i})-(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}K_{\cdot})}.

We have that

Z_{n}(y)^{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\Lambda_{k}(y)=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\Lambda_{k}(y)+Z_{n}(y),

(4.9)

where the second equality uses that
\Lambda_{n}(y)=Z_{n}(y)
. Thus all we need to do to prove Proposition
4.11
, is to bound
\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]
.

To do this, we introduce some additional notation. We recall Definitions
1.1
,
4.9
and the convention that
T_{n}^{(k)}=\vartheta^{\ast}\sum_{j=k+1}^{k+n}X_{j}-\kappa_{j}(\vartheta^{\ast})
.

Definition 4.15
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\in\{0,\dots,n-1\}
,
y\leq\xi_{0}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
define

\displaystyle f_{k}(x)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}^{k}\left[\sum_{|u|=n-k}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n-k\},m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k+\cdot)}^{x,J_{\xi_{0}}}(V(u_{\cdot})-(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}K_{k+\cdot})}\right],

\displaystyle q_{k}(x)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{k}\Bigg[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n-k\},m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k+\cdot)}^{x-K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast},J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}^{(k)}/\vartheta^{\ast})\Bigg].

(4.10)

Both
q_{k}(x)
and
f_{k}(x)
depend on
n
and
y
, which we omit from notation.

By conditioning on
\mathcal{F}_{k}:=\sigma(u,V(u):|u|\leq k)
we will prove the following upper bound on
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]
in Section
6.1.1
.

Proposition 4.16
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\in\{0,\dots,n-1\}
and
y\leq\xi_{0}
, we have that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]

\displaystyle\leq ce^{\vartheta^{\ast}y-2\vartheta^{\ast}\xi_{0}}p_{n}^{\frac{k}{n}-2}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallsmile}(k)}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)\max_{x\geq 0}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}x}q_{k}(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)-x).

In particular, we have that

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]\leq ce^{\vartheta^{\ast}y-2\vartheta^{\ast}\xi_{0}}p_{n}^{\frac{k}{n}-2}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallsmile}(k)}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y).

(4.11)

It will be useful to derive an upper bound on
p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)/p_{n}
, recall (
2.2
) and (
4.6
). This is done in the following proposition, which is proved in Section
12
.

Proposition 4.17
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}\atop}\left\{\frac{p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)}{p_{n}}\leq C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Remark 4.18
.

Proposition
4.17
also holds, with minor changes in the proof, when one replaces
J_{\xi_{0}}
in (
4.6
) by
[\xi_{0}-2,\xi_{0}]
, this version will be used in Section
6.1.2
for the proof of Lemma
6.2
.

The next two lemmata allow us to bound
\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}]
.

Lemma 4.19
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that for all
y\leq\xi_{0}
,

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{k=\lceil\log(n)^{7}\rceil}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]\leq C_{\varepsilon}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

(4.12)

Proof of Lemma
4.19
assuming Proposition
4.17
.

Applying Proposition
4.17
to equation (
4.11
) of Proposition
4.16
yields that there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that for
y\leq\xi_{0}

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{k\in[\log(n)^{7},n-1]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]\leq C_{\varepsilon}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}p_{n}^{\frac{k}{n}-1}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallsmile}(k)}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

(4.13)

By Lemma
2.6
there is a
C>0
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{|\log(p_{n})|}{\log(n)}\leq C
, which together with (
4.13
) implies that there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that for
y\leq\xi_{0}

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{k=\lceil\log(n)^{7}\rceil}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]\leq C_{\varepsilon}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{k=\lceil\log(n)^{7}\rceil}^{n-1}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallsmile}(k)}e^{C\log(n)\left(1-\frac{k}{n}\right)}\right].

(4.14)

We can bound
\sup_{n}\sum_{k=\lceil\log(n)^{7}\rceil}^{n-1}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallsmile}(k)}e^{C\log(n)\left(1-\frac{k}{n}\right)}<\infty
, which together with (
4.14
) finishes the proof.
∎

Handling
k<\log(n)^{7}
in (
4.12
) requires a more nuanced argument because the gain from
h_{n}^{\smallsmile}(k)
in (
4.14
) is not sufficient. We prove the following lemma in Section
6.1.2
.

Lemma 4.20
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that for all
y\leq\xi_{0}
,

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]\leq C_{\varepsilon}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Proof of Proposition
4.11
.

We combine (
4.9
) with Lemma
4.19
and Lemma
4.20
.
∎

4.3
Bound on the Left Tail of
M_{n}-m_{n}

The aim of this section is to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 4.21
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
y_{\varepsilon}\leq 0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}-m_{n}\leq y_{\varepsilon}\right]\leq\varepsilon\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

The idea is, to cut the tree at depth
l
, use that there are at least
2^{l}
particles in generation
l
, and then to finish the argument by applying the lower bound on the right tail proved in Theorem
4.8
.

Slightly more detailed, we note that, similarly to Lemma
4.2
, we get the following lemma.

Lemma 4.22
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime},c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}>0
such that for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\min_{u:|u|=l}V(u)\leq-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l\right]\leq e^{-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}l}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Then by cutting the tree at depth
l
one can make the calculation that there are
c_{\varepsilon},c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime},c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}
and
C_{\varepsilon}
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n-l}\leq y]\leq e^{-c^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}l}+\left(1-C_{\varepsilon}|y+c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l|^{-c_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{2^{l}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon,

and by choosing
l,y
right, but importantly independent of
n
, we get that
e^{-c^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}l}+\left(1-C_{\varepsilon}|y+c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l|^{-c_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{2^{l}}\leq\varepsilon
, which allows us to conclude Theorem
4.21
. In the final calculation we can’t use
m_{n-l}
, but will instead need to use time-shifted objects, also it will be necessary to prove, that
m_{n}-m_{n-l}
isn’t too big. All of this is done in Section
7
.

4.4
Proof of Theorem
1.2
assuming Theorems
4.1
and
4.21

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
y\geq 0
. Set

A_{n,\varepsilon}(y):=\left\{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[|M_{n}-m_{n}|\geq y]\leq\varepsilon/2\right\}.

By applying Theorems
4.1
and
4.21
there is a
y_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[A_{n,\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon})^{c}\right]\leq\varepsilon/2.

(4.15)

Furthermore, we have that

\displaystyle P[|M_{n}-m_{n}|\geq y_{\varepsilon}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[|M_{n}-m_{n}|\geq y_{\varepsilon}\right]\mathbf{1}_{A_{n,\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon})}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[|M_{n}-m_{n}|\geq y_{\varepsilon}\right]\mathbf{1}_{A_{n,\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon})^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon/2+\mathbb{P}\left[A_{n,\varepsilon}(y_{\varepsilon})^{c}\right]

(4.16)

Combining (
4.15
) and (
4.16
) yields that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}P[|M_{n}-m_{n}|\geq y_{\varepsilon}]\leq\varepsilon,

which gives that
(M_{n}-m_{n})_{n\geq 0}
is tight w.r.t.
P
.∎

5
Details for the Proof of Theorem
4.1
– Upper Bound on the Right Tail of
M_{n}-m_{n}

5.1
Proof of Proposition
4.7

We recall Definition
4.4
. Proposition
4.7
follows directly from the following two lemmata.

Lemma 5.1
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\tau(y)\leq n-\log(n)^{7}\right]\leq C_{\varepsilon}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}(y+1)}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Lemma 5.2
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
C_{\varepsilon},c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[n-\log(n)^{7}<\tau(y)<n\right]\leq C_{\varepsilon}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

For all
y<0
and all
k\leq n-\log(n)^{7}
we have that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[\tau(y)=k]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\sum_{u:|u|=k}\mathbf{1}_{\{V(u)-(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}K_{k}+m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}(k)+y+1\geq 0\}}\geq 1\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L.\ref{Lem:MT1}}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[e^{-T_{k}};T_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}+m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}(k)+y+1\geq 0\right]

\displaystyle\leq e^{\vartheta^{\ast}(y+1)}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallfrown}(k)/2}p_{n}^{-\frac{k}{n}}.

(5.1)

Combining Lemma
2.6
with (
5.1
) gives that there is a
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\forall_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\,\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[\tau(y)\leq n-\log(n)^{7}]\leq e^{\vartheta^{\ast}(y+1)}\hskip-3.0pt\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor n-\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}\hskip-3.0pte^{\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallfrown}(k)/2}n^{C_{\varepsilon}\frac{k}{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-\varepsilon.

(5.2)

From
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor n-\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallfrown}(k)/2}n^{C_{\varepsilon}\frac{k}{n}}<\infty
for all
C_{\varepsilon}>0
and (
5.2
) we can conclude the claim of the lemma.
∎

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Lemma
5.2
. First, we need an additional definition, after this we will give a brief overview over the structure of the proof of Lemma
5.2
. We recall the definition
T_{n}^{(k)}=\vartheta^{\ast}\sum_{j=k+1}^{k+n}X_{j}-\kappa_{j}(\vartheta^{\ast})
.

Definition 5.3
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\leq n
and
y\leq 0
we set

\displaystyle N_{k}(y)

\displaystyle:=\Bigg\{u\in\mathbf{T},|u|=n-k:\forall_{j\leq n-k-1}\,V(u_{j})-(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}K_{j}+m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}(j)+y+1\leq 0,

\displaystyle\qquad V(u_{n-k})-(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}K_{n-k}+m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}(n-k)+y+1\geq 0\Bigg\},

(5.3)

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{j}(y)

\displaystyle:=T_{j}/\vartheta^{\ast}+m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}(j)+y+1,

\displaystyle q_{k}(y)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\forall_{j\leq n-k-1}\,\widetilde{T}_{j}(y)\leq 0,\widetilde{T}_{j}(y)\geq 0\right].

The dependence on
n
is omitted from the notation.

For
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
x<0
we define

\displaystyle\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n-k-1\},m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}}^{y+1,J_{-l}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right],

(5.4)

\displaystyle q_{k,\text{end}}(x)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{n-k-1}\Bigg[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,k+1\},m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}(n-k-1+\cdot)}^{x-m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}(n-k-1),J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}^{(n-k-1)}/\vartheta^{\ast})\Bigg].

(5.5)

The dependence on
n
and
y
is omitted from the notation, we emphasize, that
q_{k,\text{end}}(x)
doesn’t depend on
y
.

From Definition
4.4
and (
5.3
) it is immediate that
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\tau(y)=n-k\right]\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[|N_{k}(y)|\geq 1\right]
. This together with the Markov inequality and the many-to-one Lemma
2.5
yields that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\tau(y)=n-k\right]\leq p_{n}^{-\frac{n-k}{n}}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}(y+1)+\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallfrown}(k)/2}q_{k}(y),

(5.6)

where we used that
h_{n}^{\smallfrown}(n-k)=h_{n}^{\smallfrown}(k)
. The idea is to decompose
q_{k}(y)
according to the position of
\widetilde{T}_{n-k-1}
. Lemmata
5.4
and
5.5
will establish, that

q_{k}(y)\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}e^{-c_{\varepsilon}l^{2}}\widetilde{q}_{k}(l),

(5.7)

i.e. that
\widetilde{T}_{n-k-1}\approx l
costs an
e^{-c_{\varepsilon}l^{2}}
factor. The term
\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)
then needs to be compared to
p_{n}^{-1}
, recall (
2.2
), for this we want to use the barrier computation of Proposition
4.5
. To do this we need to compute an upper bound on
\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)(p_{n}^{\smallfrown,-l}(y))^{-1}
, recall (
4.4
). This is done in Corollary
5.6
, which is proved using Lemma
2.8
. Corollary
5.7
handles the summation over
l
in (
5.7
). Finally, we need one more barrier computation, which is stated in Lemma
5.8
and proved in Section
12
.

Lemma 5.4
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c,C>0
such that for all
x\geq 0
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[(T_{n-k}-T_{n-k-1})/\vartheta^{\ast}\geq x\right]\leq Ce^{-cx^{2}}.

Proof.

We have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[(T_{n-k}-T_{n-k-1})/\vartheta^{\ast}\geq x\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[X_{n-k}-(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}\log(\mathcal{L}_{n-k})-\frac{(\vartheta^{\ast})}{2}\geq x\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[X_{1}-\frac{\vartheta^{\ast}}{2}\geq x+(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}\log(\mathcal{L}_{n-k})\right]

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{(x+(\vartheta^{\ast})^{-1}\log(\mathcal{L}_{n-k})-\vartheta^{\ast}/2)^{2}}{2}}\leq Ce^{-cx^{2}}

for some
c,C>0
, since
\log(\mathcal{L}_{n-k})\geq 0
. The second to last step has used that
X_{1}-\vartheta^{\ast}/2
is
\mathcal{N}(\vartheta^{\ast}/2,1)
distributed.
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
c_{\varepsilon,1}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\forall_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\forall_{k\in\{1,\dots,\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor\}}\,q_{k}(y)\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)Ce^{-c_{\varepsilon,1}l^{2}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Proof.

This proof is mainly partitioning with respect to the value of
\widetilde{T}_{n-k-1}
and using that by Lemma
5.4
having
\widetilde{T}_{n-k-1}\approx-l
and
\widetilde{T}_{n-k}\geq 0
costs roughly
e^{-cl^{2}}
, where considering
\widetilde{T}_{k}
instead of
T_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}
will only change the
c
.

We have that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}
,
k\in\{1,\dots,\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor\}

\displaystyle q_{k}(y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\forall_{j\leq n-k-1}\,\widetilde{T}_{j}\leq 0,\widetilde{T}_{n-k}\geq 0,\widetilde{T}_{n-k-1}\in[-l-1,-l]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\forall_{j\leq n-k-1}\,\widetilde{T}_{j}\leq 0,\widetilde{T}_{n-k-1}\in[-l-1,-l],\widetilde{T}_{n-k}-\widetilde{T}_{n-k-1}\geq l\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L.\ref{Lem:UBDeltaT},D.\ref{Def:tkhard}}}{{\leq}}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)Ce^{-c\left(\log(p_{n})/(n\vartheta^{\ast})+1/2(k^{1/6}-(k-1)^{1/6})+l\right)^{2}}.

(5.8)

The statement of the lemma follows from (
5.8
) and Lemma
2.6
.
∎

We recall the definitions (
4.4
), (
5.4
) and (
5.5
).

Corollary 5.6
.

Fix
k\leq n
. For
0\leq l\leq|\xi_{0}|
, we have that

\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}q_{k,\text{end}}(x)\leq p_{n}^{\smallfrown,\xi_{0}}(y).

(5.9)

For
l\geq|\xi_{0}|
we have that

\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}q_{k,\text{end}}(x)\leq p_{n}^{\smallfrown,-l}(y).

(5.10)

Proof.

First, let
l\leq|\xi_{0}|
. By applying (
2.3
) from Lemma
2.8
for
Z_{\cdot}=T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast}+m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}
,
z_{1}=y
,
z_{2}=\xi_{0}
,
I=\{0,\dots,n\}
,
t_{0}=n-k-1
and
x_{0}=-l
we get, that

\displaystyle p_{n}^{\smallfrown,\xi_{0}}(y)

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)\inf_{x\in J_{x_{0}-1}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{n-k-1,\dots,n\},m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}-m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}(n-k-1)}^{x,J_{\xi_{0}}}((T_{\cdot}-T_{n-k-1})/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)\inf_{x\in[-l-2,-l-1]}q_{k,\text{end}}(x),

(5.11)

where the last step follows from (
5.5
) and a time shift. Since
l\leq|\xi_{0}|
equation (
5.11
) implies (
5.9
).

The case
l\geq|\xi_{0}|
, i.e. proving (
5.10
), is analogous, but we take
z_{2}=-l
instead, thus we get

\displaystyle p_{n}^{\smallfrown,-l}(y)

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)\inf_{x\in[-l-2,-l-1]}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{n-k-1}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,k+1\},m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}(n-k-1+\cdot)}^{x-m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}(n-k-1),J_{-l}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,\xi_{0}-1]}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{n-k-1}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,k+1\},m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}(n-k-1+\cdot)}^{x-\xi_{0}-l-m_{n,h/2}^{\smallfrown}(n-k-1),J_{-l}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,\xi_{0}-1]}q_{k,\text{end}}(x),

where the last step used that since
l\geq|\xi_{0}|
we have
\xi_{0}+l\geq 0
. Now, observing that
[\xi_{0}-2,\xi_{0}-1]\subseteq[\xi_{0}-2,-1]
finishes the proof.
∎

Corollary 5.7
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
C_{\varepsilon},c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\forall_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\forall_{k\in\{1,\dots,\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor\}}\,p_{n}^{-1}q_{k}(y)\leq\left(\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}q_{k,\text{end}}(x)\right)^{-1}C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Proof.

To shorten the displays in this proof we set

I_{n}:=([-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z})\times\{1,\dots,\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor\}.

Furthermore, in this proof
\sum_{j=a}^{b}
is to be read as
\sum_{j=\lceil a\rceil}^{\lfloor b\rfloor}
in case that
a,b
aren’t integers.

With
c_{\varepsilon,1}
from Lemma
5.5
we have that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\forall_{(y,k)\in I_{n}}\,p_{n}^{-1}q_{k}(y)\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}p_{n}^{-1}\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)Ce^{-c_{\varepsilon,1}l^{2}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

(5.12)

We first handle the summation from
l=0
to
|\xi_{0}|
in (
5.12
). By (
5.9
) we have that for all
(y,k)\in I_{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{|\xi_{0}|}p_{n}^{-1}\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)Ce^{-c_{\varepsilon,1}l^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}q_{k,\text{end}}(x)\right)^{-1}\sum_{l=0}^{|\xi_{0}|}p_{n}^{\smallfrown,\xi_{0}}(y)p_{n}^{-1}Ce^{-c_{\varepsilon,1}l^{2}}.

(5.13)

By applying Proposition
4.5
and using that
e^{-c_{\varepsilon,1}l^{2}}
is summable, (
5.13
) implies that there are
c_{\varepsilon}
,
C_{\varepsilon}
such that

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon/3

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\forall_{(y,k)\in I_{n}}\,\sum_{l=0}^{|\xi_{0}|}p_{n}^{-1}\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)Ce^{-c_{\varepsilon,1}l^{2}}\leq\left(\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}q_{k,\text{end}}(x)\right)^{-1}C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right]

(5.14)

Now we handle the summation from
|\xi_{0}|
to
\sqrt{\log(n)\lambda/c_{\varepsilon,1}}
in (
5.12
), where
\lambda>0
is such that
p_{n}^{-1}n^{-\lambda}
converges to zero
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. according to Lemma
2.6
.

A completely parallel argument to the proof of (
5.14
), but using the summability of
e^{-c_{\varepsilon,1}l^{2}}l^{c_{\varepsilon}}
, yields that there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon/3

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\forall_{(y,k)\in I_{n}}\,\hskip-16.36024pt\sum_{l=|\xi_{0}|}^{\sqrt{\log(n)\lambda/c_{\varepsilon,1}}}\hskip-11.38092ptp_{n}^{-1}\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)Ce^{-c_{\varepsilon,1}l^{2}}\leq\left(\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}q_{k,\text{end}}(x)\right)^{-1}\hskip-4.26773ptC_{\varepsilon}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right]

(5.15)

Finally, we handle
l>\sqrt{\log(n)\lambda/c_{\varepsilon,1}}
in (
5.12
). Since
\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)
is a probability it is smaller than
1
, which yields that

\displaystyle\sum_{l=\sqrt{\log(n)\lambda/c_{\varepsilon,1}}}^{\infty}p_{n}^{-1}\widetilde{q}_{k}(l)Ce^{-c_{\varepsilon,1}l^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}p_{n}^{-1}e^{-\lambda\log(n)}

(5.16)

Since
p_{n}^{-1}e^{-\lambda\log(n)}\to 0
in probability, equations (
5.12
), (
5.14
), (
5.15
) and (
5.16
) finish the proof.
∎

We need one more Lemma for the proof of Lemma
5.2
, the proof of this lemma involves barrier computations and thus is done in Section
12
. We recall the Definition (
5.5
) of
q_{k,\text{end}}(x)
.

Lemma 5.8
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallfrown}(k)/2}\left(\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}q_{k,\text{end}}(x)\right)^{-1}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

We have now collected all ingredients necessary to finish the proof of Lemma
5.2

Proof of Lemma
5.2
assuming Lemma
5.8
.

Applying (
5.6
) yields that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[n-\log(n)^{7}<\tau(y)<n\right]\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}(y+1)}p_{n}^{-1}q_{k}(y)e^{\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallfrown}(k)/2}.

(5.17)

Combining Corollary
5.7
and Lemma
5.8
implies that there are
C_{\varepsilon},c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

1-\varepsilon\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\forall_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\,\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}p_{n}^{-1}q_{k}(y)e^{\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallfrown}(k)/2}\leq C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right]

(5.18)

Equations (
5.17
) and (
5.18
) together imply Lemma
5.2
.
∎

6
Details for Theorem
4.8
– Lower Bound on the Right Tail of
M_{n}-m_{n}

6.1
Proof of Proposition
4.11

6.1.1
Proof of Proposition
4.16

In this section, fix
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\in\{0,\dots,n-1\}
and
y\leq\xi_{0}
. We recall Definitions
4.9
and
4.15
. Furthermore, define

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}(u,j)

\displaystyle:=V(u_{j})-K_{j}/\vartheta^{\ast}+m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(j),

\displaystyle A_{k}(u)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\forall_{j\leq k}\,\widetilde{V}(u,j)\leq 0\right\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{j}

\displaystyle:=T_{j}/\vartheta^{\ast}+m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(j),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\sigma(u,V(u):|u|\leq k).

We will use two results on
f_{k}
and
q_{k}
in the proof of Proposition
4.16
. First, as a direct consequence of the many-to-one Lemma
2.5
applied to
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{k}
we have that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}

f_{k}(x)\leq ce^{\vartheta^{\ast}x+\vartheta^{\ast}y-\vartheta^{\ast}\xi_{0}-K_{k}}p_{n}^{-1}q_{k}(x)\qquad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(6.1)

Furthermore, by conditioning on
\mathcal{F}_{k}
it follows directly from the definitions, that for all
y<0

p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[q_{k}((T_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}+K_{k})/\vartheta^{\ast});\forall_{j\leq k}\,\widetilde{T}_{j}\leq 0\right]\qquad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

(6.2)

Proof of Proposition
4.16
.

We first use the tree structure by conditioning on
\mathcal{F}_{k}
, which gives that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)|\mathcal{F}_{k}]

\displaystyle\leq c\sum_{|u|=k}\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}(u)}f_{k}(V(u))^{2}.

The
\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}(u)}
just means that
u
stays below the barrier up until time
k
, and the
f_{k}(V(u))^{2}
is an upper bound for the number of pairs of particles, which descend from
u
for which both particles stay below the barrier in
\{k,\dots,n\}
and end up at the right place. In this calculation, we use that given
V(u)
and
\mathcal{L}
the events that two different particles behave this way are independent.

Applying (
6.1
) to bound
f_{k}(V(u))
and taking expectation gives that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]\leq c\Gamma_{k}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\sum_{|u|=k}\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}(u)}\left(e^{\vartheta^{\ast}V(u)-K_{k}}q_{k}(V(u))\right)^{2}\right],

where
\Gamma_{k}:=e^{2\vartheta^{\ast}y-2\vartheta^{\ast}\xi_{0}}p_{n}^{-2}
.

If we wouldn’t have the square, applying the many-to-one Lemma
2.5
and (
6.2
) would allow us to bound
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}]
by
c\Gamma_{k}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)
; so we want to pull the factor
e^{\vartheta^{\ast}V(u)-K_{k}}q_{k}(V(u))
out of the expectation. For this, we bound it by
\max_{x\geq 0}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}x-\vartheta^{\ast}m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)}q_{k}(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)-x)
, which gives that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]

\displaystyle\leq c\Gamma_{k}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}y-\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallsmile}(k)}p_{n}^{\frac{k}{n}}\max_{x\geq 0}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}x}q_{k}(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)-x)

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{|u|=k}\mathbf{1}_{A_{k}(u)}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}V(u)-K_{k}}q_{k}(V(u))\right].

Applying Lemma
2.5
then gives that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]

\displaystyle\leq c\Gamma_{k}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}y-\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallsmile}(k)}p_{n}^{\frac{k}{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[q_{k}((T_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}+K_{k})/\vartheta^{\ast});\forall_{j\leq k}\,\widetilde{T}_{j}\leq 0\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\max_{x\leq 0}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}x}q_{k}(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)-x),

Applying (
6.2
) finishes the proof of Proposition
4.16
.∎

6.1.2
Proof of Lemma
4.20

We recall (
2.2
) and Definitions
4.9
,
4.15
. In order to derive an upper bound on
\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]
we need an upper bound on
p_{n}^{-1}\max_{x\geq 0}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}x}q_{k}(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)-x)
. For this, we need one more definition.

Definition 6.1
.

Set

\displaystyle p_{\text{start},k}:=\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-1,0]}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,k\},m_{n,h}^{\smallsmile}}^{\xi_{0},J_{x}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right].

The proof of Lemma
4.20
is split into two further lemmata. The proof of Lemma
6.3
involves barrier computations and thus is done in Section
12
.

Lemma 6.2
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{k\leq\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}\left\{p_{n}^{-1}\max_{x\geq 0}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}x}q_{k}(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)-x)\leq C_{\varepsilon}(p_{\text{start},k})^{-1}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Lemma 6.3
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallsmile}(k)}(p_{\text{start},k})^{-1}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Proof of Lemma
4.20
assuming Lemma
6.2
,
6.3
.

Plugging Lemma
6.2
and Lemma
6.3
into Proposition
4.16
, yields that there exists a
C_{\varepsilon/2}>0
such that

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon/2

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}[\Lambda_{k}(y)]\leq C_{\varepsilon/2}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}p_{n}^{-1}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)\right],

(6.3)

where
e^{-2\vartheta^{\ast}\xi_{0}}
has been pulled into the
C_{\varepsilon/2}
, which is fine, since
\xi_{0}
is a (negative) constant.

Applying Proposition
4.17
yields that there are
C_{\varepsilon/2},c_{\varepsilon/2}>0
such that

1-\varepsilon/2\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[p_{n}^{-1}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)\leq C_{\varepsilon/2}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon/2}}\right].

(6.4)

Combining (
6.3
) and (
6.4
) yields the claim of Lemma
4.20
.
∎

To prepare the proof of Lemma
6.2
we need a statement parallel to Corollary
5.6
. The proof is analogous, but using (
2.4
) instead of (
2.3
). We recall (
4.6
) and (
4.10
).

Corollary 6.4
.

Fix
k\leq n
. For
x\geq|\xi_{0}|
we have that

\displaystyle q_{k}\left(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)-x\right)\leq\left(\sum_{j=0}^{1}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}-j}(-\lceil x\rceil)\right)\left(p_{\text{start},k}\right)^{-1}.

(6.5)

For
0\leq x\leq|\xi_{0}|
, we have that

\displaystyle q_{k}(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)-x)\leq\left(\sum_{j=0}^{1}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}-j}(\xi_{0})\right)\left(p_{\text{start},k}\right)^{-1}.

(6.6)

Proof.

We begin with the proof of (
6.5
). We apply (
2.4
) for
Z_{\cdot}=T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast}+m_{n,h}^{\smallsmile}
,
z_{1}=-\lceil x\rceil
,
z_{2}=\xi_{0}
,
I=\{0,\dots,n\}
,
t_{0}=k
and
x_{0}=-x
and get that

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j=0}^{1}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}-j}(-\lceil x\rceil)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,k\},m_{n,h}^{\smallsmile}}^{-\lceil x\rceil,J_{x_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{k,\dots,n\},m_{n,h}^{\smallsmile}-m_{n,h}^{\smallsmile}(k)}^{x_{0},J_{\xi_{0}}}((T_{\cdot}-T_{k})/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,k\},m_{n,h}^{\smallsmile}}^{-\lceil x\rceil,J_{-x}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]q_{k}(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)-x)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,k\},m_{n,h}^{\smallsmile}}^{\xi_{0},J_{\xi_{0}+\lceil x\rceil-x}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]q_{k}(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)-x),

where the last step used that
-\lceil x\rceil\leq\xi_{0}
and the second equality uses the definition (
4.10
) of
q_{k}
as well as

m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k+\cdot)-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)=m_{n,h}^{\smallsmile}(k+\cdot)-m_{n,h}^{\smallsmile}(k).

Since
\xi_{0}+\lceil x\rceil-x\in[\xi_{0}-1,0]
this allows us to conclude (
6.5
).

In the case
x\leq|\xi_{0}|
we take
z_{1}=\xi_{0}
instead. The application of (
2.4
) yields that

\left(\sum_{j=0}^{1}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}-j}(\xi_{0})\right)\geq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,k\},m_{n,h}^{\smallsmile}}^{\xi_{0},J_{-x}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]q_{k}(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)-x)

and since
-x\in[\xi_{0}-1,0]
this allows us to conclude (
6.6
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

Define
d_{0}=0
,
d_{1}=-\xi_{0}
,
d_{2}=\log(n)^{2}
and
d_{3}=\infty
and

\pi_{j,k}:=\max_{x\in[d_{j-1},d_{j}]}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}x}p_{n}^{-1}q_{k}(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)-x),\qquad j=1,2,3,\quad k\leq\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor.

By definition we have
\max_{x\geq 0}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}x}p_{n}^{-1}q_{k}(K_{k}/\vartheta^{\ast}-m_{n,h,y}^{\smallsmile}(k)-x)=\max_{j\leq 3}\{\pi_{j,k}\}
, thus it suffices to prove that for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
,
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
C_{\varepsilon/3}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{k\leq\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}\left\{\pi_{j,k}\leq C_{\varepsilon/3}(p_{\text{start},k})^{-1}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon/3.

(6.7)

The case
j=1
:
By applying (
6.6
) we have that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
k\leq\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor

\displaystyle\pi_{1,k}\leq p_{n}^{-1}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{1}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}-j}(\xi_{0}-1)\right)(p_{\text{start},k})^{-1}

(6.8)

Applying Proposition
4.17
yields that there is a
C_{\varepsilon/3}>0
such that

1-\varepsilon/3\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[p_{n}^{-1}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{1}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}-j}(\xi_{0}-1)\right)\leq C_{\varepsilon/3}\right]

(6.9)

equations (
6.8
) and (
6.9
) yield (
6.7
) for
j=1
.

The case
j=2
:
By applying (
6.5
) we have that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
k\leq\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor

\displaystyle\pi_{2,k}\leq\max_{-\xi_{0}\leq x\leq\log(n)^{2}}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}x}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{1}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}-j}(-\lceil x\rceil)\right)\left(p_{\text{start},k}\right)^{-1}.

(6.10)

Applying Proposition
4.17
yields that there are
c_{\varepsilon/3},C_{\varepsilon/3}>0
such that

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon/3

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\forall_{x\in[-\log(n),\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\,p_{n}^{-1}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{1}p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}-j}(x)\right)\leq C_{\varepsilon/3}|x|^{c_{\varepsilon/3}}\right],

(6.11)

and since
\max_{x\geq 0}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}x}\lceil x\rceil^{c_{\varepsilon/3}}\leq C_{\varepsilon/3}<\infty
equations (
6.10
) and (
6.11
) yield (
6.7
) for
j=2
.

The case
j=3
:
By bounding
q_{k}(x)\leq 1
we have that
\pi_{3,k}\leq p_{n}^{-1}e^{-\log(n)^{2}}
, which
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. converges to 0 by Lemma
2.6
. This gives (
6.7
) for
j=3
.

Thus we have proved (
6.7
) for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
which finishes the proof.
∎

7
Proof of Theorem
4.21
– Cutting the Tree

We recall Definitions
1.1
,
2.1
and
2.3
. Furthermore, we recall the convention that under
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{l}
we write
S_{n}=X_{l+1}+\dots+X_{n+l}
to be shifted in time by
l
, without this being reflected in the notation. We also extend this convention to
T_{n}
, i.e. under
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{l}
we have
T_{n}=\vartheta^{\ast}\sum_{j=l+1}^{n+l}\left(X_{j}-\kappa_{j}(\vartheta^{\ast})\right)
.

Proof of Lemma
4.22
.

Since our jumps are symmetric, we have that
\min_{u:|u|=n}V(u)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}-M_{n}
. Thus by Lemma
4.2
we have that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}<-2K_{n}/\vartheta^{\ast}\right]\leq e^{-K_{n}}.

It now suffices to prove, that there are
c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
,
c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[K_{n}\geq c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}n]

\displaystyle\geq 1-\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[K_{n}\leq c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}n]

\displaystyle\geq 1-\varepsilon.

This is true since
K_{n}/n
converges
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. because the
\kappa_{k}(\vartheta^{\ast})
are i.i.d. with finite variance.
∎

Definition 7.1
.

For
n,l\in\mathbb{N}
, set

\displaystyle K_{n}^{(l)}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{j=l+1}^{n+l}\kappa_{j}(\vartheta^{\ast}),

\displaystyle p_{n}^{(l)}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{l}\left[\forall_{s\leq n}\,\xi_{0}+T_{s}/\vartheta^{\ast}\leq 0,\xi_{0}+T_{n}/\vartheta^{\ast}\in[\xi_{0}-1,\xi_{0}]\right],

\displaystyle m_{n}^{(l)}

\displaystyle:=K_{n}^{(l)}/\vartheta^{\ast}+\log(p_{n}^{(l)})/\vartheta^{\ast}.

These are the by
l
time-shifted analogues of
K_{n}
,
p_{n}
and
m_{n}
.

We note that the following version of Theorem
4.8
holds, since we only make a statement about the distribution of
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[\dots]
, which is invariant under time-shifts

Corollary 7.2
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon}>0
,
C_{\varepsilon}\in(0,1]
such that for
y\leq\xi_{0}
,

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{l}[M_{n-l}-m_{n-l}^{(l)}\geq\xi_{0}-y-1]\geq e^{\vartheta^{\ast}y}C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{-c_{\varepsilon}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Lemma 7.3
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon}
,
C_{\varepsilon}
,
c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
,
c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}>0
such that for
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
y\in[-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l+1,0]
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n-l}^{(l)}\leq y]\leq e^{-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}l}+\left(1-C_{\varepsilon}|\xi_{0}-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l-y-1|^{-c_{\varepsilon}}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}(\xi_{0}-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l-y-1))}\right)^{2l}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-\varepsilon

Proof.

We have that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}-m_{n-l}^{(l)}\leq y\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{l}\leq-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l]+\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{l}\geq-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l,\forall_{v:|v|=l}\,\max_{u:|u|=n,u_{l}=v}V(u)-m_{n-l}^{(l)}\leq c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l+y\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{l}\leq-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l]+\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{l}\left[M_{n-l}-m_{n-l}^{(l)}\leq c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l+y\right]^{2l}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{l}\leq-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l]+\left(1-\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{l}\left[M_{n-l}-m_{n-l}^{(l)}\geq c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l+y\right]\right)^{2l}.

Now applying Lemma
4.22
and Corollary
7.2
yields the claim.
∎

Lemma 7.4
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
l_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
,
y_{\varepsilon}<0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n-l_{\varepsilon}}^{(l_{\varepsilon})}\leq y_{\varepsilon}]\leq\varepsilon\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Proof.

Choosing
y=-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l+1
in Lemma
7.3
yields that

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[M_{n}-m_{n-l}^{(l)}\leq-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}l+1\right]\leq e^{-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}l}+\left(1-C_{\varepsilon}|\xi_{0}-2|^{-c_{\varepsilon}}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}(\xi_{0}-2)}\right)^{2l}\Bigg].

Since
0<C_{\varepsilon}\leq 1
we have
\left(1-C_{\varepsilon}|\xi_{0}-2|^{-c_{\varepsilon}}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}(\xi_{0}-2)}\right)\in[0,1)
. Thus, we can choose
l
such that
e^{-c_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}l}+\left(1-C_{\varepsilon}|\xi_{0}-2|^{-c_{\varepsilon}}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}(\xi_{0}-2)}\right)^{2l}\leq\varepsilon
, which yields the claim.
∎

Lemma 7.5
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
,
l\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
there is a
C_{\varepsilon,l}>0
such that,

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\left|K_{n}-K_{n-l}^{(l)}\right|\leq C_{\varepsilon,l}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-\varepsilon,

(7.1)

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\log(p_{n})-\log(p_{n-l}^{(l)})\leq C_{\varepsilon,l}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-\varepsilon.

(7.2)

Furthermore, for all
\varepsilon>0
,
l\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
there is a

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[m_{n}-m_{n-l}^{(l)}\leq C_{\varepsilon,l}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon

The first statement is immediate since by definition
K_{n}-K_{n-l}^{(l)}=K_{l}
and
K_{l}/l\to 0

\mathbb{P}

For the proof of the second statement we define for

,

,
x\leq 0

\displaystyle p_{n-l}^{(l)}(x)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{l}\left[\forall_{s\leq n-l}x+T_{s}/\vartheta^{\ast}\leq 0,\xi_{0}+T_{n}/\vartheta^{\ast}\in[\xi_{0}-1,\xi_{0}]\right],

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}_{\text{start},l}

\displaystyle:=\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-1,0]}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,l]}^{\xi_{0},J_{x}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right].

As in Corollary
6.4
applying (
2.4
) yields that for
\xi_{0}\leq x\leq 0

\displaystyle p_{n-l}^{(l)}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,n]}^{\xi_{0},[\xi_{0}-2,\xi_{0}]}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right](\widetilde{p}_{\text{start},l})^{-1},\quad\text{for}\ \xi_{0}\leq x\leq 0.

(7.3)

We use
g_{\mu,\sigma^{2}}(x)
to denote the Gaussian density with mean

and variance

. We use the Markov property at time

to get that

\displaystyle p_{n}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{0}g_{\xi_{0}-W_{l},l}(z)p_{n-l}^{(l)}(z)\;\mathrm{d}z

(7.4)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:TimeShiftToSpaceShift}}}{{\leq}}\int_{-\infty}^{\xi_{0}}g_{\xi_{0}-W_{l},l}(x)p_{n-l}^{(l)}(x)\;\mathrm{d}x+(\widetilde{p}_{\text{start},l})^{-1}\int_{\xi_{0}}^{0}g_{\xi_{0}-W_{l},l}(x)\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,n]}^{\xi_{0},[\xi_{0}-2,\xi_{0}]}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]\;\mathrm{d}x.

Similarly to Proposition
4.5
we see that
2
2

2

Technically we use a time-shifted version of the result. For these the constants bounded in Section

for all
\varepsilon>0

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon/3

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{x\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\frac{\inf_{z\in[x-1,x]}p_{n-l}^{(l)}(x)}{p_{n-l}^{(l)}}\leq C_{\varepsilon,l}|x|^{c_{\varepsilon,l}}\right\}\right],

(7.5)

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,n]}^{\xi_{0},[\xi_{0}-2,\xi_{0}]}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]}{p_{n}}\right\}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\right].

Actually we have shifted the starting point by one compared to the precise statement of Lemma

.
is a

\mathbb{P}
