Proof of Theorem
4.21
.

By Lemma
7.4
there are
l_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
,
z_{\varepsilon}<0
such that

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon/2

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n-l_{\varepsilon}}^{(l_{\varepsilon})}\leq z_{\varepsilon}]\leq\varepsilon\right].

(7.9)

Since for all
y<0

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n}\leq y]=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n-l_{\varepsilon}}^{(l_{\varepsilon})}\leq m_{n}-m_{n-l_{\varepsilon}}^{(l_{\varepsilon})}+y],

Lemma
7.5
implies that there is a
C_{\varepsilon,l_{\varepsilon}}>0
such that for all
y<0

1-\varepsilon/2\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n}\leq y]\leq\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[M_{n}-m_{n-l_{\varepsilon}}^{(l_{\varepsilon})}\leq y+C_{\varepsilon,l_{\varepsilon}}]\right].

(7.10)

Taking
y_{\varepsilon}:=z_{\varepsilon}-C_{\varepsilon,l_{\varepsilon}}
in (
7.10
) and combining it with (
7.9
) yields the claim of the theorem.
∎

8
Shift of the Starting Point – Preparation for Section
12

In this section, let
B
be a Brownian motion starting at
x
with regard to
\mathbb{P}_{x}
. For
t\geq 0
let
W^{(t)}:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R}
be continuous with
W_{0}=0
and let
h_{t}:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R}
be a continuous function with
h_{t}(0)=h_{t}(t)=0
. We will use
W
and
h
as a shorthand for
(W^{(t)})_{t\geq 0}
,
(h_{t})_{t\geq 0}
, we will suppress the upper index of
W^{(t)}_{s}
and instead write
W_{s}
. Additional assumptions on
h
and
W
are stated in Section
8.1
together with further definitions, in Section
11
we check that the assumptions hold for the specific
W
we use in the first half of the paper. In Section
8.2
we will state the results of Section
8
. Sections
8.3
,
8.4
,
8.5
contain the proofs of Theorems
8.2
,
8.3
and
8.5
respectively.

8.1
Definitions and Assumptions

We recall Definition
2.1
.

Definition 8.1
.

For
y_{0},y,x\leq 0
, define

\displaystyle p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],h_{t}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(B_{\cdot})\right],

\displaystyle p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y,x)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[x,t],h_{t}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(B_{\cdot})\right].

For
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
,
\sigma^{2}>0
write

g_{\mu,\sigma^{2}}(x):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\sigma^{2}}}e^{-\frac{(x-\mu)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}

(8.1)

for the Gaussian density function.

Define

\displaystyle 1\vee\sup_{s\in[e,t]}\frac{|W_{s}|}{\sqrt{s}\cdot\sqrt{\log(s)}}

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{C}_{\log}(W,t)=:\mathcal{C}_{\log},

(8.2)

\displaystyle 1\vee\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\frac{\max\{|h_{t}(s)|,|h_{t}(t-s)|\}}{\sqrt{1+s}}

\displaystyle=:C_{1}(h):=C_{1},

(8.3)

and assume that both quantities are finite. Furthermore, set

\displaystyle\gamma:=\gamma(W,h,t)

\displaystyle:=\inf\left\{\gamma\geq 0:\forall_{2\leq s\leq t/2}\,\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,s],h_{t}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{-1,[-4(C_{1}-1)\sqrt{s},-2C_{1}\sqrt{s}]}(B_{\cdot})\right]\geq s^{-\gamma}\right\}.

(8.4)

We assume that
\gamma>0
. Furthermore, define

\displaystyle e^{-128-16C_{1}^{2}-80\mathcal{C}_{\log}^{2}-134C_{1}-96\mathcal{C}_{\log}-32C_{1}\mathcal{C}_{\log}}

\displaystyle=:C_{2}(W,h,t)=:C_{2}.

(8.5)

The constants in the definition of
C_{2}
aren’t important, what matters is, that
C_{2}
is a continuous function of
\mathcal{C}_{\log}
and
C_{1}
.

Finally, for
\lambda>0
arbitrary but fixed, set

\displaystyle c_{1}

\displaystyle:=c_{1}(\lambda):=\sqrt{8(\lambda+1)},

(8.6)

\displaystyle C_{3}

\displaystyle:=C_{3}(W,h,\lambda,t):=\max\{48,128\mathcal{C}_{\log}^{2},64\sqrt{3}C_{1}^{2},\left(32\gamma+128\right)^{4},\sqrt{5}c_{1}\}.

(8.7)

8.2
Summary of Results

Theorem 8.2
.

Let
-2t^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq y\leq x\leq-e
,
y_{0}\leq 0
and
t\geq 64
. We have that

\displaystyle\frac{p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2})}{p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x)}\in\left[1,C_{2}^{-1}|y|^{2\gamma}\right].

(8.8)

Theorem 8.3
.

For
t>0
,
y\in[-2\log(t)^{2},-e]
,
x\in[y,0]
,
y_{0}\leq 0
, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y,y^{2})}{p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2})}\geq ce^{-2C_{1}}e^{-\mathcal{C}_{\log}\sqrt{\log(y^{2})}}.

Theorem
8.3
can be proved by comparing the density of a Brownian motion at time
y^{2}
when starting at
y
to the same starting at
x
. We point out that we will only apply Theorem
8.3
for positive
h
. In this case, the term
e^{-2C_{1}}
in Theorem
8.3
is not necessary.

Theorem 8.4
.

For
y\in[-2\log(t)^{2},-e]
,
x\in[y,-e]
,
y_{0}\leq 0
and
\lambda>0
there is a
t_{0}(\lambda)>0
such that for
t\geq t_{0}(\lambda)
,

\displaystyle\frac{p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y,y^{2})}{p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}^{-1}4^{\gamma+2}e^{4C_{1}}|y|^{2\gamma+3C_{3}}+Ce^{-\lambda\log(t)}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x)^{-1}.

(8.9)

The proof of Theorem
8.4
takes up most of the section, an overview is given in Section
8.5.1
. We also want to point out that the interval in which the Brownian motion is at time
t
being of length 1 (or even
O(1)
) is not necessary for Theorem
8.4
to hold.

For these theorems to be useful, it is necessary to control the constants defined in Section
8.1
, for the specific choices of
W
and
h
relevant in the first half of the paper, we will do this in Section
11
.

8.3
Proof of Theorem
8.2

We proceed with the upper bound in (
8.8
). We recall Definition
2.1
.
In this section, for
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}<\infty
we set

A_{t_{1},t_{2}}:=\mathcal{B}_{[t_{1},t_{2}],h_{t}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}(B_{\cdot}).

We first condition on the endpoint. For
x,y_{0}\leq 0
, we have that
B_{t}-W_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(x-W_{t},t)
under
\mathbb{P}_{x}
and thus, see (
8.1
),

\displaystyle p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{y_{0}-1}^{y_{0}}g_{x-W_{t},t}(z)\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,t}|B_{t}-W_{t}=z\right]\;\mathrm{d}z.

(8.10)

Under
\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[\;\cdot\;|B_{t}-W_{t}=z\right]
the process
(B_{s})_{s\in[0,t]}
is a Brownian bridge from
x
to
z+W_{t}
. We use that to prove that the marginals of
(B_{s})_{s\in[0,t]}
are associated w.r.t. that measure.

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
(X_{s})_{s\in[0,t]}
be a Brownian bridge from
x
to
y
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
t_{1}<\dots<t_{n}\in[0,t]
and
A,B
be decreasing in
(X_{t_{1}},\dots,X_{t_{n}})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[A\cap B]\geq\mathbb{P}[A]\mathbb{P}[B].

Proof.

Let
(Y_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be a Brownian motion starting at
x
, we have that
(X_{s})_{s\in[0,t]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\left(Y_{t}-\frac{s}{t}(Y_{t}-y)\right)_{s\in[0,t]}
, in particular
(X_{t_{1}},\dots,X_{t_{n}})
are normally distributed and a straightforward calculation yields that for
j\leq k

\displaystyle\Cov[X_{t_{j}},X_{t_{k}}]

\displaystyle=\Cov\left[Y_{t_{j}}-\frac{t_{j}}{t}Y_{t}-\frac{t_{j}}{t}y,Y_{t_{k}}-\frac{t_{k}}{t}Y_{t}-\frac{t_{k}}{t}y\right]=t_{j}\left(\frac{t-t_{k}}{t}\right)\geq 0.

(8.11)

By
[
24
]
, equation (
8.11
) implies that
(X_{t_{1}},\dots,X_{t_{n}})
are associated. Since
\mathbf{1}_{A}
,
\mathbf{1}_{B}
are decreasing in
(X_{t_{1}},\dots,X_{t_{n}})
, this gives that
\Cov[\mathbf{1}_{A},\mathbf{1}_{B}]\geq 0
.
∎

Lemma
8.5
and (
8.10
) imply that for
t\geq 0
,
0\geq x\geq y
and
y_{0}\leq 0
,

p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x)\geq\int_{y_{0}-1}^{y_{0}}g_{x-W_{t},t}(z)\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}}|B_{t}-W_{t}=z\right]\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{y^{2},t}|B_{t}-W_{t}=z\right]\;\mathrm{d}z

(8.12)

We want to pull the factor
\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}}|B_{t}-W_{t}=z\right]
in (
8.12
) out of the integral and for this need to bound it uniformly for
z\in[y_{0}-1,y_{0}]
. This is done in Lemma
8.6
whose proof is postponed. The main idea is, that conditioning on
B_{t}-W_{t}=z
does barely change the distribution of
(B_{s})_{s\leq y^{2}}
for
y
and
z
small enough.

Lemma 8.6
.

For
t\geq 64
,
0\geq x\geq y
,
-2t^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq y\leq-e
,
z\leq 0
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}}|B_{t}-W_{t}=z\right]

\displaystyle\geq C_{2}\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}},B_{y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}\in[4C_{1}y,2C_{1}y]\right].

The bound
t\geq 64
is of no further significance and is just a more concrete way to say, that we need
t
big enough, but importantly not depending on
W
.

Equation (
8.12
) and Lemma
8.6
directly imply the following corollary.

Corollary 8.7
.

For
t\geq 64
,
0\geq x\geq y\geq-2t^{\frac{1}{4}}
,
y_{0}\leq 0
, we have that

p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x)\geq C_{2}\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}},B_{y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}\in[4C_{1}y,2C_{1}y]\right]p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2}).

We can control
\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}},B_{y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}\in[4C_{1}y,2C_{1}y]\right]
for
y\leq x\leq-1
by applying (
8.4
). This is done in the next lemma.

Lemma 8.8
.

We have that for
-2t^{\frac{1}{4}}\leq y\leq x\leq-1
and
t\geq 64
,

\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}},B_{y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}\in[4C_{1}y,2C_{1}y]\right]\geq|y|^{-2\gamma}.

Proof.

We have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}},B_{y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}\in[4C_{1}y,2C_{1}y]\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle y\leq x\leq-1}}{{\geq}}\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[\forall_{s\leq y^{2}}\,B_{s}+h_{t}(s)-(1+x)\leq W_{s},B_{y^{2}}-(1+x)-W_{y^{2}}\in[(4C_{1}-1)y,2C_{1}y]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{-1}\left[A_{0,y^{2}},B_{y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}\in[(4C_{1}-1)y,2C_{1}y]\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Ass:QuenchedWall}}}{{\geq}}|y|^{-2\gamma},

where we note that the application of (
8.4
) uses that
y^{2}\leq 4t^{1/2}\leq t/2
for
t\geq 64
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
8.2
assuming Lemma
8.6
.

The lower bound in (
8.8
) is immediate by monotonicity.

Combining Corollary
8.7
and Lemma
8.8
yields the upper bound in (
8.8
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.6
.

First, we note that the event
A_{0,y^{2}}
is decreasing in
B_{\cdot}
. In particular, it is monotonous in the starting and endpoint of a Brownian bridge, such that for all
z\leq 0
we have

\mathbb{P}_{x}[A_{0,y^{2}}|B_{t}-W_{t}=z]\geq\mathbb{P}_{x}[A_{0,y^{2}}|B_{t}-W_{t}=0].

(8.13)

Next, we define

\displaystyle\mu_{1}

\displaystyle=x+y^{2}/t(W_{t}-x)-W_{y^{2}},

\displaystyle\quad\mu_{2}

\displaystyle=x-W_{y^{2}},

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle=y^{2}\frac{t-y^{2}}{t},

\displaystyle\quad\sigma_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=y^{2},

and recall (
8.1
). The relevance of these is that the random variable
B_{y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}
has the density function
g_{\mu_{2},\sigma_{2}^{2}}
under
\mathbb{P}_{x}
and the density function
g_{\mu_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2}}
under
\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[\;\cdot\;|B_{t}-W_{t}=0\right]
. The latter implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}}|B_{t}-W_{t}=0\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{-h_{t}(y^{2})}g_{\mu_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2}}(r)\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}}|B_{t}-W_{t}=0,B_{y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}=r\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{-h_{t}(y^{2})}g_{\mu_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2}}(r)\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}}|B_{y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}=r\right],

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Ass:WachsH}}}{{\geq}}\int_{4C_{1}y}^{2C_{1}y}\frac{g_{\mu_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2}}(r)}{g_{\mu_{2},\sigma_{2}^{2}}(r)}g_{\mu_{2},\sigma_{2}^{2}}(r)\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}}|B_{y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}=r\right]\mathrm{d}r.

(8.14)

We have that

\displaystyle\frac{g_{\mu_{1},\sigma_{1}^{2}}(r)}{g_{\mu_{2},\sigma_{2}^{2}}(r)}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{t}{t-y^{2}}}e^{\frac{(r-\mu_{2})^{2}}{\sigma_{2}^{2}}-\frac{(r-\mu_{1})^{2}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}}\geq e^{\frac{(r-\mu_{2})^{2}}{\sigma_{2}^{2}}-\frac{(r-\mu_{1})^{2}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}}.

(8.15)

Direct calculation using that
-2t^{1/4}\leq y\leq x\leq-e
and (
8.2
) yields that for
r\in[4C_{1}y,2C_{1}y]

\displaystyle e^{\frac{(r-\mu_{2})^{2}}{\sigma_{2}^{2}}-\frac{(r-\mu_{1})^{2}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\geq C_{2}

(8.16)

Plugging (
8.15
) and the inequality (
8.16
) into (
8.14
) yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}}|B_{t}-W_{t}=0\right]

\displaystyle\geq C_{2}\int_{4C_{1}y}^{2C_{1}y}g_{\mu_{2},\sigma_{2}^{2}}(r)\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}}|B_{y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}=r\right]\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=C_{2}\mathbb{P}_{x}\left[A_{0,y^{2}},B_{y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}\in[4C_{1}y,2C_{1}y]\right],

(8.17)

where the last step uses that under
\mathbb{P}_{x}
the random variable
B_{y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}
has the density
g_{\mu_{2},\sigma_{2}^{2}}(r)
. Combining (
8.13
) with (
8.17
) yields the claim of the lemma.
∎

8.4
Proof of Theorem
8.3

For
z\leq 0
and
r\in\mathbb{R}
, define

\displaystyle\mu_{z,y^{2},h}

\displaystyle:=z+h_{t}(y^{2})-W_{y^{2}},

\displaystyle\Delta_{y^{2},W,h}(s)

\displaystyle:=h_{t}(s+y^{2})-h_{t}(y^{2})-(W_{s+y^{2}}-W_{y^{2}}),

\displaystyle p_{t;y^{2},h}^{(y_{0})}(r)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{r}\Big[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t-y^{2}],\Delta_{y^{2},W,h}}^{J_{y_{0}}}\Big].

We recall (
8.1
). Using the Markov property we have that

p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(z,y^{2})=\int_{-\infty}^{0}g_{\mu_{z,y^{2},h},y^{2}}(r)p_{t;y^{2},h}^{(y_{0})}(r)\;\mathrm{d}r.

(8.18)

We finish the proof by taking
z=y
in (
8.18
) and bounding
g_{\mu_{y,y^{2},h},y^{2}}(r)g_{\mu_{x,y^{2},h},y^{2}}(r)^{-1}
. A direct computation yields that

g_{\mu_{y,y^{2},h},y^{2}}(r)g_{\mu_{x,y^{2},h},y^{2}}(r)^{-1}=\exp\left(\frac{r(y-x)}{y^{2}}+\frac{x^{2}-y^{2}}{2y^{2}}+\frac{(W_{y^{2}}-h_{t}(y^{2}))(y-x)}{y^{2}}\right).

(8.19)

Applying (
8.18
) for
z=y
yields that

\displaystyle p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y,y^{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{0}g_{\mu_{y,y^{2},h},y^{2}}(r)p_{t;y^{2},h}^{(y_{0})}(r)\;\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: LBQuotDens}}}{{=}}e^{\frac{x^{2}-y^{2}}{2y^{2}}+\frac{(y-x)W_{y^{2}}}{y^{2}}-\frac{h_{t}(y^{2})(y-x)}{y^{2}}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{\frac{r(y-x)}{y^{2}}}g_{\mu_{y,y^{2},h},y^{2}}(r)p_{t;y^{2},h}^{(y_{0})}(r)\;\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle y-x\leq 0}}{{\geq}}e^{\frac{x^{2}-y^{2}}{2y^{2}}+\frac{(y-x)W_{y^{2}}}{y^{2}}-\frac{h_{t}(y^{2})(y-x)}{y^{2}}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}g_{\mu_{y,y^{2},h},y^{2}}(r)p_{t;y^{2},h}^{(y_{0})}(r)\;\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: SeedRec}}}{{=}}e^{\frac{x^{2}-y^{2}}{2y^{2}}+\frac{(y-x)W_{y^{2}}}{y^{2}}-\frac{h_{t}(y^{2})(y-x)}{y^{2}}}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle|y|\geq e,\eqref{Ass:WBMlog},\eqref{Ass:WachsH}}}{{\geq}}ce^{-2C_{1}}e^{-\mathcal{C}_{\log}\sqrt{\log(y^{2})}}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2}).\qed

8.5
Proof of Theorem
8.4

8.5.1
Definitions and Outline

As in Section
8.4
we will use (
8.18
), but the factor
e^{\frac{r(1+y)}{y^{2}}}
is not a-priori controllable. To fix this we will split the domain of integration into
[-L,0]
,
[-c_{1}(t),-L]
,
(-\infty,-c_{1}(t)]
. On the first and the last interval, we will be able to control the integrand. On the middle region, we will use an analogue to (
8.18
), which is stated in (
8.23
). This will give us a double integral over
[-c_{1}(t),-L]\times(-\infty,0]
, we again split up the domain of integration into three parts and iterate the process. The
L
in the
l
-th iteration will depend on the variable of integration of the
(l-1)
-th iteration in a way such that staying in the middle region for the long time is unlikely.

Definition 8.9
.

For
t\geq 0
,
u,v\in[0,t]
, set
\Delta_{u,v}(W):=W_{u}-W_{v}
,
\Delta_{u,v}(h_{t}):=h_{t}(u)-h_{t}(v)
and
\Delta_{u,v}:=\Delta_{u,v}(h_{t})-\Delta_{u,v}(W)
.

For
\sigma,z,z^{\prime}\geq 0
with
\sigma\leq t
,
\sigma+z\leq t
and
r\in\mathbb{R}
, define

\displaystyle p_{t;\sigma,h}^{(y_{0})}(r)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{r}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t-\sigma],\Delta_{\cdot+\sigma,\sigma}}^{J_{y_{0}}}\right],

(8.20)

\displaystyle p_{t;\sigma,h}^{(y_{0})}(r,z)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{r}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[z,t-\sigma],\Delta_{\cdot+\sigma,\sigma}}^{J_{y_{0}}}\right],

(8.21)

\displaystyle\mu({z,\sigma,z^{\prime},h})

\displaystyle:=z+\Delta_{\sigma+z^{\prime},\sigma}.

(8.22)

If
\sigma=0
it is suppressed from notation.

Figure 3:
The events in Definition
8.9
. The event in (
8.20
) corresponds to a Brownian motion starting at time
\sigma
and location
r
staying below the black line from time
\sigma
to time
t
and ending up in the gray interval, the thin blue curve is a sample in this event. The event in (
8.21
) is the same, but ignoring the black barrier until time
\sigma+z
, the thick red curve serves as a sample.

We recall (
8.1
). A direct consequence of the Markov property for Brownian motion is that for
z\in\mathbb{R}
and
z^{\prime},\sigma\geq 0
with
\sigma+z^{\prime}\leq t
we have that

p_{t;\sigma,h}^{(y_{0})}(z,z^{\prime})=\int_{-\infty}^{0}g_{\mu(z,\sigma,z^{\prime},h),z^{\prime}}(r)p_{t;\sigma+z^{\prime},h}^{(y_{0})}(r)\;\mathrm{d}r.

(8.23)

Definition 8.10
.

Fix
\lambda>0
and
k=\left\lceil(2\lambda\log(t))^{1/3}+1\right\rceil
. Set
z_{0}:=y
. For
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
z_{1},\dots,z_{l}\in\mathbb{R}
set
\sigma_{l}(z_{0},\dots,z_{l}):=\sum_{j=0}^{l}z_{j}^{2}
and for
j\leq l
set
L_{j}(z_{j-1}):=C_{3}z_{j-1}\log(|z_{j-1}|)
.

Introduce the shorthand
\mu_{j}:=\mu(z_{j},\sigma_{j-1},z_{j}^{2},h)
, using the notation introduced in (
8.22
).

The dependence of
L_{j}
and
\sigma_{j}
on
z_{0},\dots,z_{j}
will be omitted in the following. Let
L^{(l)}(y):=L_{l}(L^{(l-1)}(y))
,
L^{(0)}(y)=y
. Finally, set
c_{1}(t):=c_{1}t^{1/5}(\log(t))^{1/2}
.

We claim that under the assumption that
kc_{1}(t)\leq t
, which will be proved to hold for big enough
t
in Lemma
8.12
,

\displaystyle p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y,y^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=2}^{k}\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{1}}\dots\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l-1}}\int_{L_{l}}^{0}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\left(g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\right)p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}

(8.24)

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=2}^{k-1}\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{1}}\dots\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l-1}}\int^{L_{l}\wedge(-c_{1}(t))}_{-\infty}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\left(g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\right)p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}

(8.25)

\displaystyle+\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{1}}\dots\int^{L_{k-1}}_{-c_{1}(t)}\int_{-\infty}^{L_{k}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\left(g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\right)\;\mathrm{d}z_{k}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}

(8.26)

\displaystyle+\int_{L_{1}}^{0}g_{\mu(y,0,y^{2},h),y^{2}}(z_{1})p_{t;y^{2},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{1})\;\mathrm{d}z_{1}

(8.27)

\displaystyle+\int_{-\infty}^{L_{1}\wedge(-c_{1}(t))}g_{\mu(y,0,y^{2},h),y^{2}}(z_{1})p_{t;y^{2},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{1})\;\mathrm{d}z_{1}.

(8.28)

where all integrals for which the lower limit is bigger than the upper limit are defined to be 0. The previous display follows by repeatedly applying (
8.23
) and noticing that
p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\leq p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l},z_{l}^{2})
and that the condition
kc_{1}(t)\leq t
guarantees that
\sigma_{l-1}+z_{l}^{2}\leq t
on the region of integration considered. Example trajectories for the summands (
8.24
)–(
8.28
) are displayed in Figure
4
.

(a)

The black line is the start of a trajectory in (
8.24
), the combination of the black line until
y^{2}
and the thin green line is the start of a trajectory in (
8.26
) and the combination of the black line until
y^{2}
and the thick blue line is the start of a trajectory in (
8.25
)

(b)

The thick blue line is the start of a trajectory in (
8.27
), the thin red one of a trajectory in (
8.28
).

Figure 4:
Sketching the summands in (
8.24
) to (
8.28
) for
k=2
and
h=W=0
.

Remark 8.11
.

In terms of the iteration described at the beginning of this section the summands in (
8.24
) correspond to the probability to stay in the “middle” region for the first
l-1
steps and then end up in
[L_{l},0]
.

The summands in (
8.25
) correspond to the probability to stay in the middle region for the first
l-1
steps and then end up very low in the
l
-th step, this can be controlled, since in the
l
-th step a big jump is made.

The term in (
8.26
) corresponds to the probability that a particle stays in the middle region for so long, that even though it never made a big jump, the particle has ended up very low. Here in every step, we pick up a factor and will need to prove, that the product of these factors is small. For this, the choice of
k
, i.e.
k
being large, will be relevant.

The term in (
8.27
) corresponds to landing in
[0,L_{1}]
in the first step. Here we can directly control the integrand without needing to iterate.

The term in (
8.28
) corresponds to making a big jump in the first step, which has low probability.

We proceed by proving properties of the
k
we have chosen.

Lemma 8.12
.

For
k=\left\lceil(2\lambda\log(t))^{1/3}+1\right\rceil
and
t\geq(3c_{1}\lambda)^{\frac{10}{3}}
, we have that

\frac{1}{8}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\frac{L^{(l)}(y)^{2}}{L^{(l-1)}(y)^{2}}\geq 2\lambda\log(t).

(8.29)

as well as
kc_{1}(t)<t
.

Proof.

It is immediate from the definitions that
L^{(l)}\geq C_{3}^{l}\cdot|y|
, where we use that
\log(|y|)\geq 1
. Thus we have that

\displaystyle\frac{L^{(l)}(y)^{2}}{L^{(l-1)}(y)^{2}}

\displaystyle=C_{3}^{2}\log(|L_{l-1}(y)|)^{2}\geq C_{3}^{2}(l-1)^{2}\log(C_{3})\geq C_{3}(l-1)^{2}

where in the last step we used that
C_{3}\geq e
. Thus

\frac{1}{8}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\frac{L^{(l)}(y)^{2}}{L^{(l-1)}(y)^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{8}C_{3}\sum_{l=1}^{k-1}l^{2}\geq(k-1)^{3}\geq 2\lambda\log(t)

by the choice of
k
. Furthermore, we have that

kc_{1}(t)\leq(3\lambda\log(t))^{1/3}c_{1}(t)\leq t,

since we assume that
t\geq(3c_{1}\lambda)^{\frac{10}{3}}
.
∎

In the following, we will consider the summands (
8.24
)–(
8.28
) in the decomposition one by one and provide bounds for them.

8.5.2
Bounds for (
8.24
) to (
8.28
), proof of theorem
8.4
assuming these bounds

Lemma 8.13
.

For
t\geq(2\sqrt{\lambda+1})^{40}
,
y\in[-2\log(t)^{2},-e]
and
y_{0}\leq 0
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=2}^{k}\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{1}}\dots\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l-1}}\int_{L_{l}}^{0}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\left(g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\right)p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}^{-1}4^{\gamma+2}y^{2\gamma}\inf_{x\in[y,-e]}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2}).

The main step in the proof of Lemma
8.13
will be to bound
\int_{L_{l}}^{0}g_{\mu_{l-1},z_{l-1}^{2}}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}
by replacing
g_{\mu_{l-1},z_{l-1}^{2}}(z_{l})
by
g_{\mu(x,0,\sigma_{l-1},h),\sigma_{l-1}}(z_{l})
, which can be done since we have
z_{l}\in[L_{l},0]
.

Lemma 8.14
.

For
t>0
,
y\in[-2\log(t)^{2},-e]
,
y_{0}\leq 0
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{l=2}^{k-1}\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l}}\dots\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l-1}}\int^{L_{l}\wedge(-c_{1}(t))}_{-\infty}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\left(g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\right)p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}\leq 8ke^{-\frac{c_{1}^{2}\log(t)}{8}}.

For proving Lemma
8.14
it suffices to bound
p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})
by 1 and to use, that
|z_{l}|
is big on the region of integration of the last integral, in particular the bound only involves the last two integrals, for the other integrals all we use is that the
g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}
are density functions.

Lemma 8.15
.

For
t\geq(3c_{1}\lambda)^{\frac{10}{3}}
,
y\in[-2\log(t)^{2},-e]
,
y_{0}\leq 0
, we have that

\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{1}}\dots\int^{L_{k-1}}_{-c_{1}(t)}\int_{-\infty}^{L_{k}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\left(g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\right)\;\mathrm{d}z_{k}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}\leq e^{-2\lambda\log(t)}.

To prove Lemma
8.15
we can again use
p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\leq 1
, but here the last two integrals alone aren’t enough. Instead we prove by induction that in the
l
-th integral we pick up a factor
e^{-\frac{1}{8}\frac{L^{(l)}(y)^{2}}{L^{(l-1)}(y)^{2}}}
, (
8.29
) then allows us to conclude.

Lemma 8.16
.

For
t>0
,
y\in[-2\log(t)^{2},-e]
,
x\in[y,-e]
,
y_{0}\leq 0
, we have that

\int_{L_{1}}^{0}g_{\mu(y,0,y^{2},h),y^{2}}(z_{1})p_{t;y^{2},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{1})\;\mathrm{d}z_{1}\leq Ce^{4C_{1}}e^{3C_{3}\log(|y|)}\inf_{x\in[y,-e]}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2}).

The proof of Lemma
8.16
closely mirrors the proof of Theorem
8.3
.

Lemma 8.17
.

There is a
t_{0}(\lambda)>0
such that for
t\geq t_{0}(\lambda)
,
y\in[-2\log(t)^{2},-e]
,
y_{0}\leq 0
,

\int_{-\infty}^{L_{1}\wedge(-c_{1}(t))}g_{\mu(y,0,y^{2},h),y^{2}}(z_{1})p_{t;y^{2},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{1})\;\mathrm{d}z_{1}\leq 8e^{-\frac{c_{1}^{2}t^{2/5}\log(t)}{8y^{2}}}.

Lemma
8.17
is quickly proved by applying Gaussian tail estimates.

Proof of Theorem
8.4
assuming Lemma
8.13
to Lemma
8.17
.

By using Lemmata
8.13
–
8.17
and (
8.24
)–(
8.28
) and
y\in[-2\log(t)^{2},-e]
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y,y^{2})}{p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}^{-1}4^{\gamma+2}e^{4C_{1}}|y|^{2\gamma+3C_{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad+Cp_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2})^{-1}\cdot\left(ke^{-\frac{c_{1}^{2}\log(t)}{8}}+e^{-\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{32}t^{2/5}\log(t)^{1-4}}+e^{-2\lambda\log(t)}\right).

(8.30)

We recall our choice
c_{1}=\sqrt{8(\lambda+1)}
in (
8.6
). Furthermore, we note that by definition
k\leq t
for
t\geq((2\lambda)^{1/3}+2)^{3/2}
, that for
t
big enough
\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{4}t^{2/5}\log(t)^{1-4}\geq\lambda\log(t)
and that by (
8.8
)
p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2})^{-1}\leq p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x)^{-1}
. Combining these observations with (
8.30
) finishes the proof.
∎

8.5.3
Details

Preparation

We recall (
8.1
) and Definition
8.9
. The aim of this paragraph is to prove the following lemma, which allows replacing
g_{\mu_{l-1},z_{l-1}^{2}}(z_{l})
by
2g_{0,4z_{l-1}^{2}}(z_{l})
.

Lemma 8.18
.

Fix
l\in\mathbb{N}
. Assume that for all
r\leq l-1
we have that
z_{r}\leq C_{3}z_{r-1}\log(|z_{r-1}|)
. Then we have that for all
r\leq l-1
,

\displaystyle g_{\mu_{r},z_{r}^{2}}(z_{r+1})

\displaystyle\leq 2g_{0,4z_{r}^{2}}(z_{r+1}).

To prove Lemma
8.18
we will compare
\sigma_{r}
to
z_{r}^{2}
,
z_{r+1}^{2}
and establish bounds on
\Delta_{\sigma_{i-1},\sigma_{i-2}}(f)
,
f\in\{W,h_{t}\}
, these are stated in the next three lemmata, whose proofs are postponed since they are mostly calculation.

Lemma 8.19
.

Fix
l\in\mathbb{N}
. Assume that for all
r\leq l-1
we have that
z_{r}\leq C_{3}z_{r-1}\log(|z_{r-1}|)
. Then we have that for all
r\leq l-1

\displaystyle\sigma_{r}

\displaystyle\leq 2z_{r}^{2},

(8.31)

\displaystyle\sigma_{r-1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{z_{r}^{2}}{\log(|z_{r-1}|)^{2}}.

(8.32)

Lemma 8.20
.

Fix
l\in\mathbb{N}
. Assume that for all
r\leq l-1
we have that
z_{r}\leq C_{3}z_{r-1}\log(|z_{r-1}|)
. Then we have that for all
r\leq l-1

\displaystyle|\Delta_{\sigma_{r-1},\sigma_{r-2}}(W)|\leq\frac{1}{8}|z_{r}-z_{r-1}|

Lemma 8.21
.

Fix
l\in\mathbb{N}
. Assume that for all
r\leq l-1
we have that
z_{r}\leq C_{3}z_{r-1}\log(|z_{r-1}|)
. Then we have that for all
r\leq l-1

|\Delta_{\sigma_{r-1},\sigma_{r-2}}(h_{t})|\leq\frac{1}{8}|z_{r}-z_{r-1}|.

Proof of Lemma
8.18
assuming Lemmata
8.20
,
8.21
.

Follows by plugging Lemma
8.20
, Lemma
8.21
into the definition of
g_{\mu_{r-1},z_{r-1}^{2}}(z_{r})
and noticing that
z_{r}\leq 4z_{r-1}
by (
8.7
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.19
.

Equation (
8.32
) is implied by (
8.31
) using that
z_{r+1}^{2}\geq\log(|z_{r}|)^{2}z_{r}^{2}
. Thus it remains to prove (
8.31
). We have that
z_{r}\leq C_{3}z_{r-1}\log(|z_{r-1}|)
and since
z_{r-1}\leq y\leq-e
we have that
\log(|z_{r-1}|)\geq 1
and thus

z_{r}\leq C_{3}z_{r-1}<0.

Squaring and iterating this bound gives that for
k\in\{0,\dots,r\}
we have
z_{r-k}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{C_{3}^{2k}}z_{r}^{2}
which yields that

\sigma_{r}=\sum_{k=0}^{r}z_{k}^{2}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{r}\frac{1}{C_{3}^{2k}}z_{r}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-1/C_{3}^{2}}z_{r}^{2}\leq 2z_{r}^{2}

where the second to last step uses that
C_{3}^{2}>1
and the last step uses that
1-1/C_{3}^{2}\geq 1/2
since
C_{3}^{2}\geq 2
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.20
.

Using the triangle inequality and
|z_{i-1}|\leq\frac{1}{C_{3}}|z_{i}|
shows that it suffices to prove that for
k\in\{1,2\}

|W_{\sigma_{i-k}}|\leq\frac{C_{3}-1}{16C_{3}}|z_{i}|.

By (
8.7
) we have that
(C_{3}-1)/C_{3}\geq\frac{1}{2}
and thus it suffices to prove that

\frac{|W_{\sigma_{i-k}}|}{|z_{i}|}\leq\frac{1}{32}

(8.33)

for
k\in\{1,2\}
. We note that for
i=1
and
k=2
(
8.33
) is trivial, since
W_{\sigma_{-1}}=W_{0}=0
. For the other pairs
i,k
by Lemma
8.19
we have that

0<\sigma_{i-k}\leq 2z_{i-1}^{2},

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{|W_{\sigma_{i-k}}|}{|z_{i}|}=\frac{|W_{\sigma_{i-k}}|}{\sigma_{i-k}^{1/2}\log(\sigma_{i-k})}\frac{\sigma_{i-k}^{1/2}\log(\sigma_{i-k})}{|z_{i}|}\leq\mathcal{C}_{\log}\frac{2|z_{i-1}|\log(2z_{i-1}^{2})}{C_{3}|z_{i-1}|\log(|z_{i-1}|)}\leq\frac{1}{32},

where the last step uses (
8.7
). This yields (
8.33
) and finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.21
.

As in Lemma
8.20
it suffices to prove that

\frac{|h_{t}(\sigma_{i-k})|}{|z_{i}|}\leq\frac{1}{32}

for
k\in\{1,2\}
.
By (
8.3
) we have that for
k\in\{1,2\}

\displaystyle|h_{t}(\sigma_{i-k})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}|\sqrt{1+\sigma_{i-k}}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L.\ref{Lem: IneqSig}}}{{\leq}}C_{1}\sqrt{1+2z_{i-k}^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle|z_{i-k}|\geq 1}}{{\leq}}C_{1}\sqrt{3}|z_{i-k}|\leq 2C_{1}\frac{\sqrt{3}}{C_{3}}|z_{i}|,

where the last step uses our assumption that
z_{3}\leq C_{3}z_{r-1}\log(|z_{r-1}|)\leq 0
and the fact that
\log(|z_{r-1}|)\geq 1
, since
z_{r-1}\leq y\leq-e
.
Using (
8.7
) this yields the claim.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.13

The proof of this lemma is split into two steps. First, we bound the last two integrals in (
8.24
). For this, we control
g_{\mu_{l-1},z_{l-1}^{2}}(z_{l})g_{\mu(x,0,\sigma_{l-1},h),\sigma_{l-1}}(z_{l})^{-1}
for
z_{l}\in[L_{l},0]
, giving the following lemma.

Lemma 8.22
.

Let
t\geq(2\sqrt{\lambda+1})^{40}
,
y\in[-2\log(t)^{2},-e]
,
y_{0}\leq 0
and
l\geq 2
. Assume that for all
r\leq l-1
we have that
z_{r}\leq C_{3}z_{r-1}\log(|z_{r-1}|)
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l-1}}g_{\mu_{l-2},z_{l-2}^{2}}(z_{l-1})\int_{L_{l}}^{0}g_{\mu_{l-1},z_{l-1}^{2}}(z_{l})p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\mathrm{d}z_{l-1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}^{-1}4^{\gamma+2}|z_{l-2}|^{2\gamma}e^{-\frac{C_{3}^{2}}{16}}\inf_{x\in[y,-e]}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2})

We note that the condition
t\geq(2\sqrt{\lambda+1})^{40}
is not optimal, and should be thought of as
t
big enough in a way, which does not depend on
W
.

After this, we bound the other integrals in (
8.24
) by induction giving the following lemma.

Lemma 8.23
.

For
y\leq\xi_{0}
arbitrary and all
l\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that

\int_{-\infty}^{L_{1}}\dots\int_{-\infty}^{L_{l}}z_{l}^{2\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{l}g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}\leq\sqrt{8}e^{-\frac{lC_{3}^{2}}{16}}|y|^{2\gamma}.

Proof of Lemma
8.13
assuming Lemmata
8.22
and
8.23
.

Combining Lemma
8.22
and Lemma
8.23
gives that

\displaystyle\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{1}}\dots\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l-1}}\int_{L_{l}}^{0}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\left(g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\right)p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}^{-1}4^{\gamma+2}|y|^{2\gamma}e^{-\frac{(l-1)C_{3}^{2}}{16}}\inf_{x\in[y,-e]}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2}).

Summing this inequality over
l\geq 2
directly yields the claim of Lemma
8.13
since by (
8.7
) we have
1/\left(1-e^{-\frac{C_{3}^{2}}{16}}\right)\leq 2
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.22

Lemma 8.24
.

We have that for
y\in[-2\log(t)^{2},-e]
,
x\in[y,-e]
,
l\geq 2
,
z_{l}\in[L_{l},0]
,
z_{l-1}\leq L_{l-1}
,

\displaystyle\frac{g_{\mu_{l-1},z_{l-1}^{2}}(z_{l})}{g_{\mu(x,0,\sigma_{l-1},h),\sigma_{l-1}}(z_{l})}\leq\sqrt{2}e^{C_{3}^{3/2}\left(\log(|z_{l-2}|)\right)^{3/2}}.

Proof.

Using the definition of
g
in (
8.1
), dropping negative terms, using that
z_{l}\geq L_{l}
and simplifying gives the bound

\displaystyle g_{\mu_{l-1},z_{l-1}^{2}}(z_{l})g_{\mu(x,0,\sigma_{l-1},h),\sigma_{l-1}}(z_{l})^{-1}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\leq\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{l-1}}}{|z_{l-1}|}e^{\frac{L_{l}}{z_{l-1}}+\frac{L_{l}}{z_{l-1}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{l-2}(|W_{\sigma_{l-1}}|+|h_{t}(\sigma_{l-1})|)}{\sigma_{l-1}|z_{l-1}|}+\frac{|W_{\sigma_{l-2}}|+|h_{t}(\sigma_{l-2})|}{|z_{l-1}|}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\qquad\cdot e^{\frac{x^{2}+h_{t}(\sigma_{l-1})^{2}+W_{\sigma_{l-1}}^{2}+2|x|\cdot|h_{t}(\sigma_{l-1})|+2|x|\cdot|W_{\sigma_{l-1}}|+2|h_{t}(\sigma_{l-1})|\cdot|W_{\sigma_{l-1}}|}{2\sigma_{l-1}}}.

(8.34)

This upper bound can be simplified further by plugging in the definition of
L_{l}
and bounding the terms involving
W
and
h
. Using Assumptions (
8.3
), (
8.2
) and Lemma
8.19
direct calculation gives that

\displaystyle\frac{(|W_{\sigma_{l-1}}|+|h_{t}(\sigma_{l-1})|)\cdot\sigma_{l-2}}{\sigma_{l-1}z_{l-1}}

\displaystyle\leq 8\mathcal{C}_{\log}\left(\log(|z_{l-1}|)\right)^{1/2}+8C_{1},

\displaystyle\frac{|W_{\sigma_{l-2}}|+|h_{t}(\sigma_{l-2})|}{z_{l-1}}

\displaystyle\leq 8\mathcal{C}_{\log}+8C_{1}

\displaystyle\frac{x^{2}+W_{\sigma_{l-1}}^{2}+h_{t}(\sigma_{l-1})^{2}}{\sigma_{l-1}}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\mathcal{C}_{\log}^{2}\log(2z_{l-1}^{2})+C_{1}^{2},

\displaystyle\frac{\left|xW_{\sigma_{l-1}}\right|+\left|xh_{t}(\sigma_{l-1})\right|}{\sigma_{l-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\mathcal{C}_{\log}+\sqrt{2}C_{1},

\displaystyle\frac{|h_{t}(\sigma_{l-1})|\cdot|W_{\sigma_{l-1}}|}{\sigma_{l-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\mathcal{C}_{\log}\left(\log(2z_{l-1}^{2}))\right)^{1/2}.

Plugging these and the definition of
L_{l}
in Definition
8.10
into (
8.34
) and simplifying yields the claim of the lemma, we note that while simplifying we also used Assumption (
8.7
) as well as that
\sqrt{\sigma_{l-1}}/|z_{l-1}|\leq\sqrt{2}
by Lemma
8.19
.
∎

Lemma
8.24
and (
8.8
) allow us to bound the last integral in (
8.24
).

Lemma 8.25
.

For
t\geq(2\sqrt{\lambda+1})^{40}
,
y\in[-2\log(t)^{2},-e]
,
y_{0}\leq 0
,
l\geq 2
and
z_{j}\in[-c_{1}(t),0]
we have that for all
j\leq l-1
,

\int_{L_{l}}^{0}g_{\mu_{l-1},z_{l-1}^{2}}(z_{l})p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\leq C_{2}^{-1}\sqrt{2}2^{2\gamma}e^{C_{3}^{3/2}\left(\log(|z_{l-1}|)\right)^{3/2}}|z_{l-1}|^{2\gamma}\inf_{x\in[y,-e]}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2}).

Proof.

Fix
x\in[y,-e]
arbitrary. We have that

\displaystyle\int_{L_{l}}^{0}g_{\mu_{l-1},z_{l-1}^{2}}(z_{l})p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L.\ref{Lem: CrudeDensQuot}}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{2}e^{C_{3}^{3/2}\left(\log(|z_{l-1}|)\right)^{3/2}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}g_{\mu(x,0,\sigma_{l-1},h),\sigma_{l-1}}(z_{l})p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: Iter}}}{{=}}\sqrt{2}e^{C_{3}^{3/2}\left(\log(|z_{l-1}|)\right)^{3/2}}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,\sigma_{l-1}).

(8.35)

Since
z_{j}\in[-c_{1}(t),0]
for all
j\leq l-1
we have that

\sqrt{\sigma_{l-1}}\leq\sqrt{2}|z_{l-1}|\leq\sqrt{2}c_{1}(t)\leq 2t^{1/4},

where the last inequality uses that
t\geq(2\sqrt{\lambda+1})^{40}
and
c_{1}(t)=\sqrt{8(\lambda+1)}t^{1/5}(\log(t))^{1/2}
. Thus, we can apply (
8.8
) to conclude

\displaystyle p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,\sigma_{l-1}

\displaystyle)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: OS1}}}{{\leq}}C_{2}^{-1}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x)\sigma_{l-1}^{\gamma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L.\ref{Lem: IneqSig}}}{{\leq}}C_{2}^{-1}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2})|z_{l-1}|^{2\gamma}2^{2\gamma}.

(8.36)

Together (
8.35
), (
8.36
) and the fact that
x\in[y,-e]
was arbitrary, finish the proof.
∎

A direct calculation gives that for
s\geq C_{3}\log(|z_{l-2}|)

s^{2\gamma}e^{C_{3}^{3/2}\log(s|z_{l-2}|)^{3/2}}\leq e^{\frac{s^{2}}{16}}.

(8.37)

With this, we have all tools needed for the proof of Lemma
8.22
.

Proof of Lemma
8.22
.

Set

I(z_{l-1}):=\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l-1}}g_{\mu_{l-2},z_{l-2}^{2}}(z_{l-1})\int_{L_{l}}^{0}g_{\mu_{l-1},z_{l-1}^{2}}(z_{l})p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\mathrm{d}z_{l-1}.

Using Lemma
8.25
and Lemma
8.18
yields that

\displaystyle\frac{I(z_{l-1})}{\sqrt{2}2^{2\gamma}C_{2}^{-1}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L.\ref{Lem: BoundLastInt}}}{{\leq}}\int_{-\infty}^{L_{l-1}}g_{\mu_{l-2},z_{l-2}^{2}}(z_{l-1})e^{C_{3}^{3/2}\left(\log(|z_{l-1}|)\right)^{3/2}}|z_{l-1}|^{2\gamma}\;\mathrm{d}z_{l-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L.\ref{Lem:Removemu}}}{{\leq}}\int_{-\infty}^{C_{3}z_{l-2}\log(|z_{l-2}|)}|z_{l-1}|^{2\gamma}2g_{0,4z_{l-2}^{2}}(z_{l-1})e^{C_{3}^{3/2}\left(\log(|z_{l-1}|)\right)^{3/2}}\;\mathrm{d}z_{l-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s=\frac{z_{l-1}}{z_{l-2}},\eqref{eq:Fu}}}{{\leq}}|z_{l-2}|^{2\gamma}\sqrt{8}\int_{C_{3}\log(|z_{l-2}|)}^{\infty}g_{0,8}(s)\;\mathrm{d}s\leq 8|z_{l-2}|^{2\gamma}e^{-\frac{C_{3}^{2}\log(|z_{l-2}|)^{2}}{16}}

\displaystyle\leq 8|z_{l-2}|^{2\gamma}e^{-\frac{C_{3}^{2}}{16}},

where the second to last step used Gaussian tail estimates
4
4

4

See for example Theorem 1.2.6 in
[
5
, p. 13]
.
and the last that by our assumptions
|z_{l-2}|\geq|y|\geq e
. Rearranging yields the claim.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.23

We prove this by induction. Induction basis (
l=1
): By applying Lemma
8.18
and (
8.37
) we get analogously to the calculation in the proof of Lemma
8.22

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{L_{1}}|z_{1}|^{2\gamma}g_{\mu_{0},z_{0}^{2}}(z_{1})\;\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\leq|z_{0}|^{2\gamma}\int_{C_{3}\log(|z_{0}|)}^{\infty}\sqrt{8}g_{0,8}(s)\;\mathrm{d}s\leq\sqrt{8}|z_{0}|^{2\gamma}e^{-\frac{C_{3}^{2}}{16}}.

Induction step (
l\to l+1
): We have that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{L_{1}}\dots\int_{-\infty}^{L_{l+1}}|z_{l+1}|^{2\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{l}g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l+1}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{IH}}}{{\leq}}\int_{-\infty}^{L_{1}}|z_{1}|^{2\gamma}e^{-\frac{lC_{3}^{2}}{16}}g_{\mu_{0},z_{0}^{2}}(z_{1})\;\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{IB}}}{{\leq}}e^{-\frac{lC_{3}^{2}}{16}}|y|^{2\gamma}e^{-\frac{C_{3}^{2}}{16}}=|y|^{2\gamma}e^{-\frac{(l+1)C_{3}^{2}}{16}}.\qed

Proof of Lemma
8.14

Let
l\in\{2,\dots,k-1\}
. To keep notation slightly lighter, we will as in the proof of Lemma
8.13
handle the last two integrals in (
8.25
) first. Since
p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\leq 1
we have that

\displaystyle\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l-1}}\int_{-\infty}^{-c_{1}t^{1/5}\log(t)^{1/2}\wedge L_{l}}\prod_{i=l-1}^{l}\left(g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\right)p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\mathrm{d}z_{l-1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l-1}}\int_{-\infty}^{-c_{1}t^{1/5}\log(t)^{1/2}\wedge L_{l}}\prod_{i=l-1}^{l}\left(g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\right)\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\mathrm{d}z_{l-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L.\ref{Lem:Removemu}}}{{\leq}}\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l-1}}\int_{-\infty}^{-c_{1}t^{1/5}\log(t)^{1/2}\wedge L_{l}}\prod_{i=l-1}^{l}2g_{0,4z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\mathrm{d}z_{l-1}

\displaystyle\leq 4\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l-1}}g_{0,4z_{l-2}^{2}}(z_{l-1})e^{-\frac{(-c_{1}(t)\wedge L_{l})^{2}}{8z_{l-1}^{2}}}\;\mathrm{d}z_{l-1}

(8.38)

On
[-t^{1/5},L_{l-1}]
we have that

e^{-\frac{(-c_{1}(t)\wedge L_{l})^{2}}{8z_{l-1}^{2}}}\leq e^{-\frac{c_{1}^{2}\log(t)}{8}}

and on
[-c_{1}(t),-t^{1/5}]
we have that

\displaystyle e^{-\frac{(-c_{1}(t)\wedge L_{l})^{2}}{8z_{l-1}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{C_{3}^{2}\log(|z_{l-1}|)^{2}}{8}}\leq e^{-\frac{c_{1}^{2}\log(t)}{8}},

where the last step used that by (
8.7
)
C_{3}^{2}\geq 5c_{1}^{2}
.

Plugging both of these into (
8.38
) and using that
g_{\mu_{l-2},z_{l-2}^{2}}
is a density function gives that

\displaystyle\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l-1}}\int_{-\infty}^{-c_{1}t^{1/5}\log(t)^{1/2}\wedge L_{l}}\prod_{i=l-1}^{l}\left(g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\right)p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\mathrm{d}z_{l-1}

\displaystyle\leq 8e^{-\frac{c_{1}^{2}\log(t)}{8}}

(8.39)

Since for
i<l-1
all
g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}
are density functions, (
8.39
) implies that

\displaystyle\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l}}\dots\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{l-1}}\int^{L_{l}\wedge(-c_{1}(t))}_{-\infty}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\left(g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\right)p_{t;\sigma_{l-1},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{l})\;\mathrm{d}z_{l}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 8e^{-\frac{c_{1}^{2}\log(t)}{8}}.

(8.40)

Finally, summing (
8.40
) for
l\in\{2,\dots,k-1\}
yields the statement of Lemma
8.14
. ∎

Proof of Lemma
8.15

One preparatory lemma is needed for this.

Lemma 8.26
.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}
arbitrary,
y\leq\xi_{0}

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{L_{1}}\dots\int_{-\infty}^{L_{m-1}}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}|z_{i-1}|}e^{-\frac{z_{i}^{2}}{8z_{i-1}^{2}}}e^{-\frac{L_{m}^{2}}{8z_{m-1}^{2}}}\;\mathrm{d}z_{m-1}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}\leq e^{-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\frac{L^{(l)}(y)^{2}}{L^{(l-1)}(y)^{2}}}.

Proof.

We prove this using induction. Induction basis (
m=1
): Here no integrals remain and the statement trivially holds by definition of
L^{(1)}(y)
and
L^{(0)}(y)
.

Induction step (
m\to m+1
): We have that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{L_{1}}\dots\int_{-\infty}^{L_{m}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}|z_{i-1}|}e^{-\frac{z_{i}^{2}}{8z_{i-1}^{2}}-\frac{L_{m+1}^{2}}{8z_{m}^{2}}}\;\mathrm{d}z_{m}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{IH}}}{{\leq}}\int_{-\infty}^{L_{1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}|y|}e^{-\frac{z_{1}^{2}}{8y^{2}}}e^{-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{l=1}^{m}\frac{L^{(l)}(z_{1})^{2}}{L^{(l-1)}(z_{1})^{2}}}\;\mathrm{d}z_{1}

since
\frac{L^{(l)}(x)^{2}}{L^{(l-1)}(x)^{2}}=C_{3}^{2}\log(|L^{(l-1)}(x)|)^{2}
is decreasing in
x
for
x\leq-1
and
z_{1}\leq y
, which can be seen directly from the definition in
8.10
, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{L_{1}}\dots\int_{-\infty}^{L_{m}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}|z_{i-1}|}e^{-\frac{z_{i}^{2}}{8z_{i-1}^{2}}-\frac{L_{m+1}^{2}}{8z_{m}^{2}}}\;\mathrm{d}z_{m}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\sum_{l=1}^{m}\frac{L^{(l)}(L_{1}(y))^{2}}{L^{(l-1)}(L_{1}(y))^{2}}}\int_{-\infty}^{L_{1}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}|y|}e^{-\frac{z_{1}^{2}}{8y^{2}}}\;\mathrm{d}z_{1}\leq e^{-\sum_{l=2}^{m+1}\frac{L^{(l)}(y)^{2}}{L^{(l-1)}(y)^{2}}}e^{-\frac{L_{1}(y)^{2}}{8y^{2}}}.\qed

Proof of Lemma
8.15
.

Repeating the calculation in Lemma
8.14
we get that

\displaystyle\int_{-c_{1}(t)}^{L_{1}}\dots\int^{L_{k-1}}_{-c_{1}(t)}\int^{L_{k}}_{-\infty}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\left(g_{\mu_{i-1},z_{i-1}^{2}}(z_{i})\right)\;\mathrm{d}z_{k}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-\infty}^{L_{1}}\dots\int_{-\infty}^{L_{k-1}}\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}|z_{i-1}|}e^{-\frac{z_{i}^{2}}{8z_{i-1}^{2}}}e^{-\frac{L_{k}^{2}}{8z_{k-1}^{2}}}\;\mathrm{d}z_{k-1}\dots\mathrm{d}z_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L.\ref{Lem: LastHilf}}}{{\leq}}e^{-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\frac{L^{(l)}(y)^{2}}{L^{(l-1)}(y)^{2}}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: Bedk}}}{{\leq}}e^{-2\lambda\log(t)}.\qed

Proof of Lemma
8.16

We have that

\displaystyle\int_{L_{1}}^{0}g_{\mu(y,0,y^{2},h),y^{2}}(z_{1})p_{t;y^{2},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{1})\;\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: LBQuotDens}}}{{=}}e^{\frac{x^{2}-y^{2}}{2y^{2}}+\frac{(y-x)W_{y^{2}}}{y^{2}}-\frac{h_{t}(y^{2})(y-x)}{y^{2}}}\int_{L_{1}}^{0}e^{\frac{z_{1}(y-x)}{y^{2}}}g_{\mu(x,0,y^{2},h),y^{2}}(z_{1})p_{t;y^{2},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{1})\;\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\frac{x^{2}-y^{2}}{2y^{2}}+\frac{(y-x)W_{y^{2}}}{y^{2}}-\frac{h_{t}(y^{2})(y-x)}{y^{2}}}e^{\frac{C_{3}y\log(|y|)(y-x)}{y^{2}}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}g_{\mu(x,0,y^{2},h),y^{2}}(z_{1})p_{t;y^{2},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{1})\;\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq: SeedRec},\eqref{Ass:WBMlog},\eqref{Ass:WachsH}}}{{\leq}}Ce^{4C_{1}}e^{2\mathcal{C}_{\log}\sqrt{\log(y^{2})}}e^{2C_{3}\log(|y|)}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Ass:AssC0W}}}{{\leq}}Ce^{4C_{1}}e^{3C_{3}\log(|y|)}p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x,y^{2}).\qed

Proof of Lemma
8.17

By definition
L_{1}\wedge(-c_{1}(t))\leq-c_{1}(t)
and thus

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{L_{1}\wedge(-c_{1}(t))}g_{\mu(y,0,y^{2},h),y^{2}}(z_{1})p_{t;y^{2},h}^{(y_{0})}(z_{1})\;\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L.\ref{Lem: WExp},\ref{Lem: hExp}}}{{\leq}}\int_{-\infty}^{-c_{1}(t)}2g_{0,4y^{2}}(z_{1})\;\mathrm{d}z_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 8e^{-\frac{c_{1}^{2}t^{2/5}\log(t)}{8y^{2}}}.\qed

9
Getting rid of
h_{t}
– Preparation for Section
12

In all of this section we assume that
t\geq 0
,
y\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
,
y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
are fixed.

Furthermore, let
(X_{s})_{s\geq 0}
be the universal object in what follows and assume that under
\mathbb{P}_{y}
it is distributed as a Brownian motion starting at
y
. In this section, we will analyze how adding a shift
h_{t}
to a barrier changes the probability that
X_{s}
stays below the barrier. In Section
9.1
the additional shift will be linear, in Sections
9.3
and
9.4
we add a positive/negative banana. The main tool in this section is the Girsanov theorem, we define measures with regard to which
(X_{s}+h_{t}(s))_{s\in[0,t]}
is a Brownian motion and control the corresponding Radon-Nikodym derivatives.

9.1
Linear Shifts

We recall Definition
2.1
.

Definition 9.1
.

Consider
(c_{t})_{t\geq 0}
in
\mathbb{R}
and set

\displaystyle A_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],h_{t}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot}),

\displaystyle A_{t,h,c}^{(y_{0})}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],h_{t}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{s}+sc_{t}).

In this section, we prove the following theorem.

Theorem 9.2
.

We have that for
t\geq 0
and
y,y_{0}\leq 0
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}_{y}[A_{t,h,c}^{(y_{0})}]}{\mathbb{P}_{y}[A_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}]}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{tc_{t}^{2}}{2}}e^{-yc_{t}-h_{t}(t)c_{t}+|y_{0}-1|\cdot|c_{t}|}e^{W_{t}c_{t}},

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}_{y}[A_{t,h,c}^{(y_{0})}]}{\mathbb{P}_{y}[A_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}]}

\displaystyle\geq e^{-\frac{tc_{t}^{2}}{2}}e^{-yc_{t}-h_{t}(t)c_{t}-|y_{0}-1|\cdot|c_{t}|}e^{W_{t}c_{t}}.

Proof.

Set

\displaystyle Z_{t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}_{y}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{y}}:=\exp\left(-\int_{0}^{t}c_{t}\;\mathrm{d}X_{s}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}c_{t}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\right)=\exp\left(-c_{t}(X_{t}-X_{0})-\frac{tc_{t}^{2}}{2}\right)

The Girsanov Theorem then gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[A_{t,h,c}^{(y_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{y}}\left[(Z_{t})^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{A_{t,h,c}^{(y_{0})}}\right]=e^{\frac{tc_{t}^{2}}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{y}}\left[e^{c_{t}X_{t}-yc_{t}}\mathbf{1}_{A_{t,h,c}^{(y_{0})}}\right].

(9.1)

On
A_{t,h,c}^{(y_{0})}
we have that

W_{t}-tc_{t}+y_{0}-1-h_{t}(t)\leq X_{t}\leq W_{t}-tc_{t}-h_{t}(t)+y_{0}.

(9.2)

Plugging (
9.2
) into (
9.1
) yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[A_{t,h,c}^{(y_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle\leq e^{\frac{tc_{t}^{2}}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{y}}\left[e^{(W_{t}-tc_{t}-h_{t}(t)+y_{0}-\mathbf{1}_{c_{t}<0})c_{t}-yc_{t}}\mathbf{1}_{A_{t,h,c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{tc_{t}^{2}}{2}}e^{-yc_{t}-h_{t}(t)c_{t}+|y_{0}-1|\cdot|c_{t}|}e^{W_{t}c_{t}}\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[A_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y}[A_{t,h,c}^{(y_{0})}]

\displaystyle\geq e^{\frac{tc_{t}^{2}}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{y}}\left[e^{(W_{t}-tc_{t}+y_{0}-\mathbf{1}_{c_{t}>0}-h_{t}(t))c_{t}-yc_{t}}\mathbf{1}_{A_{t,h,c}^{(y_{0})}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq e^{-\frac{tc_{t}^{2}}{2}}e^{-yc_{t}-h_{t}(t)c_{t}-|y_{0}-1|\cdot|c_{t}|}e^{W_{t}c_{t}}\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[A_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}\right].

Rearranging yields the claim.
∎

9.2
General Setup

Fix
t>0
and
h_{t}:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R}
with
h_{t}(t)=0=h_{t}(0)
. Set
\chi(t):=\log(t)^{1.5}
. Define
\widehat{W}
via
\widehat{W}_{s}:=W_{t-s}-W_{t}
,
s\in[0,t]
. We recall Section
8.1
, we use
\widehat{C}
to denote variants of constants using
\widehat{W}
instead of
W
, for example we set
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\log}:=\mathcal{C}_{\log}(\widehat{W},t)
.

From now on we will need two additional assumptions. Namely, we assume that

\log(t)^{-0.5}\max\{\mathcal{C}_{\log},\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\log}\}\leq\frac{1}{3}

(9.3)

and that there are
C_{4}:=C_{4}(W,t)<\infty
,
\widehat{C}_{4}:=C_{4}(\widehat{W},t)<\infty
,
c_{2}:=c_{2}(W,t)>0
,
\widehat{c}_{2}:=c_{2}(\widehat{W},t)>0
such that for all
r\leq\chi(t)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{-1}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,r],-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{0}}(X_{\cdot})\right]\geq c_{2}(1+r)^{-C_{4}},

(9.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{-1}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,r],-\widehat{W}_{\cdot}}^{J_{0}}(X_{\cdot})\right]\geq\widehat{c}_{2}(1+r)^{-\widehat{C}_{4}}.

(9.5)

We will check in Section
11
that these assumptions hold for the specific
W
we use in the first half of the paper.

Definition 9.3
.

For
t\geq 0
,
h_{t}:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R}
,
y\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
,
y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
define

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}^{(y_{0})}_{t,h}\right]:=\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot}+h_{t}(\cdot)+\frac{\cdot}{t}W_{t})\right],

\displaystyle p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[A_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}\right]:=\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot}+h_{t}(\cdot))\right]

\displaystyle p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[A_{t}^{(y_{0})}\right]:=\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot})\right].

Set

\displaystyle Z_{t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}_{y}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{y}}=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}-h_{t}^{\prime}(s)-\frac{W_{t}}{t}\;\mathrm{d}X_{s}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left(h^{\prime}_{t}(s)+\frac{W_{t}}{t}\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-h_{t}^{\prime}(t)X_{t}+h_{t}^{\prime}(0)y-\frac{W_{t}(X_{t}-y)}{t}+\int_{0}^{t}h_{t}^{\prime\prime}(s)X_{s}\mathrm{d}s-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left(h^{\prime}_{t}(s)+\frac{W_{t}}{t}\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right)

(9.6)

We have that

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{y}}\left[(Z_{t})^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{A}_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}}\right]

(9.7)

and since Girsanov implies that
(X_{s}+h_{t}(s)+\frac{s}{t}W_{t})_{s\leq t}
is a Brownian motion with regard to
\mathbb{Q}_{y}
(
9.7
) reduces controlling
\widetilde{p}_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)/p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)
to controlling
(Z_{t})^{-1}
on
\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{A}_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}}
. Furthermore, Section
9.2
allows us to control
\widetilde{p}_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)/p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)
since applying Theorem
9.2
with
c_{t}=\frac{W_{t}}{t}
yields that for
t\geq e
,
y,y_{0}\in[-2\log(t)^{2},0]

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{p}_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)}{p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}}e^{-y\frac{W_{t}}{t}}e^{|y_{0}-1|\cdot\left|\frac{W_{t}}{t}\right|}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Ass:WBMlog}}}{{\leq}}e^{\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}}e^{10\mathcal{C}_{\log}},

(9.8)

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{p}_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)}{p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)}

\displaystyle\geq e^{\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}}e^{-y\frac{W_{t}}{t}}e^{-|y_{0}-1|\cdot\left|\frac{W_{t}}{t}\right|}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Ass:WBMlog}}}{{\geq}}e^{\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}}e^{-10\mathcal{C}_{\log}}.

(9.9)

9.3
Bound Used in the Upper Bound of the Right Tail

In this section, we consider
h^{\smallfrown}_{t}:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R},s\mapsto-\left(((1+s)\wedge(1+t-s))^{1/6}-1\right)/2
.

The aim of this section is to derive an upper bound on
p_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(y)/p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)
, to formulate this upper bound we need some additional definitions. We recall (
8.2
), (
8.4
). (
8.5
) and Definition
9.3
.

Definition 9.4
.

For
\widehat{W}:=(W_{t-s}-W_{t})_{s\in[0,t]}
and
y,y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
set

\displaystyle(p_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}

\displaystyle:=p^{\ast}_{t}(W,t,h^{\smallfrown})^{-1}:=e^{5\mathcal{C}_{\log}+\mathcal{C}_{\log}^{2}}C_{2}^{-1}c_{2}^{-1}(1+\chi(t))^{C_{4}}\log(t)^{4\gamma}p_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(y)^{-1},

\displaystyle(\widehat{p}_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}

\displaystyle:=\widehat{p}^{\ast}_{t}(W,t,h^{\smallfrown})^{-1}:=e^{5\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\log}+\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\log}^{2}}\widehat{C}_{2}^{-1}\widehat{c}_{2}^{-1}(1+\chi(t))^{\widehat{C}_{4}}\log(t)^{4\widehat{\gamma}}\widehat{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(y)^{-1},

where
\widehat{p}_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)
uses
\widehat{W}
instead of
W
.

Choose
\kappa:=\kappa(W,\widehat{W},t,h^{\smallfrown},y,y_{0})>0
such that

\forall_{s\leq\chi(t)}\qquad-\kappa(1+s)^{\frac{2}{3}}-h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(s)\leq-2(\max\{\mathcal{C}_{\log},\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\log}\})(1+s)^{\frac{2}{3}}

(9.10)

Furthermore, fix
1/8\geq\delta>0
and choose
\eta:=\eta(W,\widehat{W},t,h^{\smallfrown},\delta,y,y_{0})<0
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}6e^{-\frac{\kappa^{2}l^{\frac{4}{3}-1}+\eta^{2}l^{-1}}{2}}e^{15\mathcal{C}_{\log}+\mathcal{C}_{\log}^{2}}c_{2}^{-1}(l+1)^{C_{4}}C_{2}^{-2}e^{4C_{1}}4^{\gamma+2}\left(|y|+3\mathcal{C}_{\log}(1+l)^{\frac{2}{3}}+|\eta|\right)^{4\gamma+3C_{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta,

(9.11)

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}6e^{-\frac{\kappa^{2}l^{\frac{4}{3}-1}+\eta^{2}l^{-1}}{2}}e^{15\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\log}+\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\log}^{2}}\widehat{c}_{2}^{-1}(l+1)^{\widehat{C}_{4}}\widehat{C}_{2}^{-2}e^{4C_{1}}4^{\widehat{\gamma}+2}\left(|y_{0}|+3\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\log}(1+l)^{\frac{2}{3}}+|\eta|\right)^{4\widehat{\gamma}+3\widehat{C}_{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta.

(9.12)

In this section, we will prove the following theorem.

Theorem 9.5
.

There exist
c,C>0
such that for
\lambda>0
arbitrary there is a
t_{0}(\lambda)\geq 0
such that for
t\geq t_{0}(\lambda)
with (
9.3
) and
y,y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
,

\displaystyle\frac{p_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(y)-\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{2}p_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0}-1)}(y-j)\right)}{p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{c(\mathcal{C}_{\log}+\kappa-\eta)}\left(1+t^{-\lambda}p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)^{-1}\right)+Ct^{-\lambda}((p_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}+(\widehat{p}_{t}^{\ast})^{-1})p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)^{-1}.

Remark 9.6
.

We note that
\eta
and
\kappa
can be chosen as continuous functions of
y
,
y_{0}
,
C_{1}
,
\mathcal{C}_{\log}
,
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\log}
,
C_{2}^{-1}
,
\widehat{C}_{2}^{-1}
,
\gamma
,
\widehat{\gamma}
,
C_{4}
,
\widehat{C_{4}}
,
c_{2}^{-1}
,
\widehat{c_{2}}^{-1}
,
\delta
.

Definition 9.7
.

To slightly shorten the following definitions shorten
X_{s}^{h^{\smallfrown}}:=X_{s}+h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(s)
. Using this define

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{start}}

\displaystyle:=\{\forall_{s\leq\chi(t)}\,X_{s}^{h^{\smallfrown}}\geq-\kappa(1+s)^{\frac{2}{3}}+y+\eta\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{end}}

\displaystyle:=\{\forall_{s\geq t-\chi(t)}\,X_{s}^{h^{\smallfrown}}\geq-\kappa(1+t-s)^{\frac{2}{3}}+X_{t}+\eta\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{start}}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{end}},

\displaystyle B_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t),k}

\displaystyle:=\Bigg\{\min\Big\{\min_{\chi(t)\leq s\leq t/2}\frac{X_{s}^{h^{\smallfrown}}+s/tW_{t}-y}{(1+s)^{\frac{2}{3}}},\min_{t/2\leq s\leq t-\chi(t)}\frac{X_{s}^{h^{\smallfrown}}-(t-s)/tW_{t}-y_{0}}{(1+t-s)^{\frac{2}{3}}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt\in[-k,-k+1)\Bigg\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}(y)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}\right].

Note that

\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}\right]\leq\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}\right]=p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y),

(9.13)

using that under
\mathbb{Q}_{y}
we have that
(X_{s}^{h^{\smallfrown}}+\frac{s}{t}W_{t})_{s\leq t}
is a Brownian motion starting at
y
.

The proof of Theorem
9.5
is split into the following two propositions.

Proposition 9.8
.

There are
c,C>0
such that for
y,y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
and
\lambda>0
there is a
t_{0}(\lambda)>0
such that for all
t\geq t_{0}(\lambda)
,

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}(y)}{p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{c(\kappa-\eta+\mathcal{C}_{\log})}\exp\left(\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}\right)\left(1+t^{-\lambda}p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)^{-1}\right).

Proposition 9.9
.

There is a
C>0
such that for
y,y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
,
\lambda>0
there is a
t_{0}(\lambda)>0
such that for
t\geq t(\lambda)
, for which additionally (
9.3
) holds,

\frac{\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(y)-\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{2}\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0}-1)}(y-j)\right)}{\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}(y)}\leq 2+Ct^{-\lambda}((p_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}+(\widehat{p}_{t}^{\ast})^{-1})\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}(y)^{-1}.

Proof of Theorem
9.5
assuming Propositions
9.8
and
9.9
.

Combine (
9.9
) with Proposition
9.8
and Proposition
9.9
.
∎

Lemma 9.10
.

There is a
C>0
such that for
y,y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
and for all
k\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}\cap B_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t),k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\frac{(k-1)^{2}}{2}\left(\lfloor\chi(t)\rfloor-1\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}}.

Proof of Proposition
9.8
assuming Lemma
9.10
.

∎

We have that

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}(y)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{y}}\left[(Z_{t})^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}}\right]

(9.14)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{y}}\left[(Z_{t})^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}}\mathbf{1}_{B_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t),k}}\right]

We can infer by direct calculation from (
9.6
) and Definition
9.7
that there are
c,C>0
such that on
\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}\cap B_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t),k}

\displaystyle(Z_{t})^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{c(\mathcal{C}_{\log}+\kappa-\eta+k)}\exp\left(\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}\right).

(9.15)

In this calculation, we use assumption (
8.2
) as well as
|y|,|y_{0}|\leq\log(t)^{2}
.
Plugging (
9.15
) into (
9.14
) yields that

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}(y)

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{c(\kappa-\eta+\mathcal{C}_{\log})}\exp\left(\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}e^{ck}\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}\cap B_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t),k}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L.\ref{Lem:Bound},\eqref{eq:Triv}}}{{\leq}}Ce^{c(\kappa-\eta+\mathcal{C}_{\log})}\exp\left(\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}\right)\left(p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}e^{ck}e^{-\frac{(k-1)^{2}}{2}\left(\lfloor\chi(t)\rfloor-1\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(e^{c(\kappa-\eta+\mathcal{C}_{\log})}\exp\left(\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}\right)\left(p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)+e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left(\lfloor\chi(t)\rfloor-1\right)^{\frac{4}{3}}}\right)\right)

Since
e^{-1/2(\lfloor\chi(t)\rfloor-1)^{\frac{4}{3}}}t^{\lambda}\to 0
for
t\to\infty
and all
\lambda>0
, rearranging this yields the claim of Proposition
9.8
.

Proof of Lemma
9.10
.

We have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[B_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t),k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=\lfloor\chi(t)\rfloor}^{\left\lceil\frac{t}{2}\right\rceil}\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\min_{l-1\leq s\leq l}X_{s}^{h^{\smallfrown}}+s/tW_{t}-y<-(k-1)\cdot l^{\frac{2}{3}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip-43.0pt+\sum_{l=\left\lfloor\frac{t}{2}\right\rfloor}^{\lceil t-\chi(t)\rceil}\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\min_{l\leq s\leq l+1}X_{s}^{h^{\smallfrown}}-(t-s)/tW_{t}-y_{0}<-(k-1)(t-l)^{\frac{2}{3}},X_{t}\in[y_{0}-1,y_{0}]\right].

(9.16)

First, handle the sum from
\lfloor\chi(t)\rfloor
to
\left\lceil\frac{t}{2}\right\rceil
in (
9.16
). Since
(X_{s}^{h^{\smallfrown}}+s/tW_{t}-y)_{s\in[0,t]}
is a Brownian motion starting at 0 with regard to
\mathbb{Q}_{y}
we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{l=\lfloor\chi(t)\rfloor}^{\left\lceil\frac{t}{2}\right\rceil}\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\min_{l-1\leq s\leq l}X_{s}^{h^{\smallfrown}}+s/tW_{t}-y<-(k-1)\cdot l^{\frac{2}{3}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=\lfloor\chi(t)\rfloor}^{\left\lceil\frac{t}{2}\right\rceil}4e^{-\frac{(k-1)^{2}l^{\frac{4}{3}}}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\frac{(k-1)^{2}}{2}(\lfloor\chi(t)\rfloor-1)^{\frac{4}{3}}}.

(9.17)

The second sum in (
9.16
) can be handled analogously. Combining (
9.16
) and the last two facts proves the lemma.
∎

Proof of Proposition
9.9

We recall Definitions
9.4
and
9.7
. We have that

\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}(y)\geq\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(y)-\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{start}}^{c}\right]-\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{end}}^{c}\right].

(9.18)

Next, we prove upper bounds for
\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{start}}^{c}\right]
and
\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{end}}^{c}\right]
, we do this in detail for the first term and handle the second term by symmetry. Thus we aim to prove the following two propositions.

Proposition 9.11
.

There is a
C>0
such that for
y,y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
,
\lambda>0
there is a
t_{0}(\lambda)>0
such that for
t\geq t_{0}(\lambda)
, for which (
9.3
) holds,

\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{start}}^{c}\right]\leq\delta\left(\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0}-1)}(y)+\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(y)\right)+Ct^{-\lambda}(p_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}.

Proposition 9.12
.

There is a
C>0
such that for
y,y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
,
\lambda>0
there is a
t_{0}(\lambda)>0
such that for
t\geq t_{0}(\lambda)
, for which (
9.3
) holds,

\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}^{(y_{0})}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{end}}^{c}\right]\leq\delta\left(\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0}-1)}(y-2)+\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0}-1)}(y-1)\right)+Ct^{-\lambda}(\widehat{p}_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}.

Remark 9.13
.

We prove Proposition
9.12
by time reversal, thus essentially copying the proof of Proposition
9.11
.

Proof of Proposition
9.9
assuming Propositions
9.11
,
9.12
.

Plugging Propositions
9.11
,
9.12
into equality (
9.18
) immediately yields the claim of Proposition
9.9
.
∎

We need one further definition before proceeding with the proof of Proposition
9.11
.

Definition 9.14
.

For
r\leq\chi(t)
set

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle:=\inf\left\{s\leq t:X_{s}-y\leq-2\mathcal{C}_{\log}(1+s)^{\frac{2}{3}}+\eta\right\},

\displaystyle w_{r}

\displaystyle:=y-2\mathcal{C}_{\log}(1+r)^{\frac{2}{3}}+W_{t}r/t+\eta,

(9.19)

\displaystyle m_{r}(s)

\displaystyle:=W_{s+r}-h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(s+r).

(9.20)

The rough strategy is to bound

\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{start}}^{c}\right]\leq\sum\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\tau\in[l-1,l]\right]\mathbb{P}_{w_{r}-W_{t}r/t}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t-r],-m_{r}(\cdot)+(r+\cdot)/tW_{t}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot})\right],

use that
\tau
has exponential tails and bound

\mathbb{P}_{w_{r}-W_{t}r/t}\left[\forall_{s\leq t-r}\,X_{s}\leq m_{r}(s),X_{t-r}-W_{t}\in[y_{0}-1,y_{0}]\right]

by applying Theorems
9.2
,
8.2
and
8.4
. We break this up into a few lemmata, which we state next. After this, we will prove Proposition
9.11
assuming the lemmata. Finally, we will prove the lemmata.

Lemma 9.15
.

We have that for
t\geq e
and
y,y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{start}}^{c}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil\chi(t)\rceil}\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\tau\in[l-1,l]\right]\cdot

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\max_{r\in[l-1,l]}\Bigg(e^{\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{t}-\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}\frac{t-r}{t}+5\mathcal{C}_{\log}}\mathbb{P}_{w_{r}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t-r],-m_{r}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot})\right]\Bigg).

Lemma 9.16
.

For
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
y,y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
, we have that

\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\tau\in[l-1,l]\right]\leq 2e^{-\frac{(2\mathcal{C}_{\log})^{2}l^{\frac{4}{3}-1}+\eta^{2}l^{-1}}{2}}.

Lemma 9.17
.

We have that for
t>0
and
y,y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{w_{r}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t-r],-m_{r}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(p_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0}-1)}(w_{r}-W_{r})+p_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(w_{r}-W_{r})\right)\mathbb{P}_{-1}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,r],-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{0}}(X_{\cdot})\right]^{-1}.

Lemma 9.18
.

We have that for
y,y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
,
\lambda>0
arbitrary and
t
for which (

) holds,

\displaystyle p_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(w_{r}-W_{r})

\displaystyle\leq p_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(y)C_{2}^{-2}e^{4C_{1}}4^{\gamma+2}\left(|w_{r}-W_{r}|\right)^{4\gamma+3C_{3}}+Ce^{-5\mathcal{C}_{\log}-\mathcal{C}_{\log}^{2}}t^{-\lambda}(p_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}.

Proof of Proposition
9.11
assuming Lemmata

–

.

By (

\displaystyle p_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(y)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}}e^{10\mathcal{C}_{\log}}\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(y).

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil\chi(t)\rceil}2e^{-\frac{(2\mathcal{C}_{\log})^{2}l^{\frac{4}{3}-1}+\eta^{2}l^{-1}}{2}}\max_{r\in[l-1,l]}\Bigg(e^{\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{t}-\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}\frac{t-r}{t}+5\mathcal{C}_{\log}}\cdot

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\cdot\Big(\left(p_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0}-1)}(w_{r}-W_{r})+p_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(w_{r}-W_{r})\right)\mathbb{P}_{-1}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,r],-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{0}}(X_{\cdot})\right]^{-1}\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\mathcal{C}_{\log}^{2}+\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}-\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}\frac{t-\lceil\chi(t)\rceil}{t}}t^{-\lambda}(p_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}+\left(\widetilde{p}_{t,h}^{(y_{0}-1)}(y)+\widetilde{p}_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)\right)C_{2}^{-2}e^{4C_{1}}4^{\gamma+2}e^{15\mathcal{C}_{\log}+\mathcal{C}_{\log}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\left(\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil\chi(t)\rceil}2e^{-\frac{(2\mathcal{C}_{\log})^{2}l^{\frac{4}{3}-1}+\eta^{2}l^{-1}}{2}}c_{2}^{-1}(l+1)^{C_{4}}e^{\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}-\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}\frac{t-l}{t}}\max_{r\in[l-1,l]}|w_{r}-W_{r}|^{4\gamma+3C_{3}}\right).

(9.22)

(9.23)

\displaystyle\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}-\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}\frac{t-l}{t}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{W_{t}^{2}\lceil\chi(t)\rceil}{2t^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Ass:WBMlog}}}{{\leq}}\mathcal{C}_{\log}^{2}.

(9.24)

Proof of Lemma
9.15
.

By the choice of
\kappa
in (

) we have that
-\kappa(1+s)^{\frac{2}{3}}-h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(s)\leq-2\mathcal{C}_{\log}(1+s)^{\frac{2}{3}}

. Thus we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{start}}^{c}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}\cap\left\{\exists_{s\leq\chi(t)}:X_{s}-y\leq-\kappa(1+s)^{\frac{2}{3}}-h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(s)+\eta\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}\cap\left\{\exists_{s\leq\chi(t)}:X_{s}-y\leq-2\mathcal{C}_{\log}(1+s)^{\frac{2}{3}}+\eta\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil\chi(t)\rceil}\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\tau\in[l-1,l]\right]\cdot\max_{r\in[l-1,l]}\mathbb{P}_{w_{r}-W_{t}r/t}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t-r],-m_{r}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{s}+W_{t}(s+r)/t)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil\chi(t)\rceil}\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\tau\in[l-1,l]\right]\cdot\max_{r\in[l-1,l]}\mathbb{P}_{w_{r}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t-r],-m_{r}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{s}+W_{t}s/t)\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle T.\ \ref{Theo: LinTerm},\eqref{Ass:WBMlog}}}{{\leq}}\sum_{l=1}^{\lceil\chi(t)\rceil}\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\tau\in[l-1,l]\right]\max_{r\in[l-1,l]}e^{\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{t}-\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}\frac{t-r}{t}+5\mathcal{C}_{\log}}\mathbb{P}_{w_{r}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t-r],-m_{r}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot})\right],

where in the last step we applied Theorem

for time

and

.
∎

Proof of Lemma
9.16
.

We have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\tau\in[l-1,l]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\min_{s\in[0,l]}X_{s}-y\leq-2\mathcal{C}_{\log}l^{\frac{2}{3}}+\eta\right]

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[(X_{l}-y)/\sqrt{l}\leq-2\mathcal{C}_{\log}l^{\frac{2}{3}-1/2}+\eta l^{-1/2}\right]\leq 2e^{-\frac{(2\mathcal{C}_{\log})^{2}l^{\frac{4}{3}-1}+\eta^{2}l^{-1}}{2}}.\qed

Proof of Lemma
9.17
.

Applying (
2.4
) from Lemma
2.8
for
z_{1}=w_{r}-W_{r}
,
z_{2}=y_{0}
,

,

,

and

yields that

\displaystyle p_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0}-1)}(w_{r}-W_{r})+p_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(w_{r}-W_{r})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{w_{r}-W_{r}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,r],h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{w_{r}+h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(r)-W_{r}}}(X_{\cdot})\right]\cdot

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\cdot\mathbb{P}_{w_{r}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[r,t],h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot}-X_{r})\right].

Using

and the definition of

in (

) yields that

\mathbb{P}_{w_{r}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[r,t],h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot}-X_{r})\right]=\mathbb{P}_{w_{r}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t-r],-m_{r}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot})\right]

and thus it remains to prove that

\mathbb{P}_{w_{r}-W_{r}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,r],h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{w_{r}+h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(r)-W_{r}}}(X_{\cdot})\right]\geq\mathbb{P}_{-1}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,r],-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{0}}(X_{\cdot})\right].

(9.25)

Equation (
9.25
) is an immediate consequence of the facts that on the one hand
w_{r}-W_{r}\leq-1
by the definition of
\mathcal{C}_{\log}
and
w_{r}

h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(s)\leq 0
for all

.
∎

Proof of Lemma
9.18
.

Since
r\leq\chi(t)
, recalling (

) and applying (

) yields that

\displaystyle|w_{r}-W_{r}|

\displaystyle\leq\log(t)^{2}+(\mathcal{C}_{\log})(1+\log(t)^{1.5})+\log(t)^{1.5}\mathcal{C}_{\log}\leq 2\log(t)^{2}

by assumption (

). Furthermore, using the definition of

, yields that

to get that there is a

which is what we wanted to prove.
∎

Proof of Proposition
9.12
.

Define
Y_{s}:=X_{t-s}-X_{t}
. Using the symmetry of
h_{t}^{\smallfrown}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{end}}^{c}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Big[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],-\widehat{W}_{\cdot}}^{y_{0}-1,J_{y}}\left(Y_{\cdot}+h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(s)+\frac{s}{t}\widehat{W}_{t}\right),\exists_{s\in[0,\chi(t)]}Y_{s}\leq-\kappa(1+s)^{\frac{2}{3}}-h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(s)+y_{0}+\eta\Big]

(9.26)

Furthermore, we have that

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y_{0})}(y)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],-W_{\cdot}}^{y,J_{y_{0}}}\left(X_{s}+h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(s)+\frac{s}{t}W_{t}\right)\right]\geq\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],-\widehat{W}_{\cdot}}^{y_{0},J_{y+1}}\left(Y_{\cdot}+h^{\smallfrown}_{t}(s)+\frac{s}{t}\widehat{W}_{t}\right)\right]

(9.27)

We notice that
(Y_{s})_{s\in[0,t]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(X_{s})_{s\in[0,t]}
. Thus rerunning the argument proving Proposition
9.11
, while considering equations (
9.26
) and (
9.27
), finishes the proof.
∎

9.4
Bounds Used in the Lower Bound of the Right Tail

In this section we consider
h_{t}^{\smallsmile}:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R},s\mapsto\left(((1+s)\wedge(1+t-s))^{1/6}-1\right)
and
y\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
,
y_{0}\in[-\log(t)^{2},0]
.

We prove the following theorem.

Theorem 9.19
.

For
\eta
,
\kappa
defined analogously to Theorem
9.5
, but using

instead of

, we have that there are

such that for arbitrary

such that for

with (

) and for

\displaystyle\frac{p_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}(y)}{p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)-\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}\sum_{j=0}^{2}p_{t}^{(y_{0}-1)}(y-j)}

\displaystyle\geq Ce^{-c(\mathcal{C}_{\log}+\kappa-\eta)}\left(\frac{1}{2}-Ct^{-\lambda}\left((p_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}+(\widehat{p}_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}+1\right)p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)^{-1}\right).

Sketch of the proof of Theorem
9.19
.

We recall (
9.6
). Furthermore, define

\displaystyle B_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t),2}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\min_{\chi(t)\leq s\leq t/2}\frac{X_{s}^{h^{\smallsmile}}+s/tW_{t}-y}{(1+s)^{\frac{2}{3}}}\geq-2\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\cap\left\{\min_{t/2\leq s\leq t-\chi(t)}\frac{X_{s}^{h^{\smallsmile}}-(t-s)/tW_{t}-y_{0}}{(1+t-s)^{\frac{2}{3}}}\geq-2\right\}.

(9.28)

As in (

) we can infer from (

) and (

such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}(y)

\displaystyle\geq Ce^{-c(\mathcal{C}_{\log}+\kappa-\eta)}\exp\left(\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}\right)\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t)}\cap{B_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t),2}}\right].

(9.29)

We have that
\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t)}\cap{B_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t),2}}\right]\geq\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t)}\right]-\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t)}\cap B_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t),2}^{c}\right]
. As in Lemma
9.10
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t)}\cap B_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t),2}^{c}\right]

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{-\lambda}

(9.30)

for
t
big enough, depending on
\lambda
, but not on
W
.

Equations (
9.29
) and (
9.30
) imply that for
t
big enough depending on
\lambda
, but not on
