,

\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}(y)\geq Ce^{-c(\mathcal{C}_{\log}+\kappa-\eta)}\exp\left(\frac{W_{t}^{2}}{2t}\right)\left(\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t)}\right]-t^{-\lambda}\right).

(9.31)

Furthermore, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t)}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}\right]-\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\text{start}}^{c}\right]-\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\text{end}}^{c}\right]

\displaystyle=p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)-\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\text{start}}^{c}\right]-\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\text{end}}^{c}\right].

From here one proceeds as in the proof of Proposition
9.9
. We need to calculate the tails of
\tau
with regard to
\mathbb{Q}_{y}
instead of
\mathbb{P}_{y}
. This change results in the calculation

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\tau\in[l-1,l]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\min_{s\in[0,l]}X_{s}^{h^{\smallsmile}}+s/tW_{t}-y\leq-2\mathcal{C}_{\log}(1+l)^{\frac{2}{3}}+h^{\smallsmile}_{t}(l)+l^{\frac{2}{3}}\mathcal{C}_{\log}+\eta\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\min_{s\in[0,l]}X_{s}^{h^{\smallsmile}}+s/tW_{t}-y\leq-\mathcal{C}_{\log}(1+l)^{\frac{2}{3}}+\eta\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2e^{-\frac{(\mathcal{C}_{\log})^{2}l^{\frac{4}{3}-1}+\eta^{2}l^{-1}}{2}}.

The rest of the proof of Proposition
9.9
needs only very minor changes
5
5

5

Since we work with regard to
\mathbb{Q}_{y}
our Brownian motion is
(X_{s}+h^{\smallsmile}_{t}(s)+s/tW_{t})_{s\in[0,t]}
. Thus the analogue to
m_{r}
of (
9.20
) won’t have an
h^{\smallsmile}_{t}
term, which only simplifies the situation.
to give that

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\text{start}}^{c}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\delta\left(p_{t}^{(y_{0}-1)}(y)+p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)\right)+Ct^{-\lambda}(p_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}

as well as

\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}\cap\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\text{end}}^{c}\right]\leq\delta\left(p_{t}^{(y_{0}-1)}(y-2)+p_{t}^{(y_{0}-1)}(y-1)\right)+Ct^{-\lambda}(\widehat{p}_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}

which yields that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{Q}_{y}\left[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t)}^{(y_{0})}\right]}{p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)-\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}\sum_{j=0}^{2}p_{t}^{(y_{0}-1)}(y-j)}\geq\frac{1}{2}-Ct^{-\lambda}\left((p_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}+(\widehat{p}_{t}^{\ast})^{-1}\right)p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)^{-1}.

(9.32)

Combining (
9.8
), (
9.31
) and (
9.32
) and writing

\displaystyle\frac{p_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}(y)}{p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)-\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}\sum_{j=0}^{2}p_{t}^{(y_{0}-1)}(y-j)}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}(y)}{\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}(y)}\frac{\widetilde{p}_{t,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(y_{0})}(y)}{\mathbb{Q}_{y}[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t)}]}\frac{\mathbb{Q}_{y}[\widetilde{A}_{t,h^{\smallsmile},\chi(t)}]}{p_{t}^{(y_{0})}(y)-\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}\sum_{j=0}^{2}p_{t}^{(y_{0}-1)}(y-j)}

gives Theorem
9.19
.
∎

10
Crude lower bound on
p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)

The aim of this section is to provide a lower bound on
\smash{p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)}
of Definition
8.1
. This will be used to deal with the
p_{t,h}^{(y_{0})}(x)^{-1}
terms occurring in Theorems
8.4
,
9.5
and
9.19
and additionally be the main ingredient in the proof of Lemma
2.6
. We recall Definition
8.1
. The idea of the proof is to replace the event
\{\forall_{s\leq t}\,X_{s}\leq W_{s}-h_{t}(s)\}
with
\{\forall_{s\leq t}X_{s}\leq g_{t}(s)\}
with a
g_{t}
for which we can apply Girsanov’s theorem, to reduce the situation to a ballot theorem for Brownian motion. The
g_{t}(s)
will roughly be a piecewise linear approximation of the running minimum of
W_{s}-h_{t}(s)
. The next definition defines
g_{t}
precisely as well as some related quantities, see Figure
5
.

Figure 5:
The event in (
10.1
). Drawn are
(W_{s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
(dotted line),
\Xi_{j}(W)
,
j\in\{0,\dots,5\}
(dashed lines) and
g_{t}
(solid piecewise linear curve) with parameters
h=0
,
t=96
,
t_{1}=32
,
t_{2}=64
,
k_{1}=5
. We note that it is less likely to stay below
(g_{s})_{s\leq t}
than below
(W_{s})_{s\leq t}
.

Definition 10.1
.

Fix
t>0
. Set
k_{1}(t):=\lfloor\log_{2}(t/3)\rfloor
,
t_{1}(t):=2^{k_{1}}
,
t_{2}(t):=t-t_{1}
, the dependence on
t
will be omitted from notation. For
j\leq k_{1}-1
define

\displaystyle\Xi_{j}^{\text{start}}(W)

\displaystyle:=\min_{s\in[0,2^{j+1}]}W_{s},

\displaystyle\Xi_{j}^{\text{end}}(W)

\displaystyle:=\min_{s\in[t-2^{j+1},t]}W_{s}-W_{t},

\displaystyle\Xi_{j}(W)

\displaystyle:=\min\{\Xi_{j}^{\text{start}}(W),\Xi_{j}^{\text{end}}(W)\},

\displaystyle\Xi_{k_{1}}(W)

\displaystyle:=\min\{\min_{s\in[0,t]}W_{s},\min_{s\in[0,t]}W_{s}-W_{t},0\},

\displaystyle\Delta_{j}(W)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\Xi_{j+1}(W)-\Xi_{j}(W)}{2^{j}}.

For
j\in\{1,\dots,k_{1}-1\}
define
\Xi_{j}(h)
(and analogous variables) as above, substituting
-h_{t}(s)
for
W_{s}
,
s\in[0,t]
. Furthermore, choose
\Xi_{0}(h)
such that
s\Xi_{0}(h)\leq-h_{t}(s)
for all
s\in[0,1]
and
(t-s)\Xi_{0}(h)\leq-h_{t}(s)
for all
s\in[t-1,t]
.

Set
\Xi_{j}:=\Xi_{j}(W)+\Xi_{j}(h)
and
\Delta_{j}=\Delta_{j}(W)+\Delta_{j}(h)
. Finally, set

g_{t}(s)=\begin{cases}s\Xi_{0},&\ s\in[0,1),\\
\Xi_{j}+(s-2^{j})\Delta_{j},&\ s\in[2^{j},2^{j+1}]\\
\Xi_{k_{1}}+(s-t_{1})/(t_{2}-t_{1})(W_{t}-h_{t}(t)),&\ s\in[t_{1},t_{2}],\\
\Xi_{j}+(t-2^{j}-s)\Delta_{j}+W_{t}-h_{t}(t),&\ s\in[t-2^{j+1},t-2^{j}],\\
(t-s)\Xi_{0}+W_{t},&\ s\in(t-1,t].\end{cases}

Theorem 10.2
.

For
W
piecewise linear and
y,y_{0}\in[-\sqrt{t},0]
, there is a
\gamma_{0}>0
deterministic such that for
t\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],h_{t}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot})\right]

\displaystyle\geq t^{-\gamma_{0}-\log(t)^{-1}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}\hskip-0.99594pt-\hskip-0.99594pt1}\Delta_{j}^{2}2^{j}+2(1-2^{3/2})\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}\hskip-0.99594pt-\hskip-0.99594pt1}\Delta_{j}2^{j/2}+\Xi_{0}^{2}-4\Xi_{0}+3\frac{W_{t}^{2}+h_{t}(t)^{2}}{t_{2}-t_{1}}+(\sqrt{t_{1}}+|y-y_{0}|)\frac{|W_{t}|+|h_{t}(t)|}{t_{2}-t_{1}}\right)}.

Proof.

As illustrated in Figure
5
we have that by definition
g_{t}(s)\leq W_{s}
for all
s\in[0,t]
as well as
g_{t}(t)=W_{t}=W_{t}-h_{t}(t)
and thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],h_{t}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot})\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\forall_{s\leq t}\,X_{s}\leq g_{t}(s),X_{t}-g_{t}(t)\in[y_{0}-1,y_{0}]\right].

(10.1)

We apply Girsanov to remove the
g_{t}
. For controlling the Radon-Nikodym derivative appearing in the application of Girsanov, we need control over
X_{2^{j+1}}-X_{2^{j}}
,
j\in\{0,\dots,k_{1}-1\}
. Thus define

\displaystyle A_{j}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=\left\{X_{2^{j+1}}-g_{t}(2^{j+1})\in[y-2\sqrt{2^{j+1}},y-\sqrt{2^{j+1}}]\right\},

\displaystyle A_{j}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=\Big\{X_{t-2^{j+1}}-g_{t}(t-2^{j+1})\in[y_{0}-2\sqrt{2^{j+1}},y_{0}-\sqrt{2^{j+1}}]\Big\}.

Furthermore, set

\displaystyle A_{\text{start}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{y-2\leq X_{1}-g_{t}(1)\leq y-1\right\}

\displaystyle A_{\text{end}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{y_{0}-2\leq X_{t-1}-g_{t}(t-1)\leq y_{0}-1\right\}.

By monotonicity and (
10.1
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],h_{t}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{J_{y_{0}}}(X_{\cdot})\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{y}\left[A_{\text{start}},\bigcap_{i\in\{1,2\}}\bigcap_{j=0}^{k_{1}-1}A_{j}^{(i)},A_{\text{end}},\forall_{s\leq t}\,X_{s}\leq g_{t}(s),X_{t}-g_{t}(t)\in[y_{0}-1,y_{0}]\right]:=\mathbb{P}_{y}[A(y_{0})].

Consider
\mathbb{Q}_{y}
with

\displaystyle Z_{t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{d\mathbb{Q}_{y}}{d\mathbb{P}_{y}}=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}g_{t}^{\prime}(s)\;\mathrm{d}X_{s}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}(g_{t}^{\prime}(s))^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-1}\big(\Delta_{j}\left((X_{2^{j+1}}-X_{2^{j}})-(X_{t-2^{j}}-X_{t-2^{j+1}})\right)\big)-\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-1}\Delta_{j}^{2}2^{j}-\Xi_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Xi_{0}\cdot\left(X_{1}-X_{0}-(X_{t}-X_{t-1})+(X_{t_{2}}-X_{t_{1}})\frac{W_{t}-h_{t}(t)}{t_{2}-t_{1}}-\frac{(W_{t}-h_{t}(t))^{2}}{2(t_{2}-t_{1})}\right)\Bigg).

On
A(y_{0})
we have that

\displaystyle|X_{2^{j+1}}-X_{2^{j}}|

\displaystyle\leq-2^{j}\Delta_{j}+2^{\frac{j}{2}}\left(2^{\frac{3}{2}}-1\right),

\displaystyle|X_{t-2^{j}}-X_{t-2^{j+1}}|

\displaystyle\leq-2^{j}\Delta_{j}+2^{\frac{j}{2}}\left(1-2^{\frac{3}{2}}-1\right),

\displaystyle X_{1}-X_{0}

\displaystyle\geq\Xi_{0}-2,

\displaystyle X_{t}-X_{t-1}

\displaystyle\leq 2-\Xi_{0},

\displaystyle|X_{t_{2}}-X_{t_{1}}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{t_{1}}+|W_{t}-h_{t}(t)|+|y-y_{0}|.

Thus we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y}[A(y_{0})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Q}_{y}[Z_{t}^{-1};A(y_{0})]

\displaystyle\geq\exp\Bigg(\begin{aligned} &-\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-1}\Delta_{j}^{2}2^{j}-\left(2-2^{\frac{5}{2}}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-1}\Delta_{j}2^{\frac{j}{2}}-\Xi_{0}^{2}+4\Xi_{0}\\
&-\frac{3(W_{t}-h_{t}(t))^{2}}{2(t_{2}-t_{1})}-\frac{(\sqrt{t_{1}}+|y-y_{0}|)|W_{t}-h_{t}(t)|}{t_{2}-t_{1}}\Bigg)\cdot\mathbb{Q}_{y}[A(y_{0})].\end{aligned}

By the Girsanov theorem we have that
(X_{s}-g_{t}(s))_{s\in[0,t]}
is a Brownian motion with regard to
\mathbb{Q}_{y}
. This implies that there is a constant
\gamma_{0}>0
such that for
t\geq 2
arbitrary
\mathbb{Q}_{y}[A(y_{0})]\geq t^{-\gamma_{0}}
. Thus we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y}[A(y_{0})]

\displaystyle\geq t^{-\log(t)^{-1}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-1}\Delta_{j}^{2}2^{j}+2(1-2^{3/2})\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-1}\Delta_{j}2^{j/2}+\Xi_{0}^{2}-4\Xi_{0}+3\frac{W_{t}^{2}+h_{t}(t)^{2}}{t_{2}-t_{1}}+(\sqrt{t_{1}}+|y-y_{0}|)\frac{|W_{t}|+|h_{t}(t)|}{t_{2}-t_{1}}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad\hskip 327.20668pt\cdot\mathbb{Q}_{y}[A(y_{0})]

\displaystyle\geq t^{-\gamma_{0}-\log(t)^{-1}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}\hskip-0.99594pt-\hskip-0.99594pt1}\Delta_{j}^{2}2^{j}+2(1-2^{3/2})\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}\hskip-0.99594pt-\hskip-0.99594pt1}\Delta_{j}2^{j/2}+\Xi_{0}^{2}-4\Xi_{0}+3\frac{W_{t}^{2}+h_{t}(t)^{2}}{t_{2}-t_{1}}+(\sqrt{t_{1}}+|y-y_{0}|)\frac{|W_{t}|+|h_{t}(t)|}{t_{2}-t_{1}}\right)}.\qed

11
Control for the Constants Introduced in Section
8.1
, Definition
9.4
and the Proof of Lemma
2.6

We recall Definitions
2.1
and
2.3
. From now on
W
will be as in Definition
2.3
. For
t>0
define
\widehat{W}
via
\widehat{W}_{s}:=W_{t-s}-W_{t}
, where the dependence on
t
is not reflected in the notation. Furthermore, from now on we will mostly care about
h
given by
h_{t}:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R}
,
s\mapsto\pm((1+s)\wedge(1+t-s)^{1/6}-1)
. We note here, that for these
h
the
C_{1}(h)
from (
8.3
) is finite. We recall Section
8.1
, we use
\widehat{C}
to denote variants of constants using
\widehat{W}
instead of
W
, for example we set
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\log}:=\mathcal{C}_{\log}(\widehat{W},t)
. We point out that in (
8.4
) the
\mathbb{P}
will from now on be denoted by
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}
since the probability is taken given
W
, i.e. given the environment.

Consider
f:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\to\mathbb{R}
. For
a,b>0
set

\gamma_{a,b}(W,f,t):=\inf\left\{\gamma>0:\forall_{t\geq s\geq 2}\,\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,s],f(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{-1,[-b\sqrt{s},-a\sqrt{s}]}(B_{\cdot})\right]\geq s^{-\gamma}\right\}

and
\gamma_{a,b}(\widehat{W},f,t)
analogous.

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
\beta\in\{\pm 1\}
. We consider
(h_{t})_{t\geq 0}
given by
h_{t}:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R},s\mapsto\beta(((1+s)\wedge(1+t-s))^{1/6}-1)
,
b>a\geq 0
. The families of random variables
(\gamma(W,h,n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
(\gamma(\widehat{W},h,n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are tight.

Proof.

On
[2,n/2]
we have that
h_{n}(s)=\beta\left((1+s)^{1/6}-1\right):=f(s)
. Thus it suffices to prove that
(\gamma_{a,b}(\widetilde{W},f,n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
\widetilde{W}\in\{W,\widehat{W}\}
are tight. Both of these are proved analogously, we will write the proof for
\widetilde{W}=W
and note that it goes through as written when replacing
W
by
\widehat{W}
.

By Theorem 1.10 in
[
19
]
we know that there exists
\gamma>0
independent of
a,b
such that
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\log\left(\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},f(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{-1,[-b\sqrt{n},-a\sqrt{n}]}(B_{\cdot})\right]\right)}{\log(n)}=-\gamma.

in particular
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\widetilde{\gamma}:=\inf_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\log\left(\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},f(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{-1,[-b\sqrt{n},-a\sqrt{n}]}(B_{\cdot})\right]\right)}{\log(n)}>-\infty.

(11.1)

We claim that there is a
c>0
such that for all
n\geq 2

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,n],f(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{-1,[-b\sqrt{n},-a\sqrt{n}]}(B_{\cdot})\right]}{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\cdots,n\},2^{\beta}f(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{-1,[-b\sqrt{n},-a\sqrt{n}]}(B_{\cdot})\right]}\geq c.

(11.2)

Combining (
11.1
) with (
11.2
) allows us to conclude the statement of the lemma, it remains to prove that (
11.2
) holds. For this purpose, we define

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{B}_{[0,n],f(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{-1,[-b\sqrt{n},-a\sqrt{n}]}(B_{\cdot}),\quad A_{2}:=\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\cdots,n\},2^{\beta}f(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{-1,[-b\sqrt{n},-a\sqrt{n}]}(B_{\cdot}).

We need to bound
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[A_{1}]/\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[A_{2}]
. In essence this calculation boils down to the fact, that if at times
\{0,\dots,n\}

B
is below a (curved) barrier, then it is unlikely that
B
crosses a multiple of the barrier in-between these times. We have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[A_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[A_{1}|(B_{1},\dots,B_{n})\right]\right]\geq\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[A_{1}|(B_{1},\dots,B_{n})\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[j-1,j],f(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{-1}(B_{\cdot})|(B_{j-1},B_{j})\right]\right].

(11.3)

To get a lower bound on
\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[j-1,j],f(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{-1}(B_{\cdot})|(B_{j-1},B_{j})\right]
it suffices to prove that there are
c,C>0
independent of
t
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[j-1,j],f(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{-1}(B_{\cdot})|(B_{j-1},B_{j})\right]

\displaystyle\geq c\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}},\qquad\forall_{j\in\{2,\dots,n\}},

(11.4)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}\sum_{j=2}^{n}\left(1-\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[j-1,j],f(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{-1}(B_{\cdot})|(B_{j-1},B_{j})\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq C,

(11.5)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,1],f(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{-1}|(B_{0},B_{1})\right]

\displaystyle\geq c\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}.

(11.6)

Equations (
11.3
) and (
11.4
)–(
11.6
) directly imply that there is a
c>0
such that

\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[A_{1}]}{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}[A_{2}]}\geq c,

(11.7)

i.e. (
11.2
). Thus to finish the proof, we only need to prove that (
11.4
)–(
11.6
) hold.

We proceed towards this, by first proving (
11.4
) and (
11.5
) in one calculation. Set
x_{j}:=W_{j}+1-2^{\beta}f(j)
for
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
. On
A_{2}
and given
B_{j-1}
,
B_{j}
we can couple
(B_{s})_{s\in[j-1,j]}
with a Brownian bridge
(E_{s}^{j})_{s\in[0,1]}
with
E_{0}^{j}=x_{j-1}
,
E_{1}^{j}=x_{j}
and
B_{s}\leq E_{s-j+1}^{j}
for all
j\in\{2,\dots,n\}
,
s\in[j-1,j]
. This coupling yields that

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\exists_{s\in[j-1,j]}\,-1+B_{s}+f(s)>W_{s}|(B_{j-1},B_{j})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}P\left[\exists_{s\in[0,1]}E_{s}^{j}>W_{s+j-1}-f(s+j-1)+1|W\right]

(11.8)

Let
E_{s}^{\prime}
be a standard Brownian bridge. We have that

(E_{s}^{j})_{j\in[0,1]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(E_{s}^{\prime}+sx_{j}+(1-s)x_{j-1})_{s\in[0,1]}.

(11.9)

Plugging (
11.9
) into (
11.8
) and simplifying, using that
W
is piecewise linear, gives that

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\exists_{s\in[j-1,j]}\,-1+B_{s}+f(s)>W_{s}|(B_{j-1},B_{j})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}P\left[\exists_{s\in[j-1,j]}\,E_{s}^{\prime}>-f(j-\mathbf{1}_{\beta=-1})+2^{\beta}f(j-\mathbf{1}_{\beta=1})\right].

(11.10)

Direct calculation yields that there is a
c^{\prime}>1/2
such that for
j\geq 1

\displaystyle\frac{f(j)}{f(j+1)}\geq c^{\prime}.

(11.11)

Plugging (
11.11
) into (
11.10
) yields that there is a
c^{\prime\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\exists_{s\in[j-1,j]}\,-1+B_{s}+f(s)>W_{s}|(B_{j-1},B_{j})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 128.0374pt\leq\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\exists_{s\in[j-1,j]}\,E_{s}^{\prime}>c^{\prime\prime}((1+j)^{1/6}-1)\right].

(11.12)

Since the maximum of a standard Brownian bridge is stochastically dominated by the maximum of a standard Brownian motion, which can be proved using e.g. Slepian’s lemma, (
11.12
) yields that for
j\in\{2,\dots,n\}

\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\exists_{s\in[j-1,j]}-1+B_{s}+f(s)>W_{s}|(B_{j-1},B_{j})\right]\leq 2\mathbf{1}_{A_{2}}\int_{c((1+j)^{1/6}-1)}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}\;\mathrm{d}t.

(11.13)

The equations (
11.4
) and (
11.5
) are directly implied by (
11.13
).

One can prove (
11.6
) similarly taking
x_{0}=0
and showing by calculation, that
(1-s)-f(s)+s2^{\beta}f(1)>0
is bounded away from zero.
∎

Lemma 11.2
.

We have that
(\mathcal{C}_{\log}(W,n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(\mathcal{C}_{\log}(\widehat{W},n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are tight.

Proof.

By construction, we have that

\mathcal{C}_{\log}\leq\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{|W_{k+1}|}{\sqrt{k}\sqrt{\log(k)}}<\infty\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

For the last inequality, we recall that by Remark
2.4
we can apply the law of iterated logarithms for the random walk
(W_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
. The second statement of the lemma follows from the first, since
\mathcal{C}_{\log}(\widehat{W},n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{C}_{\log}(W,n)
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, since
(W_{k})_{k\in[0,n]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(-\widehat{W}_{k})_{k\in[0,n]}
.
∎

Lemma 11.3
.

For all
\lambda>0

(C_{3}(W,h,\lambda,n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(C_{3}(\widehat{W},h,\lambda,n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are tight.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Lemmata
11.1
,
11.2
and the Definition of
C_{3}
in (
8.7
).
∎

Lemma 11.4
.

We have that
(C_{2}(W,h,n)^{-1})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(C_{2}(\widehat{W},h,n)^{-1})_{t\geq 0}
are tight.

Proof.

Follows from Lemma
11.2
, (
8.3
), and the definition of
C_{2}
in (
8.5
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.6

We recall Definitions
8.1
,
10.1
and Theorem
10.2
. We prove a more general statement than Lemma
2.6
, for this we need one additional definition.

Definition 11.5
.

We call
(h_{t})_{t\in I}
,
I\in\{\mathbb{N},\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\}
,
h_{t}:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R}
, nice, if there is a
\eta<1/2
such that for all
t\in I

\displaystyle\max_{s\in[0,t]\cap I}|h_{t}(s)|

\displaystyle\leq t^{\eta},

\displaystyle\max_{s\in[0,t/3]\cap I}\frac{|h_{t}(s)|}{(1+s)^{\eta}}

\displaystyle\leq C(h),

\displaystyle\max_{s\in[0,t/3]\cap I}\frac{|h_{t}(t)-h_{t}(t-s)|}{(1+s)^{\eta}}

\displaystyle\leq C(h).

Lemma 11.6
.

For
(h_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
nice and
y(n),y_{0}(n)\in[-\sqrt{n},0]
(the arguments are omitted in what follows), there is a deterministic constant
C_{2}(h)>0
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{|\log(p_{n,h}^{(y_{0})}(y))|}{\log(n)}\leq C_{2}(h).

(11.14)

Remark 11.7
.

Lemma
2.6
is an immediate corollary of Lemma
11.6
by taking
h\equiv 0
and
y(n)=y_{0}(n)=\xi_{0}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Since
p_{n,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)
is a probability it is smaller than
1
, thus
\log(p_{n,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)))\leq 0
, and we only need to prove a lower bound on
p_{n,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)
. In the following, we will use
C(h)
for a positive constant depending on
h
, but on neither
W
nor
n
, which may change from line to line.

Use the notation from Definition
10.1
. By Theorem
10.2
we have that for
n\geq 2

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle p_{n,h}^{(y_{0})}(y)

\displaystyle\geq n^{-\gamma_{0}-\log(n)^{-1}\hskip-1.9919pt\left(\hskip-1.42271pt\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}\hskip-1.42271pt-\hskip-1.42271pt1}\hskip-1.9919pt\Delta_{j}^{2}2^{j}+2(1-2^{3/2})\hskip-1.42271pt\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}\hskip-1.42271pt-\hskip-1.42271pt1}\hskip-1.9919pt\Delta_{j}2^{j/2}+\Xi_{0}^{2}+4\Xi_{0}+3\frac{W_{n}^{2}+h_{n}(n)^{2}}{t_{2}-t_{1}}+(\sqrt{t_{1}}+|y-y_{0}|)\frac{|W_{n}|+|h_{n}(n)|}{t_{2}-t_{1}}\hskip-1.42271pt\right)},

where
k_{1}
,
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
are
n
dependent, which we omit from notation. Using that
(h_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is nice,
y,y_{0}\in[-\sqrt{n},0]
and using the law of iterated logarithm for
(W_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, recall Remark
2.4
, yields that there is a
C(h)\in(0,\infty)
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}(\log(n))^{-1}\Bigg(\Xi_{0}^{2}+4\Xi_{0}+\frac{3W_{n}^{2}+h_{n}(n)^{2}}{2(t_{2}-t_{1})}+(\sqrt{t_{1}}+|y-y_{0}|)\frac{|W_{n}|+|h_{n}(n)|}{t_{2}-t_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\Delta_{k_{1}-1}^{2}2^{k_{1}-1}+(2-2^{5/2})\Delta_{k_{1}-1}2^{(k_{1}-1)/2}\Bigg)\leq C(h).

Thus it suffices to prove that there are
C,C(h)>0
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\log(n)^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-2}\Delta_{j}(h)^{2}2^{j}+|\Delta_{j}(h)|2^{j/2}\leq C(h),

(11.15)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\log(n)^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-2}\Delta_{j}(W)^{2}2^{j}+|\Delta_{j}(W)|2^{j/2}\leq C,

(11.16)

Since
(h_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is nice we have that for all
j\leq k_{1}-2

\displaystyle|\Delta_{j}(h)|

\displaystyle\leq ct^{\eta-1}+2^{\eta(j+2)-1},

which gives (
11.15
). It is left to prove (
11.16
). For this purpose, we notice, that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
j\leq k_{1}-2

\displaystyle 2^{j}|\Delta_{j}(W)|

\displaystyle\leq\max\{\underbrace{\max_{s\in[2^{j+1},2^{j+2}]}|W_{s}-W_{2^{j+1}}|}_{=:a_{j,n,1}},\underbrace{\max_{s\in[n-2^{j+2},n-2^{j+1}]}|W_{s}-W_{n-2^{j+1}}|}_{:=a_{j,n,2}}\}.

(11.17)

We have that
a_{j,n,1}
does not depend on
n
and
(a_{j,n,1})_{j\geq 1}
is a sequence of independent random variables. With for example Doobs maximal inequality one can show that there is a
c>0
such that for
j\leq k_{1}-2

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[a_{j,n,1}^{2}2^{-j}]

\displaystyle\leq c,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|a_{j,n,1}|2^{-j/2}]

\displaystyle\leq c.

Thus the strong law of large numbers gives that there is a
C>0
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\log(n)^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-2}a_{j,n,1}^{2}2^{-j}+|a_{j,n,1}|2^{-j/2}

\displaystyle\leq C.

(11.18)

For the sum involving
a_{j,n,2}
we want to use Borel-Cantelli, and thus want to bound
\mathbb{P}[\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-2}a_{j,n,2}2^{j/2}>x]
. For this, we use the exponential Chebyshev inequality to get that for
x>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-2}|a_{j,n,2}|2^{-j/2}>x\cdot\log(n)\right]

\displaystyle\leq e^{-x\log(n)}\prod_{j=0}^{k_{1}-2}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{|a_{j,n,2}|2^{-j/2}}\right].

(11.19)

Thus we want an upper bound on the tails of
|a_{j,n,2}|
. A union bound gives that for
(\lambda_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|a_{j,n,2}|2^{-j/2}>x\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{2^{j}}\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{r=1}^{k}W_{r}\right|>2^{j/2}x\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{2^{j}}e^{-\lambda_{k}2^{j/2}x}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\lambda_{k}W_{1}}\right]^{k}+\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\lambda_{k}W_{1}}\right]^{k}\right).

Direct calculation yields that
W_{1}
is sub-Gaussian, in particular there are
C,c>0
such that
\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\pm\lambda_{k}W_{1}}\right]\leq Ce^{c\lambda_{k}^{2}}
. This implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|a_{j,n,2}|2^{-j/2}>x\right]\leq\sum_{k=1}^{2^{j}}Ce^{-\lambda_{k}2^{j/2}x+c\lambda_{k}^{2}k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lambda_{k}=2^{j/2}x(2ck)^{-1}}}{{\leq}}\sum_{k=1}^{2^{j}}Ce^{-\frac{2^{j}x^{2}}{4ck}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{j}Ce^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4c}}

This implies that for
x\geq 1

\mathbb{P}\left[e^{|a_{j,n,2}|2^{-j/2}}>x\right]=\mathbb{P}\left[|a_{j,n,2}|>2^{j/2}\log(x)\right]\leq 2^{j}Ce^{-\frac{\log(x)^{2}2^{j}}{4c}}.

and since
|a_{j,n,2}|2^{-j/2}\geq 0
we have that
\mathbb{P}\left[e^{\lambda|a_{j,n,2}|2^{-j/2}}>x\right]=1
for
x\in[0,1]
. Thus

\mathbb{E}\left[e^{|a_{j,n,2}|2^{-j/2}}\right]\leq 1+\frac{2^{j}c^{2}4}{2^{j}-4c}\leq e^{\frac{2^{j}c^{2}4}{2^{j}-4c}}.

(11.20)

For
j
big enough, we have
\frac{2^{j}c^{2}4}{2^{j}-4c}\leq 8c^{2}
, especially by changing
c
we get from (
11.19
) and (
11.20
) that

\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-2}|a_{j,n,2}|2^{-j/2}>x\cdot\log(n)\right]\leq e^{-x\log(n)}e^{c^{2}\log(n)},

which is summable in
n
for all
x
big enough, deterministic, which implies that there is a
C>0
, such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\log(n)^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-2}|a_{j,n,2}|2^{-j/2}\leq C.

(11.21)

Very similarly one can prove that there is a
C>0
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\log(n)^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-2}a_{j,n,2}^{2}2^{-j}\leq C.

(11.22)

Equations (
11.21
) and (
11.22
) imply that there is a
C>0
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\log(n)^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k_{1}-2}a_{j,n,2}^{2}2^{-j}+|a_{j,n,2}|2^{-j/2}\leq C.

(11.23)

Equation (
11.17
), (
11.18
) and (
11.18
) yield (
11.16
), which finishes the proof of (
11.14
).
∎

We come back to controlling three more constants which pop up in the bounds we derived, recall (
9.4
), Definition
9.4
and that for given
t
we set
\widehat{W}_{s}:=W_{t-s}-W_{t}

Lemma 11.8
.

In (
9.4
) we can choose
(C_{4}(W,n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
(c_{2}(W,n)^{-1})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
(C_{4}(\widehat{W},n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(c_{2}(\widehat{W},n)^{-1})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
tight.

We omit the proof of Lemma
11.8
, since its statement can be inferred from
10.2
very similarly to the proof of Lemma
11.6
.

Lemma 11.9
.

For
y=y_{0}=\xi_{0}
and
\delta\in(0,1/8)
fixed in Theorems
9.5
and
9.19
the constants
(\eta(W,\widehat{W},n,h,\delta,y,y_{0}))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(\kappa(W,\widehat{W},n,h,y,y_{0}))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
can be chosen to be tight.

Proof.

As mentioned in Remark
9.6
we can choose
\kappa
and
\eta
as a continuous function of
\gamma
,
C_{3}
,
C_{2}^{-1}
,
\mathcal{C}_{\log}
,
\widehat{\gamma}
,
\widehat{C}_{3}
,
\widehat{C}_{2}^{-1}
,
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\log}
,
y
,
y_{0}
,
C_{4}
,
c_{2}^{-1}
,
\widehat{C}_{4}
,
\widehat{c}_{2}^{-1}
which together with Lemmata
11.1
–
11.4
and Lemma
11.8
yields the claim.
∎

12
Barrier Computations – Proof of Propositions
4.5
,
4.12
and
4.17
and Lemmata
5.8
,
6.3

12.1
Proof of Proposition
4.5

We will change the start- and endpoint, remove the
h^{\smallfrown}
and the logarithmic drift one by one and start by defining all the probabilities, which we encounter on the way. We recall Definition
2.1
and
h^{\smallfrown}
,
m_{t,h}^{\smallfrown}
from Definition
4.4
and
p_{t}^{(x)}(y)
,
p_{t,h}^{(x)}(y)
from Definition
9.3
. We note, that in this section we use the
W
defined in Definition
2.3
and that the probability measure used is
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}
.

Definition 12.1
.

Let
t>0
. For
x,y\leq 0
, define

\displaystyle p_{t,h^{\smallfrown},\text{barr}}^{(x)}(y)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],m_{t,h}^{\smallfrown}}^{y,J_{x}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right],

and recall (
4.4
). Furthermore, define
\widehat{W}_{s}:=W_{t-s}-W_{t}
,
s\in[0,t]
, and
\widehat{T}_{s}:=\vartheta^{\ast}(B_{s}-\widehat{W}_{s})
and

\widehat{p}_{t,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(x)}(y):=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,t],h_{t}^{\smallfrown}}^{y,J_{x}}(\widehat{T}_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right].

We will now state the lemmata necessary to prove Proposition
4.5
, then show the proof and after that prove the lemmata. We recall the definition of
p_{n}^{\smallfrown,x}(y)
in (
4.4
).

Lemma 12.2
.

There is a
C>0
such that for
n\in\mathbb{N}
and all
x,y\leq\xi_{0}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\frac{p_{n}^{\smallfrown,x}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallfrown},\text{barr}}^{(x)}(y)}\leq C.

The proof of Lemma
12.2
is similar to the proof of (
11.4
)–(
11.5
) in Lemma
11.1
for
\beta=-1
, so we won’t give details. In words, Lemma
12.2
means, that the events to be below the barrier for all
j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
and to be below the barrier (times two) for all
s\leq n
do only differ by a constant.

Lemma 12.3
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}\atop x\in[-\log(n),\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallfrown},\text{barr}}^{(x)}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(x)}(y)}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

In words, Lemma
12.3
means, that removing the
\frac{j}{n}\log(p_{n})
part of the barrier costs a multiplicative constant.

Lemma 12.4
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
c_{\varepsilon},C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}\atop x\in[-\log(n),\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(x)}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(x)}(\xi_{0}+1)}\leq C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-\varepsilon,

(12.1)

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}+1]\cap\mathbb{Z}\atop x\in[-\log(n),\xi_{0}+1]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\frac{\widehat{p}_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(x)}(y)}{\widehat{p}_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(x)}(\xi_{0})}\leq C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-\varepsilon.

(12.2)

Lemma
12.4
states that moving the startpoint or endpoint from
y
to
\xi_{0}
costs a power of
|y|
.

Lemma 12.5
.

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
x,y\leq 0
, we have that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\displaystyle p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(x)}(y)

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{p}_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y)}(x-1),

\displaystyle\widehat{p}_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(x)}(y)

\displaystyle\leq p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(y)}(x-1).

Lemma
12.5
states that reversing time makes (close to) no difference.

Lemma 12.6
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})}{p_{n}}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Lemma
12.6
states that when starting and ending at
\xi_{0}
, the curve of the barrier can be removed by paying a multiplicative constant.

Now we have stated everything we need to prove Proposition
4.5
.

Proof of Propositions
4.5
assuming L.
12.2
–
12.6
.

In this proof we shorten
h^{\smallfrown}
to
h
.
We can write

\displaystyle\frac{p_{n}^{\smallfrown,x}(y)}{p_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{n}^{\smallfrown,x}(y)}{p^{(x)}_{n,h,\text{barr}}(y)}\frac{p^{(x)}_{n,h,\text{barr}}(y)}{p^{(x)}_{n,h}(y)}\frac{p^{(x)}_{n,h}(y)}{p^{(x)}_{n,h}(\xi_{0}+1)}\frac{p^{(x)}_{n,h}(\xi_{0}+1)}{\widehat{p}^{(\xi_{0}+1)}_{n,h}(x-1)}\frac{\widehat{p}^{(\xi_{0}+1)}_{n,h}(x-1)}{\widehat{p}^{(\xi_{0}+1)}_{n,h}(\xi_{0})}\frac{\widehat{p}^{(\xi_{0}+1)}_{n,h}(\xi_{0})}{p^{(\xi_{0})}_{n,h}(\xi_{0})}\frac{p^{(\xi_{0})}_{n,h}(\xi_{0})}{p_{n}}.

Applying Lemmata
12.2
to
12.6
then immediately yields the claim.
∎

Now we prove Lemmata
12.3
–
12.6
. We note that these proofs will make heavy use of Sections
8
and
9
.

Proof of Lemma
12.3
.

Applying Theorem
9.2
yields that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
y\in[-\log(n)^{2},0]
,
x\in[-\log(n),0]
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallfrown},\text{barr}}^{(x)}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(x)}(y)}\leq e^{-\frac{\log(p_{n})^{2}}{2n(\vartheta^{\ast})^{2}}}e^{-3\log(n)^{2}\frac{\log(p_{n})}{\vartheta^{\ast}n}}e^{W_{n}\frac{\log(p_{n})}{\vartheta^{\ast}n}}.

Since by Lemma
11.6

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
\log(p_{n})/\log(n)^{2}\to 0
and furthermore
\log(n)W_{n}/n\to 0

\mathbb{P}
-a.s., the statement of the lemma follows.
∎

Proof of Lemma
12.4
.

Applying Theorems
8.2
and
8.4
yields that for all
\lambda>0

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\bigcap_{y\in[-\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\atop x\in[-\log(n),\xi_{0}]}\Big\{\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(x)}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(x)}(\xi_{0}+1)}\leq C_{2}^{-2}4^{\gamma+2}e^{4C_{1}}|y|^{4\gamma+3C_{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+Ce^{-\lambda\log(n)}p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(x)}(\xi_{0}+1)^{-1}(2\log(n)^{2})^{2\gamma}C_{2}^{-1}\Big\}\Bigg].

By Lemma
11.1
to
11.4
the sequences
(\gamma(W,h^{\smallfrown},n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
(C_{3}(W,h^{\smallfrown},\lambda,n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(C_{2}(W,h^{\smallfrown},n)^{-1})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are tight, and Lemma
11.6
implies that there is a
\lambda>0
such that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in[-\log(n),\xi_{0}]}Ce^{-\lambda\log(n)}p_{n}^{(x)}(\xi_{0}+1)^{-1}(2\log(n)^{2})^{2\gamma}C_{2}^{-1}=0.

This allows us to conclude (
12.1
).

Equation (
12.2
) follows the same way, only replacing
W
by
\widehat{W}
in the above and using that by Lemma
11.1
to
11.4

(\gamma(\widehat{W},h^{\smallfrown},n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
(C_{3}(\widehat{W},h^{\smallfrown},\lambda,n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
(C_{2}(\widehat{W},h^{\smallfrown},n)^{-1})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are tight.
∎

Proof of Lemma
12.5
.

We have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},h^{\smallfrown}_{n}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{y,J_{x}}(B_{\cdot})\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{x-1}^{x}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[B_{n}=\mathrm{d}u+W_{n}-y\right]\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},h^{\smallfrown}_{n}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{y}(B_{\cdot})|B_{n}=u+W_{n}-y\right]

(12.3)

Set
\widehat{W}_{j}:=W_{n-j}-W_{n}
,
\widehat{B}_{j}:=B_{n-j}-B_{n}
. Since
h^{\smallfrown}_{n}
is symmetric, reversing time in (
12.3
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},h^{\smallfrown}_{n}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{y,J_{x}}(B_{\cdot})\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{x-1}^{x}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[B_{n}=\mathrm{d}u+W_{n}-y\right]\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},h^{\smallfrown}_{n}(\cdot)-\widehat{W}_{\cdot}}^{u}(\widehat{B}_{\cdot})|B_{n}=u+W_{n}-y\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{-x}^{-x+1}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\widehat{B}_{n}=\mathrm{d}u+\widehat{W}_{n}+y\right]\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},h^{\smallfrown}_{n}(\cdot)-\widehat{W}_{\cdot}}^{u}(\widehat{B}_{\cdot})|\widehat{B}_{n}=u+\widehat{W}_{n}+y\right],

(12.4)

where the last step used that
\widehat{B}_{n}=-B_{n}
and
\widehat{W}_{n}=-W_{n}
.

Since
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},h^{\smallfrown}_{n}(\cdot)-\widehat{W}_{\cdot}}^{\mathbf{u}}(\widehat{B}_{\cdot})|\widehat{B}_{n}=u-W_{n}+y\right]
is monotone in the bolded
u
we can replace it by
-x+1
. Furthermore, we can shift the region of integration by
y-1+x
in (
12.4
), which yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},h^{\smallfrown}_{n}(\cdot)-W_{\cdot}}^{y,J_{x}}(B_{\cdot})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{y-1}^{y}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\widehat{B}_{n}=\mathrm{d}r+\widehat{W}-x+1\right]\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},h^{\smallfrown}_{n}(\cdot)-\widehat{W}_{\cdot}}^{x-1}(\widehat{B}_{\cdot})|\widehat{B}_{n}=r+\widehat{W}-x+1\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,n\},h^{\smallfrown}_{n}(\cdot)-\widehat{W}_{\cdot}}^{x-1,J_{y}}(\widehat{B}_{\cdot})\right].

(12.5)

Since
(\widehat{B}_{j})_{j\in\{0,\dots,n\}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(B_{j})_{j\in\{0,\dots,n\}}
equation (
12.5
) implies the claim of the lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
12.6
.

Denote
A_{n}:=\left\{\log(n)^{-0.5}\max\{\mathcal{C}_{\log},\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\log}\}\leq\frac{1}{3}\right\}
. By Lemma
11.2
we have that
\log(n)^{-0.5}\max\{\mathcal{C}_{\log},\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\log}\}\to 0
in
\mathbb{P}
-probability and thus
\mathbf{1}_{A_{n}}\to 1
in
\mathbb{P}
-probability.
On
A_{n}
condition (
9.3
) holds, and thus we can apply Theorem
9.5
to get that there are
c,C,n_{0}(\lambda)>0
such that for
n\geq n_{0}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{A_{n}}\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})-\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}\sum_{j=0}^{2}p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(\xi_{0}-1)}(\xi_{0}-j)}{p_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{1}_{A_{n}}\Bigg(Ce^{c(\mathcal{C}_{\log}+\kappa-\eta)}\left(1+n^{-\lambda}p_{n}^{(y_{0})}(y)^{-1}\right)+Cn^{-\lambda}((p_{n}^{\ast})^{-1}+(\widehat{p}_{n}^{\ast})^{-1})p_{n}^{(y_{0})}(y)^{-1}\Bigg).

(12.6)

By Lemma
12.4
we know that there is a
C_{\varepsilon,1}>0
such that

1-\varepsilon/2\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{2}p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(\xi_{0}-1)}(\xi_{0}-j)}{p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})}\leq C_{\varepsilon,1}\right].

(12.7)

Choosing
\delta_{\varepsilon}:=\frac{1}{2C_{\varepsilon,1}+1}\wedge\frac{1}{8}
in (
12.6
), applying (
12.7
) and using
\mathbf{1}_{A_{n}}\to 1
in
\mathbb{P}
-probability implies that for all
\lambda>0

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon/2

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallfrown}}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})}{p_{n}}\leq\Bigg(Ce^{c(\mathcal{C}_{\log}+\kappa(W,\widehat{W},n,h^{\smallfrown},\xi_{0},\xi_{0})-\eta(W,\widehat{W},n,h^{\smallfrown},\delta_{\varepsilon},\xi_{0},\xi_{0}))}\cdot

\displaystyle\hskip 82.51282pt\cdot\left(1+n^{-\lambda}p_{n}^{(y_{0})}(y)^{-1}\right)+Cn^{-\lambda}((p_{n}^{\ast})^{-1}+(\widehat{p}_{n}^{\ast})^{-1})p_{n}^{(y_{0})}(y)^{-1}\Bigg].

(12.8)

By Lemma
11.2
we have that
(\mathcal{C}_{\log}(W,n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight. By Lemma
11.9
the sequences
(\eta(W,\widehat{W},n,h^{\smallfrown},\delta_{\varepsilon},\xi_{0},\xi_{0}))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(\kappa(W,\widehat{W},n,h^{\smallfrown},\xi_{0},\xi_{0}))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are tight. By Lemma
11.6
and Definition
9.4
there is a
\lambda>0
such that the terms
Cn^{-\lambda}((p_{n}^{\ast})^{-1}+(\widehat{p}_{n}^{\ast})^{-1})p_{n}^{(y_{0})}(y)^{-1}
and
n^{-\lambda}p_{n}^{-1}
go to zero in
\mathbb{P}
-probability. Thus we can conclude the statement of Lemma
12.6
from (
12.8
).
∎

12.2
Proof of Proposition
4.12
.

This section parallels Section
12.1
, although it is slightly simpler since we do not need to consider endpoints other than the (negative) constant
\xi_{0}
. Thus recall
h^{\smallsmile}
and
m_{t,h}^{\smallsmile}
from Definition
4.9
as well as Definition
12.1
.
The proof of Proposition
4.12
is split into several lemmata, we will next state those and then prove the Proposition assuming the lemmata. Since the proofs of the lemmata are very similar to Section
12.1
, we won’t repeat them, but instead just reference the corresponding lemmata in Section
12.1
.

Lemma 12.7
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that for
y\leq\xi_{0}
,

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile},\text{barr}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}\geq C_{\varepsilon}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

The proof of Lemma
12.7
parallels the proof of Lemma
12.3
and thus is based on Theorem
9.2
.

Lemma 12.8
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
C_{\varepsilon},c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that for
y\leq\xi_{0}
,

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})}\geq C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{-c_{\varepsilon}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

The proof of Lemma
12.8
is parallel to the proof of Lemma
12.4
, but uses Theorem
8.3
instead of Theorem
8.4
.

Lemma 12.9
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})}{p_{n}}\geq C_{\varepsilon}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

The proof of Lemma
12.9
is parallel to the proof of Lemma
12.6
using Theorem
9.19
in place of Theorem
9.5
.

Proof of Proposition
4.12
assuming Lemma
12.7
–
12.9
.

We have that

\displaystyle\frac{p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)}{p_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile},\text{barr}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile},\text{barr}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})}\frac{p_{n}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})}{p_{n}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile},\text{barr}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})}\frac{p_{n}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})}{p_{n}},

where we used that by monotonicity
p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)/p_{n,h^{\smallsmile},\text{barr}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)\geq 1
. Now, applying Lemmata
12.7
to
12.9
immediately yields the claim of the Proposition.
∎

12.3
Proof of Proposition
4.17

We recall
h^{\smallsmile}
and
m_{t,h}^{\smallsmile}
from Definition
4.9
as well as Definition
12.1
. We won’t give proofs for the lemmata used in the proof of Proposition
4.17
, but instead reference lemmata, which are proved similarly.

Lemma 12.10
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{y\in[-2\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\frac{p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}/2,\text{barr}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

The proof is similar to the proof of Lemma
12.2
.

Lemma 12.11
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{y\in[-2\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}/2,\text{barr}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}/2}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

The proof is parallel to the proof of Lemma
12.3
.

Lemma 12.12
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
, there are
C_{\varepsilon},c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{y\in[-2\log(n)^{2},\xi_{0}]\cap\mathbb{Z}}\left\{\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}/2}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}/2}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})}\leq C_{\varepsilon}|y|^{c_{\varepsilon}}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon.

The proof is analogous to the proof of Lemma
12.4
.

Proof of Proposition
4.17
assuming Lemmata
12.10
,
12.11
and
12.12
.

By monotonicity
p_{n,h/2}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})/p_{n}\leq 1
. Thus writing

\frac{p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)}{p_{n}}=\frac{p_{n}^{\smallsmile,\xi_{0}}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}/2,\text{barr}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}/2,\text{barr}}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}/2}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}/2}^{(\xi_{0})}(y)}{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}/2}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})}\frac{p_{n,h^{\smallsmile}/2}^{(\xi_{0})}(\xi_{0})}{p_{n}}

and applying Lemmata
12.10
,
12.11
and
12.12
yields the claim of Proposition
4.17
.
∎

12.4
Proof of Lemma
5.8

We recall Definitions
2.1
, (
2.2
) and (
5.5
). Before we can proceed with the proof, we need one additional definition.

Definition 12.13
.

Define

\displaystyle W^{(k)}_{j}

\displaystyle:=W_{n-k-1+j}-W_{n-k-1},

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{lin},k}(W)

\displaystyle:=e^{-(k+1)\frac{\left(\frac{1}{k+1}((2+k)^{1/6}-1)+\log(p_{n})/(\vartheta^{\ast}n)\right)^{2}}{2}}e^{-2|\xi_{0}-3|\cdot\left|\frac{(2+k)^{1/6}-1}{k+1}+\frac{\log(p_{n})}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}\right|}

\displaystyle\qquad e^{\left((2+k)^{1/6}-1\right)\left(\frac{(2+k)^{1/6}-1}{k+1}+\frac{\log(p_{n})}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}\right)}e^{W_{k+1}^{(k)}\left(-\frac{(2+k)^{1/6}-1}{k+1}-\frac{\log(p_{n})}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}\right)}.

Proof of Lemma
5.8
.

Shorten
((2+k)^{1/6}-(2+k-s)^{1/6})=:\eta_{k}(s)
.

By applying Theorem
9.2
for
t=k+1
,
c_{k+1}=-\frac{(2+k)^{1/6}-1}{k+1}-\frac{\log(p_{n})}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}
we get for all
k\leq\log(n)^{7}

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}q_{k,\text{end}}(x)

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{C}_{\text{lin},k}(W)\cdot\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{n-k-1}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,k+1],\eta_{k}(s)-s/(k+1)\eta_{k}(k+1)}^{x,J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right].

Since
\eta(s)
is convex and
\eta_{k}(0)=0
we have that
\eta_{k}(s)-\frac{s}{k+1}\eta_{k}(k+1)\leq 0
for all
s\in[0,k+1]
and can just drop that term only making the probability smaller. Thus we have that for all
k\leq\log(n)^{7}

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}q_{k,\text{end}}(x)

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{C}_{\text{lin},k}(W)\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{n-k-1}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,k+1]}^{x,J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]

(12.9)

We continue by providing lower bounds for both factors in (
12.9
). Applying the law of iterated logarithms for
(W_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, which has been justified in Remark
2.4
, allows us to conclude that there is a
c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{k\leq\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}\left\{\frac{|W_{k+1}^{(k)}|}{(k+1)^{3/4}}\leq c_{\varepsilon}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon/4.

(12.10)

Furthermore, by Lemma
11.6
we have that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.

\frac{|\log(p_{n})|}{\log(n)^{2}}\to 0.

(12.11)

Plugging (
12.10
) and (
12.11
) into the definition of
\mathcal{C}_{\text{lin},k}(W)
and using that we only care about
k\leq\log(n)^{7}
yields that there is a
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{k\leq\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\text{lin},k}(W)\geq C_{\varepsilon}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon/2.

(12.12)

By Lemma
11.6
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
there are random variables
\mathcal{C}_{1}^{(n)}
,
\mathcal{C}_{2}^{(n)}
such that for all
k\leq n

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{n-k}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,k]}^{x,J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]\geq\mathcal{C}_{1}^{(n)}k^{-\mathcal{C}_{2}^{(n)}}

and since the environment is i.i.d. we can choose them such that for
i\in\{1,2\}
the distribution of
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{(n)}
does not depend on
n
. This implies that there are
C_{\varepsilon},c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{k\leq\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}\left\{\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}^{n-k-1}\left[\mathcal{B}_{[0,k+1]}^{x,J_{\xi_{0}}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]\geq C_{\varepsilon}(k+1)^{-c_{\varepsilon}}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon/2.

(12.13)

Plugging equations (
12.12
) and (
12.13
) into (
12.9
) and the observation that

\sup_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}e^{\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}^{\smallfrown}(k)/2}C_{\varepsilon}(k+1)^{c_{\varepsilon}}<\infty

finishes the proof.
∎

12.5
Proof of Lemma
6.3

We recall Definition
6.1
.

Before we can proceed with the proof of Lemma
6.3
we need one additional definition.

Definition 12.14
.

Set

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\text{lin},k}^{\prime}(W)

\displaystyle:=e^{-\frac{k\log(p_{n})^{2}}{2(n\vartheta^{\ast})^{2}}}e^{-h_{n}^{\smallsmile}(k)\frac{\log(p_{n})}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}}e^{-2(\xi_{0}-2)\frac{\log(p_{n})}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}}e^{-\frac{\log(p_{n})W_{k}}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}}.

Proof of Lemma
6.3
.

The proof is parallel to the proof of Corollary
5.8
. Applying Theorem
9.2
for
t=k
,
c_{k}=-\frac{\log(p_{n})}{n\vartheta^{\ast}}
yields that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for all
k\leq\log(n)^{7}

\displaystyle p_{\text{start},k}

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{C}_{\text{lin},k}^{\prime}(W)\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-1,0]}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,k\},h_{n}^{\smallsmile}}^{\xi_{0},J_{x}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]

(12.14)

As in the proof of Corollary
5.8
combining Lemma
11.6
with the fact that there is a
c_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{k\leq\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}\left\{\frac{|W_{k+1}|}{(k+1)^{3/4}}\leq c_{\varepsilon}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon/4,

yields that there is a
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{k\leq\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}\left\{\mathcal{C}_{\text{lin},k}^{\prime}(W)\geq C_{\varepsilon}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon/2.

(12.15)

Furthermore, by setting
g_{k}:[0,k]\to\mathbb{R},s\mapsto(1+s)^{1/6}-1
we have that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,k\},h_{n}^{\smallsmile}}^{\xi_{0},J_{x}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]=p_{k,g_{k}}^{(x)}(\xi_{0})

and applying Lemma
11.6
yields that there are
c_{\varepsilon}>0
,
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left[\bigcap_{k\leq\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}\left\{\inf_{x\in[\xi_{0}-2,-1]}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{L}}\left[\mathcal{B}_{\{0,\dots,k\},h_{n}^{\smallsmile}}^{\xi_{0},J_{x}}(T_{\cdot}/\vartheta^{\ast})\right]\geq C_{\varepsilon}k^{-c_{\varepsilon}}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon/2.

(12.16)

Plugging (
12.15
) and (
12.16
) into (
12.14
) together with the observation that

\sup_{n}\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor\log(n)^{7}\rfloor}e^{-\vartheta^{\ast}h_{n}(k)^{\smallsmile}}C_{\varepsilon}k^{c_{\varepsilon}}<\infty

finishes the proof.
∎
